\mathrm{Fil}^{r;r^{\prime}}_{k}\mathfrak{M}=S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}(G^{\prime}_{1},G^{\prime}_{2})+\mathrm{Fil}^{p}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathfrak{M}

where

(4.1.11)

(G^{\prime}_{1},G^{\prime}_{2}):=\begin{cases}\big(v^{r-r^{\prime}}F^{\prime}_{0,0},\,v^{r}E^{\prime}_{2}\big)&\mbox{if }k=0;\\
\big(v^{r-r^{\prime}}F^{\prime}_{k,k},\,v^{r-r^{\prime}}F^{\prime}_{k,0}\big)&\mbox{if }0<k<r^{\prime};\\
\big(v^{r}E^{\prime}_{1},\,v^{r-r^{\prime}}F^{\prime}_{r^{\prime},0}\big)&\mbox{if }k=r^{\prime};\\
\big(v^{r}E^{\prime}_{1},\,v^{r-r^{\prime}}E^{\prime}_{2}\big)&\mbox{if }k=\infty.\end{cases}

We prove Theorem
4.1.10
in §
4.2
for the cases
r^{\prime}>1
and
k\neq\infty
and in §
4.4
for the cases
r^{\prime}=1
or
k=\infty
. The framework we developed in §
3
plays key roles. We also note that the condition
v_{p}(x)<0
for
m+1\leq k\leq r^{\prime}
is the only artificial assumption we made in this paper, while it is harmless to assume
v_{p}(\Theta)\leq 0
by Lemma
4.1.7
.

We will further check the strong divisibility of
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
. More precisely, it is necessary for
\phi^{\prime}
to satisfy

(4.1.12)

\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}=S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}\left(\phi^{\prime}_{r}(G^{\prime}_{1}),\phi^{\prime}_{r}(G^{\prime}_{2})\right)

for each
\mathbf{Case}~(k)
. We will consider the following two possibilities
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(G^{\prime}_{1})]_{E^{\prime}_{1}}\in(S_{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime})^{\times}
or
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(G^{\prime}_{2})]_{E^{\prime}_{1}}\in(S_{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime})^{\times}
, as it will turn out to be no overlaps between these two possibilities.

Definition 4.1.13
.

Let
k\in([0,r^{\prime}]\cap\mathbf{Z})
. We say that a pseudo-strongly divisible module
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
in
\mathbf{Case}~(k)
satisfies
the strong divisibility of type

\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(k)
(resp.
of type

\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(k+\tfrac{1}{2}))

if it satisfies the strong divisibility (
4.1.12
) together with

[\phi_{r}^{\prime}(G^{\prime}_{2})]_{E^{\prime}_{1}}\in(S_{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime})^{\times}\quad\big(\mbox{resp.}\,\,\,[\phi_{r}^{\prime}(G^{\prime}_{1})]_{E^{\prime}_{1}}\in(S_{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime})^{\times}\big).

If
k=\infty
, then we say that a pseudo-strongly divisible module
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
in
\mathbf{Case}~(\infty)
satisfies
the strong divisibility of type

\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\infty)
if it satisfies the strong divisibility (
4.1.12
).

Note that
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(0)
is empty as
[\phi^{\prime}(E^{\prime}_{2})]_{E^{\prime}_{1}}=0
, and that if a pseudo-strongly divisible module
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
satisfies the strong divisibility of type
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\infty)
then we have
[\phi_{r}^{\prime}(G^{\prime}_{1})]_{E^{\prime}_{1}}\in(S_{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime})^{\times}
, as
[\phi^{\prime}(E^{\prime}_{2})]_{E^{\prime}_{1}}=0
. We set

\mathcal{K}_{r^{\prime}}:=\left\{k^{\prime}\in\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbf{Z}\mid\tfrac{1}{2}\leq k^{\prime}\leq r^{\prime}+\tfrac{1}{2}\right\}\sqcup\{\infty\}.

By convention, we set
\lfloor\infty\rfloor:=\infty
, and if a pseudo-strongly divisible module
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
satisfies the strongly divisibility of type
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(k^{\prime})
for some
k^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}_{r^{\prime}}
then the filtration of
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
is automatically determined by
k^{\prime}
, i.e., it has the filtration
\mathrm{Fil}^{r;r^{\prime}}_{k}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
where
k:=\lfloor k^{\prime}\rfloor
.

In Lemma
4.1.7
, we have seen that
\tilde{\Delta}(T)
is not relevant to
\phi^{\prime}(\mathfrak{M}^{\prime})\subseteq\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
, which implies that
\tilde{\Delta}(T)
is not relevant to the strong divisibility in
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(r^{\prime}+\tfrac{1}{2})
and in
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\infty)
. Moreover, it turns out that
\tilde{\Delta}(T)
is not relevant to the strong divisibility in the other cases either.

Lemma 4.1.14
.

Assume that
v_{p}(\Theta\Omega)\leq 0
, and further that if
p=3
then
\tilde{\Delta}(T)
is a constant in
E
. If
0\leq k\leq r^{\prime}-1
then we have

(4.1.15)

\frac{1}{c^{r-r^{\prime}}}\phi^{\prime}_{r}(v^{r-r^{\prime}}F^{\prime}_{k,k})=\frac{\Lambda}{p^{r-k-1}}P_{k,k}(c,x)E^{\prime}_{1}\\
+\frac{\Lambda}{p^{r-k+1}\Theta\Omega}P_{k,k}(c,x)\big(\tilde{\Delta}(\phi(\gamma)-1)-\tilde{\Delta}(-1)\big)E^{\prime}_{2}\\
+\frac{\Lambda}{p^{r-k}\Omega}\big([P_{k,k}(c,x)cf(c)]_{c}^{(r-1)}-P_{k,k}(c,x)\Delta(\gamma-1)\big)E^{\prime}_{2},

and if
1\leq k\leq r^{\prime}
then we have

(4.1.16)

\frac{1}{c^{r-r^{\prime}}}\phi^{\prime}_{r}(v^{r-r^{\prime}}F^{\prime}_{k,0})=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{k-1}}P_{k,0}(c,x)E^{\prime}_{1}\\
+\frac{\Lambda}{p^{k+1}\Omega}P_{k,0}(c,x)\big(\tilde{\Delta}(\phi(\gamma)-1)-\tilde{\Delta}(-1)\big)E^{\prime}_{2}\\
+\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{k}\Omega}\big([P_{k,0}(c,x)cf(c)]_{c}^{(r-1)}-P_{k,0}(c,x)\Delta(\gamma-1)\big)E^{\prime}_{2}.

In particular, if
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(v^{r-r^{\prime}}F^{\prime}_{k,k})]_{E^{\prime}_{1}}\in S_{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}

(
resp. if
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(v^{r-r^{\prime}}F^{\prime}_{k,0})]_{E^{\prime}_{1}}\in S_{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime})
then
\tilde{\Delta}(T)
is not relevant to the strong divisibility.

Note that it is harmless to assume
v_{p}(\Omega)\leq 0
and
v_{p}(\Theta)\leq 0
, as we will see in §
5.1
. We also note that the assumption on
\tilde{\Delta}
is harmless either, as we will choose such
\tilde{\Delta}
when
p=3
.

Proof.

From the definition of
F^{\prime}_{k,i}
in (
4.1.1
) together with (
4.1.4
) and (
4.1.5
), it is tedious to compute
\phi^{\prime}_{r}(v^{r-r^{\prime}}F^{\prime}_{k,k})
and
\phi^{\prime}_{r}(v^{r-r^{\prime}}F^{\prime}_{k,0})
, and so we omit the detail.

For
0\leq k\leq r-1
, the coefficient of the second term of
\phi^{\prime}_{r}(v^{r-r^{\prime}}F^{\prime}_{k,k})
can be written as

(4.1.17)

[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(v^{r-r^{\prime}}F^{\prime}_{k,k})]_{E^{\prime}_{1}}\cdot\frac{1}{p^{2}\Theta\Omega}\big(\tilde{\Delta}(\phi(\gamma)-1)-\tilde{\Delta}(-1)\big)

and so it belongs to
p^{p-3}S_{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}
if
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(v^{r-r^{\prime}}F^{\prime}_{k,k})]_{E^{\prime}_{1}}\in S_{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}
, by the assumption (
4.1.8
) together with the fact (
4.1.9
). Hence,
\tilde{\Delta}
is not relevant to the strong divisibility if
p>3
. If
p=3
then the quantity (
4.1.17
) vanishes by the hypothesis. Similarly, if
1\leq k\leq r
, then the coefficient of the second term of
\phi^{\prime}_{r}(v^{r-r^{\prime}}F^{\prime}_{k,0})
can be written as in (
4.1.17
), and so by the same argument we conclude that
\tilde{\Delta}
is not relevant to the strong divisibility.
∎

Since
\tilde{\Delta}(T)
is not relevant to the strong divisibility, the following definition makes sense.

Definition 4.1.18
.

Let
k^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}_{r^{\prime}}
and
k:=\lfloor k^{\prime}\rfloor
, and let
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
be a pseudo-strongly divisible module in
\mathbf{Case}~(k)
. For given quadruple
(\Lambda,\Theta,\Omega,x)\in(E^{\times})^{3}\times E
and
\Delta(T)\in E(T)_{\Delta}
(possibly depending on
x
), we say that the quadruple
(\Lambda,\Theta,\Omega,x)
is
good for
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(k^{\prime})
with respect to
\Delta(T)
if
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
satisfies the following:

\circ

\phi^{\prime}(\mathfrak{M}^{\prime})\subseteq\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
and
N^{\prime}(\mathfrak{M}^{\prime})\subseteq\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
, i.e., the inequalities in (
4.1.8
);

\circ

\mathrm{Fil}^{r;r^{\prime}}_{k}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
, i.e.,
F^{\prime}_{k,i}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
for all
0\leq i\leq r^{\prime}-1
;

\circ

the strong divisibility of type
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(k^{\prime})
.

Recall that
\delta_{l}(T,x)
for
0\leq l\leq\lfloor\tfrac{r^{\prime}}{2}\rfloor+1
are defined in Definition
3.3.1
. Note that
\Delta(T)
of
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(k^{\prime})
will be chosen as follows: if we set

(4.1.19)

l(r^{\prime};k^{\prime}):=\begin{cases}\lceil k^{\prime}\rceil&\mbox{if }\frac{1}{2}\leq k^{\prime}\leq m+1;\\
r^{\prime}+1-\lceil k^{\prime}\rceil&\mbox{if }m+1<k^{\prime}\leq r^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2};\\
0&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}=\infty,\end{cases}

then we have

(4.1.20)

\Delta(T):=\delta_{l(r^{\prime};k^{\prime})}(T,x)

in each
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(k^{\prime})
. We recall that
\delta_{l}(T,x)=\delta_{l}(T)
unless either
r^{\prime}=2m
and
l=m+1
or
l=0
, as we pointed out right after Definition
3.3.1
.

Remark 4.1.21
.

The choice of
\Delta(T)
in (
4.1.20
) is set to assure
[\phi_{r}(v^{r-r^{\prime}}G^{\prime}_{i})]_{E^{\prime}_{2}}\in S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}
for
i=1
or
2
depending on
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(k^{\prime})
. More precisely, if
k^{\prime}=k\in\mathbf{Z}
then
\Delta(T)
is chosen to have

[P_{k,0}(c,x)cf(c)]_{c}^{(r-1)}-\Delta(\gamma-1)P_{k,0}(c,x)\in P_{k,0}(c,x)\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}

from (
4.1.16
), while if
k^{\prime}=k+\frac{1}{2}\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbf{Z}
then
\Delta(T)
is chosen to have

[P_{k,k}(c,x)cf(c)]_{c}^{(r-1)}-\Delta(\gamma-1)P_{k,k}(c,x)\in P_{k,k}(c,x)\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}

from (
4.1.15
). We believe that the choice of
\Delta
is essentially unique!

The main results in this section is to find out the precise conditions on
(\Lambda,\Theta,\Omega,x)
under which the quadruple is good for
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(k^{\prime})
with respect to
\Delta(T)
determined in (
4.1.20
) for each
k^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}_{r^{\prime}}
. The cases
r^{\prime}>1
and
k^{\prime}\neq\infty
are treated in §
4.3
while the cases
r^{\prime}=1
or
k^{\prime}=\infty
are in §
4.4
. Those conditions are summarized in following table, Table
1
. Recall that we write
k
for
\lfloor k^{\prime}\rfloor
for
k^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}_{r^{\prime}}
with convention
\lfloor\infty\rfloor=\infty
.

.

By the
equations and inequalities of
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(k^{\prime})
(resp. by
\Delta
of
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(k^{\prime})
), we mean those on
(\Lambda,\Theta,\Omega,x)\in(E^{\times})^{3}\times E
in Table

(resp. those defined in (
4.1.20
) or equivalently in Table

).

Note that we often write
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(r^{\prime};k^{\prime})
for
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(k^{\prime})
to emphasize
r^{\prime}
.

,

Table 1.
Equations, Inequalities, and
\Delta
of
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(k^{\prime})

We point out that for each
k^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}_{r^{\prime}}
the equations and inequalities of
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(k^{\prime})
always imply that
v_{p}(\Theta)\leq 0
and
v_{p}(\Omega)\leq 0
, and that if
k^{\prime}=m+\frac{1}{2}
and
r^{\prime}=2m
then the equations and inequalities in Proposition
4.3.3
imply
-1\leq v_{p}(\Omega)
, which is added to Table
1
for a uniform expression. From the second column of Table
1
we immediately get

(4.1.23)

v_{p}(\Lambda^{2}\Theta\Omega^{-1})=r^{\prime}-1.

This phenomenon is novel and we didn’t expect it. Moreover, we also point out that the equations and inequalities of
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(k^{\prime})
imply the inequalities in (
4.1.8
) as well as the inequalities in Theorem
4.1.10
.

Theorem 4.1.24
.

If the quadruple
(\Lambda,\Theta,\Omega,x)
satisfies the equations and inequalities of
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(r^{\prime};k^{\prime})
for some
k^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}_{r^{\prime}}
then the quadruple is good for
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(k^{\prime})
with respect to
\Delta(T):=\delta_{l(r^{\prime};k^{\prime})}(T,x)
.

We further compute the mod-
p
reduction of
G^{\prime}_{1},G^{\prime}_{2}
as well as of
\phi^{\prime}_{r}(G^{\prime}_{1}),\phi^{\prime}_{r}(G^{\prime}_{2})
for each
k^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}_{r^{\prime}}
, which will be used to describe the corresponding Breuil modules, for the rest of this section. In fact, due to the simpler structures of the Breuil modules, it is more convenient to compute the mod-
p
reduction of
F_{1}^{\prime},F_{2}^{\prime}
, where

(F^{\prime}_{1},\,F^{\prime}_{2}):=\begin{cases}\big(G_{1}^{\prime}+\frac{x}{\Theta}G_{2}^{\prime},\,G_{2}^{\prime}\big)&\mbox{if }r^{\prime}=2m\mbox{ and }k^{\prime}=m;\\
\big(G_{1}^{\prime},\,\frac{\Theta}{x}G_{1}^{\prime}+G_{2}^{\prime}\big)&\mbox{if }r^{\prime}=2m\mbox{ and }k^{\prime}=m+\tfrac{1}{2};\\
\big(G_{1}^{\prime},\,G_{2}^{\prime}\big)&\mbox{otherwise. }\end{cases}

This modification only arises in Proposition
4.3.3
.

4.2.
Generators of the filtration

In this subsection, we prove Theorem
4.1.10
when
r^{\prime}\geq 2
and
k^{\prime}\neq\infty
. It is harmless to assume that
r=r^{\prime}
, as we can recover the general case
r^{\prime}\leq r
by multiplying
v^{r-r^{\prime}}
to the generators. Assume that
r=r^{\prime}\geq 2
, and set

\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{k}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}:=\mathrm{Fil}^{r;r}_{k}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}

for brevity.

We further define a
\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}
-module

\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:=\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}/(\mathfrak{m},\mathrm{Fil}^{p}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}})\mathfrak{M}^{\prime},

and write
\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}
for the image of
E^{\prime}_{1}
and
E^{\prime}_{2}
, respectively, under the natural quotient map
\pi^{\prime}:\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
. We also set

\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{k}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:=\pi^{\prime}(\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{k}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}).

In this subsection, we shall investigate the conditions for
[F^{\prime}_{k,i}]_{E^{\prime}_{j}}
’s to be integral. Also, under such conditions, we shall compute their mod-
p
reductions. Moreover, we will determine the
S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}
-generators of
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{k}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
for each
\mathbf{Case}~(k)
.

4.2.1.
In
\mathbf{Case}~(0)

In this section of paragraph, we describe the
S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}
-generators of
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{k}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
and the
\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}
-generators of
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{k}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
when
k=0
, i.e., in the case
\mathbf{Case}~(0)
.

Proposition 4.2.1
.

Assume
k=0
. Then
F^{\prime}_{0,i}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
for all
0\leq i\leq r-1
if and only if

(4.2.2)

v_{p}(\Theta)\leq v_{p}(x)\quad\mbox{ and }\quad v_{p}(\Theta)\leq-r+1,

in which case we have

\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{0}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}=S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}(F^{\prime}_{0,0},v^{r}E_{2}^{\prime})+\mathrm{Fil}^{p}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}

and

\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{0}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{0,0},u^{r}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2})
with

\overline{F}^{\prime}_{0,0}=\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}+\bigg(\frac{x}{\Theta}+\frac{1}{p^{r-1}\Theta}\frac{(-1)^{r-2}}{r-1}u^{r-1}\bigg)\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}.

Proof.

If
k=0
, then by Lemma
3.1.12
we have

\displaystyle[F^{\prime}_{0,i}]_{E^{\prime}_{1}}=p^{i}P_{0,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},x\Big)=v^{i}\,\,\mbox{ and }\,\,[F^{\prime}_{0,i}]_{E^{\prime}_{2}}=\frac{p^{i}}{\Theta}Q_{0,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},x\Big)=\frac{p^{i}}{\Theta}Q_{0,i}\Big(\dfrac{v}{p},0\Big)+\frac{x}{\Theta}v^{i}.

Note that by Lemma
3.1.12
we have

\circ

Q_{0,r-1}(T,0)=0
;

\circ

for
0\leq i\leq r-2
,
Q_{0,i}(T,0)=\sum_{n=0}^{r-2-i}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{n+1}T^{n+1+i}
.

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\frac{p^{i}}{\Theta}Q_{0,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)=\frac{p^{i}}{\Theta}\sum_{n=0}^{r-2-i}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{n+1}\Big(\frac{v}{p}\Big)^{n+1+i}.

Thus,
[F^{\prime}_{0,i}]_{E^{\prime}_{2}}\in S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}
if and only if

\displaystyle v_{p}(\Theta)\leq v_{p}(x)\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad v_{p}(\Theta)\leq-r+1,

in which case, we have

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{0,0}

\displaystyle\equiv E^{\prime}_{1}+\bigg(\frac{x}{\Theta}+\frac{1}{p^{r-1}\Theta}\frac{(-1)^{r-2}}{r-1}v^{r-1}\bigg)E^{\prime}_{2}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}};

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{0,i}

\displaystyle\equiv v^{i}E^{\prime}_{1}+\frac{x}{\Theta}v^{i}E^{\prime}_{2}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}}\,\,\,\mbox{ if }0<i\leq r-1.

We note that

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{0,i}

\displaystyle\equiv v^{i}F^{\prime}_{0,0}-\frac{1}{p^{r-1}\Theta}\frac{(-1)^{r-2}}{r-1}v^{i+r-1}E^{\prime}_{2}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}};

\displaystyle v^{r}E^{\prime}_{1}

\displaystyle\equiv v^{r}F^{\prime}_{0,0}-\bigg(\frac{x}{\Theta}+\frac{1}{p^{r-1}\Theta}\frac{(-1)^{r-2}}{r-1}v^{r-1}\bigg)v^{r}E^{\prime}_{2}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}}.

Hence, we deduce that
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{0}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=S^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{0,0},u^{r}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2})
. Now, by Nakayama’s Lemma,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{0}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}/\mathrm{Fil}^{p}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}=S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}/\mathrm{Fil}^{p}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}(F^{\prime}_{0,0},v^{r}E^{\prime}_{2}),

and so we have

(4.2.3)

\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{0}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}=S_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}^{\prime}(F^{\prime}_{0,0},v^{r}E^{\prime}_{2})+\mathrm{Fil}^{p}S_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}^{\prime}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime},

which completes the proof.
∎

4.2.2.
In
\mathbf{Case}~(k)
for
0<k<r/2

In this section of paragraph, we describe the
S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}
-generators of
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{k}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
and the
\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}
-generators of
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{k}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
in the case
\mathbf{Case}~(k)
for
0<k<r/2
.

Proposition 4.2.4
.

Assume that
1\leq k<r/2
. Then
F^{\prime}_{k,i}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
for all
0\leq i\leq r-1
if and only if

(4.2.5)

-r+2k-1\leq v_{p}(\Theta)\leq-r+2k+1\quad\mbox{ and }\quad v_{p}(\Theta)\leq v_{p}(x),

in which case we have

\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{k}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}=S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}(F^{\prime}_{k,k},F^{\prime}_{k,0})+\mathrm{Fil}^{p}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}

and
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{k}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{k,k},\overline{F}^{\prime}_{k,0})
with

\displaystyle\overline{F}^{\prime}_{k,k}

\displaystyle=u^{k}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}+\bigg(\dfrac{1}{p^{r-2k-1}\Theta}\dfrac{1}{a_{k+1}}u^{r-k-1}+\dfrac{x}{\Theta}u^{k}\bigg)\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2};

\displaystyle\overline{F}^{\prime}_{k,0}

\displaystyle=p^{r-2k+1}\Theta a_{k}u^{k-1}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}+(u^{r-k}+p^{r-2k+1}xa_{k}u^{k-1})\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}.

We prove this proposition by a series of lemmas, dividing the values of
i
into three cases:
0\leq i\leq k-1
,
k\leq i\leq r-k-1
, and
r-k\leq i\leq r-1
.

Lemma 4.2.6
.

Keep the assumption of Proposition
4.2.4
. Then
F^{\prime}_{k,i}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
for
0\leq i\leq k-1
if and only if

\displaystyle v_{p}(\Theta)\geq-r+2k-1\quad\mbox{ and }\quad v_{p}(x)\geq-r+2k-1,

in which case we have

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{k,0}

\displaystyle\equiv p^{r-2k+1}\Theta a_{k}v^{k-1}E^{\prime}_{1}+(v^{r-k}+p^{r-2k+1}xa_{k}v^{k-1})E^{\prime}_{2}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}};

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{k,i}

\displaystyle\equiv v^{i+r-k}E^{\prime}_{2}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}}\,\,\mbox{ if }0<i\leq k-1.

Proof.

Assume that
0\leq i\leq k-1
. By Lemma
3.2.2
we have

\displaystyle[F^{\prime}_{k,i}]_{E^{\prime}_{1}}

\displaystyle=p^{i+r-k}\Theta P_{k,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big);

\displaystyle[F^{\prime}_{k,i}]_{E^{\prime}_{2}}

\displaystyle=p^{i+r-k}Q_{k,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)+p^{i+r-k}xP_{k,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big).

Note that
\deg_{T}P_{k,i}(T,0)\leq k-1
by Lemma
3.1.14
and that
\deg_{T}P_{k,0}(T,0)=k-1
with the leading coefficient
a_{k}\in\mathbf{Z}_{(p)}^{\times}
by Lemma
3.2.1
. Hence, we have

\displaystyle p^{i+r-k}\Theta P_{k,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)\equiv\begin{cases}p^{r-2k+1}\Theta a_{k}v^{k-1}\pmod{p^{r-2k+1}\Theta\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}&\mbox{if }i=0;\\
0\pmod{p^{r-2k+1}\Theta\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}&\mbox{otherwise},\end{cases}

and

\displaystyle p^{i+r-k}xP_{k,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)\equiv\begin{cases}p^{r-2k+1}xa_{k}v^{k-1}\pmod{p^{r-2k+1}x\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}&\mbox{if }i=0;\\
0\pmod{p^{r-2k+1}x\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}&\mbox{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Moreover, as
Q_{k,i}(T,0)
is a monic polynomial of degree
i+r-k
by Lemma
3.1.14
, we have

\displaystyle p^{i+r-k}Q_{k,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)\equiv v^{i+r-k}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}.

Now, it is immediate that
F^{\prime}_{k,i}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
for
0\leq i\leq k-1
if and only if
v_{p}(\Theta)\geq-r+2k-1
and
v_{p}(x)\geq-r+2k-1
. It is also immediate that if
v_{p}(\Theta)\geq-r+2k-1
and
v_{p}(x)\geq-r+2k-1
then
\overline{F}^{\prime}_{k,i}
for
0\leq i\leq k-1
is computed as in the statement of Lemma
4.2.6
.
∎

Lemma 4.2.7
.

Keep the assumption of Proposition
4.2.4
. Then
F^{\prime}_{k,i}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
for
k\leq i\leq r-k-1
if and only if

\displaystyle v_{p}(\Theta)\leq-r+2k+1\quad\mbox{ and }\quad v_{p}(\Theta)\leq v_{p}(x),

in which case

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{k,k}

\displaystyle\equiv v^{k}E^{\prime}_{1}+\bigg(\frac{1}{p^{r-2k-1}\Theta}\frac{1}{a_{k+1}}v^{r-k-1}+\frac{x}{\Theta}v^{k}\bigg)E^{\prime}_{2}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}};

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{k,i}

\displaystyle\equiv v^{i}E^{\prime}_{1}+\frac{x}{\Theta}v^{i}E_{2}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}}\,\,\mbox{ if }k+1\leq i\leq r-k-1.

Proof.

Assume that
k\leq i\leq r-k-1
. By Lemma
3.2.2
we have

\displaystyle[F^{\prime}_{k,i}]_{E^{\prime}_{1}}

\displaystyle=p^{i}P_{k,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big);

\displaystyle[F^{\prime}_{k,i}]_{E^{\prime}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{p^{i}}{\Theta}Q_{k,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)+\frac{p^{i}x}{\Theta}P_{k,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big).

As
P_{k,i}(T,0)
is a monic polynomial of degree
i
by Lemma
3.1.14
, we have

\displaystyle p^{i}P_{k,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)\equiv v^{i}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\frac{p^{i}x}{\Theta}P_{k,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)\equiv\frac{x}{\Theta}v^{i}\pmod{\frac{x}{\Theta}\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}.

Moreover, note that
{1}/{a_{k+1}}\in\mathbf{Z}_{(p)}
by Lemma
3.2.1
(iii) and Lemma
3.2.9
(v), that
\deg_{T}Q_{k,i}(T,0)\leq r-k-1
, and that
\deg_{T}Q_{k,k}(T,0)=r-k-1
with the leading coefficient
1/a_{k+1}\in\mathbf{Z}_{(p)}
by Lemma
3.1.14
together with Lemma
3.2.1
, and so we have

\displaystyle\frac{p^{i}}{\Theta}Q_{k,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)\equiv\begin{cases}\frac{1}{p^{r-2k-1}\Theta}\frac{1}{a_{k+1}}v^{r-k-1}\pmod{\frac{1}{p^{r-2k-1}\Theta}\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}&\mbox{if }i=k;\\
0\pmod{\frac{1}{p^{r-2k-1}\Theta}\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}&\mbox{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Now, it is immediate that
F^{\prime}_{k,i}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
for
k\leq i\leq r-k-1
if and only if
v_{p}(\Theta)\leq-r+2k+1
and
v_{p}(\Theta)\leq v_{p}(x)
. It is also immediate that if
v_{p}(\Theta)\leq-r+2k+1
and
v_{p}(\Theta)\leq v_{p}(x)
then
\overline{F}^{\prime}_{k,i}
for
k\leq i\leq r-k-1
is computed as in the statement of Lemma
4.2.7
.
∎

From Lemma
4.2.6
and Lemma
4.2.7
, it is easy to see that

F^{\prime}_{k,i}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
for
0\leq i\leq r-k-1
if and only if the inequalities in (
4.2.5
) hold. In the next lemma, we will see that we don’t get any extra inequalities from the case
r-k\leq i\leq r-1
.

Lemma 4.2.8
.

Keep the assumption of Proposition
4.2.4
, and assume further

v_{p}(x)\geq-r+2k-1,\quad v_{p}(\Theta)-2v_{p}(x)\leq r-2k+1,\quad\mbox{and}\quad v_{p}(\Theta)\leq v_{p}(x).

Then we have

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{k,r-k}

\displaystyle\equiv(v^{r-k}-p^{r-2k+1}xa_{k}v^{k-1})E^{\prime}_{1}+\frac{p^{r-2k+1}x^{2}}{\Theta}v^{k-1}E^{\prime}_{2}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}};

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{k,i}

\displaystyle\equiv v^{i}E^{\prime}_{1}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}}\,\,\mbox{ if }r-k<i\leq r-1.

Proof.

Assume that
r-k\leq i\leq r-1
. By Lemma
3.2.2
, we have

\displaystyle[F^{\prime}_{k,i}]_{E^{\prime}_{1}}

\displaystyle=p^{i}P_{k,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)-p^{i}xP_{k,i-r+k}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big);

\displaystyle[F^{\prime}_{k,i}]_{E^{\prime}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{p^{i}}{\Theta}Q_{k,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)+\frac{p^{i}x}{\Theta}\Big(P_{k,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)-Q_{k,i-r+k}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)\Big)-\frac{p^{i}x^{2}}{\Theta}P_{k,i-r+k}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big).

Since
P_{k,i}(T,0)
is a monic polynomial of degree
i
by Lemma
3.1.14
, we have

\displaystyle p^{i}P_{k,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)\equiv v^{i}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}.

We note that
\deg_{T}P_{k,i-r+k}(T,0)\leq k-1
for all
r-k<i\leq r-1
and that
\deg_{T}P_{k,0}(T,0)=k-1
with the leading coefficient
a_{k}\in\mathbf{Z}_{(p)}^{\times}
, by Lemma
3.1.14
and Lemma
3.2.1
, and so we have

\displaystyle p^{i}xP_{k,i-r+k}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)\equiv\begin{cases}p^{r-2k+1}xa_{k}v^{k-1}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}S_{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}}&\mbox{if }i=r-k;\\
0\pmod{\mathfrak{m}S_{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}}&\mbox{otherwise}\end{cases}

as
v_{p}(x)\geq-r+2k-1
, and

\displaystyle\frac{p^{i}x^{2}}{\Theta}P_{k,i-r+k}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)\equiv\begin{cases}\frac{p^{r-2k+1}x^{2}}{\Theta}a_{k}v^{k-1}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}S_{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}}&\mbox{if }i=r-k;\\
0\pmod{\mathfrak{m}S_{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}}&\mbox{otherwise}\end{cases}

as
v_{p}(\Theta)-2v_{p}(x)\leq r-2k+1
. Since
\deg_{T}Q_{k,i}(T,0)\leq r-k-1
by Lemma
3.1.14
and
v_{p}(\Theta)\leq 0
, for all
r-k\leq i\leq r-1
we have

\displaystyle\frac{p^{i}}{\Theta}Q_{k,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)\equiv 0\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}}.

As
\deg_{T}\big(P_{k,i}(T,0)-Q_{k,i-r+k}(T,0)\big)\leq r-k-1
by Lemma
3.1.14
, for all
r-k\leq i\leq r-1

we have

\displaystyle\frac{p^{i}x}{\Theta}\Big(

\displaystyle P_{k,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)-Q_{k,i-r+k}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)\Big)\equiv 0\pmod{\mathfrak{m}S_{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}}

as
v_{p}(\Theta)\leq v_{p}(x)
.

Now, it is immediate that if the inequalities in the statement of Lemma
4.2.8
hold then
\overline{F}^{\prime}_{k,i}
for
r-k\leq i\leq r-1
is computed as in the statement of Lemma
4.2.8
.
∎

It is easy to see that the inequalities in (
4.2.5
) imply the ones in Lemma
4.2.8
. Hence, from the three lemmas above, we now conclude that
F^{\prime}_{k,i}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
for all
0\leq i\leq r-1
if and only if the inequalities in (
4.2.5
) hold.

Finally, we need to check that
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{k}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
is generated by
\overline{F}^{\prime}_{k,k},\,\overline{F}^{\prime}_{k,0}
over
\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}
.

Proof of Proposition
4.2.4
.

Assume that the inequalities in (
4.2.5
) hold, and note that
\overline{F}^{\prime}_{k,i}
for all
0\leq i\leq r-1
are computed in the preceding lemmas.

We note from Lemma
4.2.6
and Lemma
4.2.7
that

\displaystyle vF^{\prime}_{k,0}-p^{r-2k+1}\Theta a_{k}F^{\prime}_{k,k}\equiv v^{r-k+1}E^{\prime}_{2}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}}.

Hence, if
1\leq i\leq k-1
then we have

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{k,i}\equiv v^{i-1}(vF^{\prime}_{k,0}-p^{r-2k+1}\Theta a_{k}F^{\prime}_{k,k})\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}},

and if
k+1\leq i\leq r-k-1
then we have

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{k,i}

\displaystyle\equiv v^{i-k}F^{\prime}_{k,k}-\frac{1}{p^{r-2k-1}\Theta a_{k+1}}v^{i-k-2}(vF^{\prime}_{k,0}-p^{r-2k+1}\Theta a_{k}F^{\prime}_{k,k})

\displaystyle\equiv v^{i-k}F^{\prime}_{k,k}-\frac{1}{p^{r-2k-1}\Theta a_{k+1}}v^{i-k-1}F^{\prime}_{k,0}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}}.

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{k,r-k}

\displaystyle\equiv v^{r-k}E^{\prime}_{1}-\frac{x}{\Theta}\big(F^{\prime}_{k,0}-v^{r-k}E^{\prime}_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\equiv v^{r-2k}(v^{k}E^{\prime}_{1}+\frac{x}{\Theta}v^{k}E^{\prime}_{2})-\frac{x}{\Theta}F^{\prime}_{k,0}

\displaystyle\equiv v^{r-2k}F^{\prime}_{k,k}-\frac{1}{p^{r-2k-1}\Theta a_{k+1}}v^{2r-3k-1}E^{\prime}_{2}-\frac{x}{\Theta}F^{\prime}_{k,0}

\displaystyle\equiv v^{r-2k}F^{\prime}_{k,k}-\frac{1}{p^{r-2k-1}\Theta a_{k+1}}v^{r-2k}\left(vF^{\prime}_{k,0}-p^{r-2k+1}\Theta a_{k}F^{\prime}_{k,k}\right)-\frac{x}{\Theta}F^{\prime}_{k,0}

modulo
\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
, and if
r-k+1\leq i\leq r-1
then we also have

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{k,i}

\displaystyle\equiv v^{i-k}F^{\prime}_{k,k}-v^{i-k}\bigg(\frac{1}{p^{r-2k-1}\Theta a_{k+1}}v^{r-k-1}+\frac{x}{\Theta}v^{k}\bigg)E^{\prime}_{2}

\displaystyle\equiv v^{i-k}F^{\prime}_{k,k}-\bigg(\frac{1}{p^{r-2k-1}\Theta a_{k+1}}v^{i-k-2}+\frac{x}{\Theta}v^{i-r+k-1}\bigg)(vF^{\prime}_{k,0}-p^{r-2k+1}\Theta a_{k}F^{\prime}_{k,k})

\displaystyle\equiv(v^{i-k}-p^{r-2k+1}xa_{k}v^{i-r+k-1})F^{\prime}_{k,k}-\bigg(\frac{1}{p^{r-2k-1}\Theta a_{k+1}}v^{i-k-1}+\frac{x}{\Theta}v^{i-r+k}\bigg)F^{\prime}_{k,0}

modulo
\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
.

Finally, it is immediate that

v^{r}E^{\prime}_{2}\equiv v^{k}F^{\prime}_{k,0}-p^{r-2k+1}\Theta a_{k}v^{k-1}F^{\prime}_{k,k}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}},

and

\displaystyle v^{r}E^{\prime}_{1}

\displaystyle\equiv v^{r-k}F^{\prime}_{k,k}-\bigg(\frac{1}{p^{r-2k-1}\Theta a_{k+1}}v^{2r-2k-1}+\frac{x}{\Theta}v^{r}\bigg)E^{\prime}_{2}

\displaystyle\equiv v^{r-k}F^{\prime}_{k,k}-\bigg(\frac{1}{p^{r-2k-1}\Theta a_{k+1}}v^{r-k-2}+\frac{x}{\Theta}v^{k-1}\bigg)\left(vF^{\prime}_{k,0}-p^{r-2k+1}\Theta a_{k}F^{\prime}_{k,k}\right)

\displaystyle\equiv(v^{r-k}+p^{r-2k+1}xa_{k}v^{k-1})F^{\prime}_{k,k}-\bigg(\frac{1}{p^{r-2k-1}\Theta a_{k+1}}v^{r-k-1}+\frac{x}{\Theta}v^{k}\bigg)F^{\prime}_{k,0}

modulo
\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
.

Hence, we deduce that
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{k}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{k,k},\overline{F}^{\prime}_{k,0})
. Now, by the same argument as in (
4.2.3
), we have
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{k}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}=S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}(F^{\prime}_{k,k},F^{\prime}_{k,0})+\mathrm{Fil}^{p}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
, which completes the proof.
∎

4.2.3.
In
\mathbf{Case}~(m)
for
r=2m

In this section of paragraph, we describe the
S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}
-generators of
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
and the
\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}
-generators of
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{m}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
in the case
\mathbf{Case}~(m)
when
r=2m
.

Proposition 4.2.9
.

Assume that
r=2m
and
k=m
and that
v_{p}(\Theta)\leq 0
. Then
F^{\prime}_{m,i}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
for all
0\leq i\leq r-1
if and only if

(4.2.10)

-1\leq v_{p}(\Theta)\quad\mbox{ and }\quad v_{p}(\Theta)-2v_{p}(x)\leq 1,

in which case we have

\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}=S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}(F^{\prime}_{m,m},F^{\prime}_{m,0})+\mathrm{Fil}^{p}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}

and
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{m}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{m,m},\overline{F}^{\prime}_{m,0})
with

\displaystyle\overline{F}^{\prime}_{m,m}

\displaystyle=(u^{m}-pxa_{m}u^{m-1})\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}-\frac{px^{2}}{\Theta}a_{m}u^{m-1}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2};

\displaystyle\overline{F}^{\prime}_{m,0}

\displaystyle=p\Theta a_{m}u^{m-1}E^{\prime}_{1}+(u^{m}+pxa_{m}u^{m-1})\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}.

We prove this proposition by a series of lemmas, dividing the values of
i
into two cases:
0\leq i\leq m-1
and
m\leq i\leq r-1
.

Lemma 4.2.11
.

Keep the assumption of Proposition
4.2.9
. Then
F^{\prime}_{m,i}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
for
0\leq i\leq m-1
if and only if

\displaystyle v_{p}(\Theta)\geq-1\quad\mbox{ and }\quad v_{p}(x)\geq-1,

in which case we have

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{m,0}

\displaystyle\equiv p\Theta a_{m}v^{m-1}E^{\prime}_{1}+(v^{m}+pxa_{m}v^{m-1})E^{\prime}_{2}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}};

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{m,i}

\displaystyle\equiv v^{i+m}E^{\prime}_{2}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}}\,\,\mbox{ if }1\leq i\leq m-1.

Proof.

Assume that
0\leq i\leq m-1
. By Lemma
3.2.2
we have

\displaystyle[F^{\prime}_{m,i}]_{E^{\prime}_{1}}

\displaystyle=p^{i+m}\Theta P_{m,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big);

\displaystyle[F^{\prime}_{m,i}]_{E^{\prime}_{2}}

\displaystyle=p^{i+m}Q_{m,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)+p^{i+m}xP_{m,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big).

Note that
\deg_{T}P_{m,i}(T,0)\leq m-1
for all
0\leq i\leq m-1
by Lemma
3.1.14
, and that
\deg_{T}P_{m,0}(T,0)=m-1
with the leading coefficient
a_{m}\in\mathbf{Z}_{(p)}^{\times}
by Lemma
3.2.1
(iii). Hence, we have

\displaystyle p^{i+m}\Theta P_{m,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)

\displaystyle\equiv\begin{cases}p\Theta a_{m}v^{m-1}\pmod{p\Theta\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}&\mbox{if }i=0;\\
0\pmod{p\Theta\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}&\mbox{otherwise}\end{cases}

and

\displaystyle p^{i+m}xP_{m,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)

\displaystyle\equiv\begin{cases}pxa_{m}v^{m-1}\pmod{px\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}&\mbox{if }i=0;\\
0\pmod{px\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}&\mbox{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Moreover, since
Q_{k,i}(T,0)
is a monic polynomial of degree
i+m
by Lemma
3.1.14
, we have

\displaystyle p^{i+m}Q_{m,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)\equiv v^{i+m}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}S_{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}}.

Now, it is immediate that
F^{\prime}_{m,i}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
for
0\leq i\leq m-1
if and only if
v_{p}(\Theta)\geq-1
and
v_{p}(x)\geq-1
. It is also immediate that if
v_{p}(\Theta)\geq-1
and
v_{p}(x)\geq-1
then
\overline{F}^{\prime}_{m,i}
for
0\leq i\leq m-1
is computed as in the statement of Lemma
4.2.11
.
∎

Lemma 4.2.12
.

Keep the assumption of Proposition
4.2.9
, and assume further that
v_{p}(\Theta)\geq-1
and
v_{p}(x)\geq-1
. Then
F^{\prime}_{m,i}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
for
m\leq i\leq r-1
if and only if

\displaystyle\quad v_{p}(\Theta)-2v_{p}(x)\leq 1,

in which case we have

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{m,m}

\displaystyle\equiv(v^{m}-pxa_{m}v^{m-1})E^{\prime}_{1}-\frac{px^{2}}{\Theta}a_{m}v^{m-1}E^{\prime}_{2}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}};

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{m,i}

\displaystyle\equiv v^{i}E^{\prime}_{1}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}}\,\,\mbox{ if }m+1\leq i\leq r-1.

Proof.

Assume that
m\leq i\leq r-1
. By Lemma
3.2.2
we have

\displaystyle[F^{\prime}_{m,i}]_{E^{\prime}_{1}}

\displaystyle=p^{i}P_{m,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)-p^{i}xP_{m,i-m}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big);

\displaystyle[F^{\prime}_{m,i}]_{E^{\prime}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{p^{i}}{\Theta}Q_{m,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)+\frac{p^{i}x}{\Theta}\Big(P_{m,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)-Q_{m,i-m}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)\Big)-\frac{p^{i}x^{2}}{\Theta}P_{m,i-m}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big).

Since
P_{m,i}(T,0)
is a monic polynomial of degree
i
by Lemma
3.1.14
, we have

\displaystyle p^{i}P_{m,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)\equiv v^{i}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}.

Similarly, as
\deg_{T}Q_{m,i}(T,0)\leq m-1
by Lemma
3.1.14
and
v_{p}(\Theta)\leq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{p^{i}}{\Theta}Q_{m,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)\equiv 0\pmod{\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}.

Moreover, we note that by Lemma
3.1.14

\deg_{T}P_{m,i-m}(T,0)\leq m-1
, and that by Lemma
3.2.1

\deg_{T}P_{m,0}(T,0)=m-1
with the leading coefficient
a_{m}\in\mathbf{Z}_{(p)}^{\times}
. Hence, we have

\displaystyle p^{i}xP_{m,i-m}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)

\displaystyle\equiv\begin{cases}pxa_{m}v^{m-1}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}&\mbox{if }i=m;\\
0\pmod{\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}&\mbox{otherwise}\end{cases}

as
v_{p}(x)\geq-1
, and

\displaystyle\frac{p^{i}x^{2}}{\Theta}P_{m,i-m}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)

\displaystyle\equiv\begin{cases}\frac{px^{2}}{\Theta}a_{m}v^{m-1}\pmod{\frac{px^{2}}{\Theta}\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}&\mbox{if }i=m;\\
0\pmod{\frac{px^{2}}{\Theta}\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}&\mbox{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Finally, it remains to deal with the term

\frac{p^{i}x}{\Theta}\Big(P_{m,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)-Q_{m,i-m}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)\Big).

By Lemma
3.1.14
we have
\deg_{T}(P_{m,i}(T,0)-T^{i})\leq m-1
and
\deg_{T}(Q_{m,i-m}(T,0)-T^{i})\leq m-1
, and so
\deg_{T}\left(P_{m,i}(T,0)-Q_{m,i-m}(T,0)\right)\leq m-1
for all
m\leq i\leq r-1
. If we write
b_{m}
for the coefficient of
T^{m-1}
in
P_{m,m}(T,0)-Q_{m,0}(T,0)
, then

\displaystyle\frac{p^{i}x}{\Theta}\Big(P_{m,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)-Q_{m,i-m}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)\Big)\equiv\begin{cases}\frac{px}{\Theta}b_{m}v^{m-1}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}&\mbox{if }i=m;\\
0\pmod{\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}&\mbox{otherwise,}\end{cases}

as the inequalities
v_{p}(x)\geq-1
and
v_{p}(\Theta)\leq 0
imply that
v_{p}(px/\Theta)\geq 0
.

In fact, it is easy to see that
v_{p}(\Theta)-2v_{p}(x)\leq 1
implies that
v_{p}(px/\Theta)>0
. Indeed, if
v_{p}(x)>-1
then it is obvious that
v_{p}(px/\Theta)>0
as
v_{p}(\Theta)\leq 0
. If
v_{p}(x)=-1
, then we have
v_{p}(\Theta)\leq-1
from
v_{p}(\Theta)-2v_{p}(x)\leq 1
, and so we have
v_{p}(\Theta)=-1
, as
v_{p}(\Theta)\geq-1
.
Hence, we conclude that if
v_{p}(x)=-1
then
v_{p}(px/\Theta)=1
.

Now, it is immediate that
F^{\prime}_{m,i}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
for
m\leq i\leq r-1
if and only if
v_{p}(\Theta)-2v_{p}(x)\leq 1
. It is also immediate that if the inequalities in the statement of Lemma
4.2.12
hold then
\overline{F}^{\prime}_{m,i}
for
m\leq i\leq r-1
is computed as in the statement of Lemma
4.2.12
.
∎

From Lemma
4.2.11
and Lemma
4.2.12
, it is easy to see that

F^{\prime}_{k,i}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
for
0\leq i\leq r-1
if and only if the inequalities in (
4.2.10
) hold. Note that
v_{p}(\Theta)-2v_{p}(x)\leq 1
together with
v_{p}(\Theta)\geq-1
implies that
v_{p}(x)\geq-1
.

Finally, we need to check that
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{m}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
is generated by
\overline{F}^{\prime}_{m,m},\,\overline{F}^{\prime}_{m,0}
over
\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}
.

Proof of Proposition
4.2.9
.

Assume that the inequalities in (
4.2.10
) hold, and note that
\overline{F}^{\prime}_{m,i}
for all
0\leq i\leq r-1
are computed in the preceding lemmas.

We note from Lemma
4.2.11
and Lemma
4.2.12
that

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{m,0}

\displaystyle\equiv v^{m}E^{\prime}_{2}+p\Theta a_{m}v^{m-1}\bigg(E^{\prime}_{1}+\frac{x}{\Theta}E^{\prime}_{2}\bigg)\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}};

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{m,m}

\displaystyle\equiv v^{m}E^{\prime}_{1}-pxa_{m}v^{m-1}\bigg(E^{\prime}_{1}+\frac{x}{\Theta}E^{\prime}_{2}\bigg)\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}},

and so we have

\displaystyle p\Theta\bigg(F^{\prime}_{m,m}+\frac{x}{\Theta}F^{\prime}_{m,0}\bigg)\equiv p\Theta v^{m}\bigg(E^{\prime}_{1}+\frac{x}{\Theta}E^{\prime}_{2}\bigg)\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}}.

Hence, if
1\leq i\leq m-1
then we have

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{m,i}\equiv v^{i}F^{\prime}_{m,0}-p\Theta a_{m}v^{i-1}\bigg(F^{\prime}_{m,m}+\dfrac{x}{\Theta}F^{\prime}_{m,0}\bigg)\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}},

and if
m+1\leq i\leq r-1
then we have

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{m,i}\equiv v^{i-m}F^{\prime}_{m,m}+pxa_{m}v^{i-m-1}\bigg(F^{\prime}_{m,m}+\dfrac{x}{\Theta}F^{\prime}_{m,0}\bigg)\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}}.

Now, it is also immediate that
u^{r}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1},\,u^{r}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}\in\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{m,m},\overline{F}^{\prime}_{m,0})
, as

v^{r}E^{\prime}_{1}\equiv v^{m-1}F^{\prime}_{m,m+1}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}}\quad\mbox{ and }\quad v^{r}E^{\prime}_{2}\equiv v^{m-1}F^{\prime}_{m,1}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}}.

Hence, we deduce that
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{m}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{m,m},\overline{F}^{\prime}_{m,0})
. Now, by the same argument as in (
4.2.3
), we have

\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}=S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}(F^{\prime}_{m,m},F^{\prime}_{m,0})+\mathrm{Fil}^{p}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
, which completes the proof.
∎

4.2.4.
In
\mathbf{Case}~(k)
for
m+1\leq k\leq r-1

In this section of paragraph, we describe the
S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}
-generators of
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{k}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
and the
\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}
-generators of
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{k}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
in the case
\mathbf{Case}~(k)
for
m+1\leq k<r
. In this case, it is enough to consider
x\in E
with
v_{p}(x)<0
.

Proposition 4.2.13
.

Assume that
m+1\leq k\leq r-1
and that
v_{p}(\Theta)\leq 0
and
v_{p}(x)<0
. Then
F^{\prime}_{k,i}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
for all
0\leq i\leq r-1
if and only if

(4.2.14)

v_{p}(x)\leq v_{p}(\Theta)\quad\mbox{ and }\quad 2k-r-1\leq v_{p}(\Theta)-2v_{p}(x)\leq 2k-r+1,

in which case

\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{k}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}=S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}(F^{\prime}_{k,k},F^{\prime}_{k,0})+\mathrm{Fil}^{p}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}

and
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{k}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{k,k},\overline{F}^{\prime}_{k,0})
with

\displaystyle\overline{F}^{\prime}_{k,k}

\displaystyle=(u^{k}-p^{2k-r+1}xa_{r-k}u^{r-k-1})\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}-\frac{p^{2k-r+1}x^{2}}{\Theta}a_{r-k}u^{r-k-1}E^{\prime}_{2};

\displaystyle\overline{F}^{\prime}_{k,0}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{\Theta}{x}u^{r-k}-\frac{\Theta}{p^{2k-r-1}x^{2}}a^{\prime}_{r,k}u^{k-1}\bigg)\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}+u^{r-k}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2},

where
a^{\prime}_{r,k}:=0
if
(r,k)=(2m+1,m+1)
and
a^{\prime}_{r,k}:=1/a_{r-k+1}
otherwise.

We prove this proposition by a series of lemmas, dividing the values of
i
into three cases:
0\leq i\leq 2k-r-1
,
2k-r\leq i\leq k-1
, and
k\leq i\leq r-1
. We will frequently use the following: if
v_{p}(x)<0
then

(4.2.15)

\frac{x^{s}}{d_{k}(x)}\equiv\frac{1}{x^{2k-r-s}}\pmod{\frac{1}{x^{2k-r-s}}\mathfrak{m}}

by Lemma
3.2.5
(iii).

Lemma 4.2.16
.

Keep the assumption of Proposition
4.2.13
. Then
F^{\prime}_{k,i}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
for
0\leq i\leq 2k-r-1
if and only if

\displaystyle v_{p}(x)\leq v_{p}(\Theta)\quad\mbox{ and }\quad v_{p}(\Theta)-2v_{p}(x)\geq 2k-r-1,

in which case we have

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{k,0}

\displaystyle\equiv\bigg(\dfrac{\Theta}{x}v^{r-k}-\dfrac{\Theta}{p^{2k-r-1}x^{2}}a_{r,k}^{\prime}v^{k-1}\bigg)E^{\prime}_{1}+v^{r-k}E^{\prime}_{2}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}};

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{k,i}

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{\Theta}{x}v^{i+r-k}E^{\prime}_{1}+v^{i+r-k}E^{\prime}_{2}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}}\,\,\mbox{ if }1\leq i\leq 2k-r-1,

where
a^{\prime}_{r,k}:=0
if
(r,k)=(2m+1,m+1)
and
a^{\prime}_{r,k}:=1/a_{r-k+1}
otherwise.

Proof.

Assume that
0\leq i\leq 2k-r-1
. By Lemma
3.2.3
we have

\displaystyle[F^{\prime}_{k,i}]_{E^{\prime}_{1}}

\displaystyle=p^{i+r-k}\Theta\frac{x^{2k-r-1}}{d_{k}(x)}P_{r-k,i+r-k}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)+p^{i+r-k}\Theta\sum_{s=0}^{2k-r-2}\frac{x^{s}}{d_{k}(x)}P_{k,i,s}\Big(\frac{v}{p}\Big);

\displaystyle[F^{\prime}_{k,i}]_{E^{\prime}_{2}}

\displaystyle=p^{i+r-k}\sum_{s=0}^{2k-r}\frac{x^{s}}{d_{k}(x)}Q_{k,i,s}\Big(\frac{v}{p}\Big)

As
P_{r-k,i+r-k}(T,0)
is a monic polynomial of degree
i+r-k
and
\deg_{x}d_{k}(x)=2k-r
, we have

\displaystyle p^{i+r-k}\Theta\frac{x^{2k-r-1}}{d_{k}(x)}P_{r-k,i+r-k}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{\Theta}{x}p^{i+r-k}P_{r-k,i+r-k}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)\pmod{\frac{\Theta}{x}\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{\Theta}{x}v^{i+r-k}\pmod{\frac{\Theta}{x}\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}

where the first congruence is due to (
4.2.15
). (Note that if
k=m+1
and
r=2m+1
then we have
2k-r-1=0
.)

We need to compute the remaining terms of
[F^{\prime}_{k,i}]_{E^{\prime}_{1}}
if
(r,k)\neq(2m+1,m+1)
. We note that if
(r,k)\neq(2m+1,m+1)
then

\circ

\deg P_{k,i,s}(T)\leq k-1
for
s\leq 2k-r-2
by Lemma
3.1.14
;

\circ

\deg_{T}P_{k,0,2k-r-2}(T)=k-1
with the leading coefficient
-1/a_{r-k+1}\in\mathbf{Z}_{(p)}^{\times}
by (iii) and (iv) of Lemma
3.2.9
.

Hence, if
(r,k)\neq(2m+1,m+1)
and
s\leq 2k-r-2
then we have

\displaystyle p^{i+r-k}\Theta\frac{x^{s}}{d_{k}(x)}P_{k,i,s}\Big(\frac{v}{p}\Big)

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{\Theta}{p^{2k-r-1-i}x^{2k-r-s}}p^{k-1}P_{k,i,s}\Big(\frac{v}{p}\Big)

\displaystyle\equiv\begin{cases}-\frac{\Theta}{p^{2k-r-1}x^{2}}\frac{1}{a_{r-k+1}}v^{k-1}&\mbox{if }(i,s)=(0,2k-r-2);\\
0&\mbox{otherwise}\end{cases}

modulo
\frac{\Theta}{p^{2k-r-1}x^{2}}\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}
, where the first congruence is due to (
4.2.15
).

As
v_{p}(x^{s}/d_{k}(x))\geq 0
for
s\leq 2k-r

\displaystyle Q_{k,i}(T,x)=\sum_{s=0}^{2k-r}\frac{x^{s}}{d_{k}(x)}Q_{k,i,s}(T)\in\mathcal{O}[T].

Moreover,
Q_{k,i}(T,x)
is a monic polynomial of degree
i+r-k
in
T
by Lemma
3.1.14
, and so we have

\displaystyle[F^{\prime}_{k,i}]_{E^{\prime}_{2}}=p^{i+r-k}Q_{k,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},x\Big)\equiv v^{i+r-k}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}.

Now, it is immediate that
F^{\prime}_{k,i}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
for
0\leq i\leq 2k-r-1
if and only if
v_{p}(x)\leq v_{p}(\Theta)
and
v_{p}(\Theta)-2v_{p}(x)\geq 2k-r-1
. It is also immediate that if
v_{p}(x)\leq v_{p}(\Theta)
and
v_{p}(\Theta)-2v_{p}(x)\geq 2k-r-1
then
\overline{F}^{\prime}_{k,i}
for
0\leq i\leq 2k-r-1
is computed as in the statement of Lemma
4.2.16
.
∎

Lemma 4.2.17
.

Keep the assumption of Proposition
4.2.13
, and assume further that
v_{p}(x)\leq v_{p}(\Theta)
. Then
F^{\prime}_{k,i}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
for
2k-r\leq i\leq k-1
if and only if

\displaystyle v_{p}(x)\geq-2k+r-1,

in which case we have

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{k,2k-r}

\displaystyle\equiv p^{2k-r+1}\Theta a_{r-k}v^{r-k-1}E^{\prime}_{1}+\big(p^{2k-r+1}xa_{r-k}v^{r-k-1}+v^{k}\big)E^{\prime}_{2}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}};

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{k,i}

\displaystyle\equiv v^{i+r-k}E^{\prime}_{2}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}}\,\,\mbox{ if }2k-r+1\leq i\leq k-1.

Proof.

Assume that
2k-r\leq i\leq k-1
. By Lemma
3.2.3
we have

\displaystyle[F^{\prime}_{k,i}]_{E^{\prime}_{1}}

\displaystyle=p^{i+r-k}\Theta\frac{x^{2k-r}}{d_{k}(x)}P_{r-k,i+r-2k}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)+p^{i+r-k}\Theta\sum_{s=0}^{2k-r-1}\frac{x^{s}}{d_{k}(x)}P_{k,i,s}\Big(\frac{v}{p}\Big);

\displaystyle[F^{\prime}_{k,i}]_{E^{\prime}_{2}}

\displaystyle=p^{i+r-k}\frac{x^{2k-r+1}}{d_{k}(x)}P_{r-k,i+r-2k}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)+p^{i+r-k}\sum_{s=0}^{2k-r}\frac{x^{s}}{d_{k}(x)}Q_{k,i,s}\Big(\frac{v}{p}\Big).

As
\deg_{T}P_{r-k,i+r-2k}(T,0)\leq r-k-1
by Lemma
3.1.14
and
\deg_{T}P_{r-k,0}(T,0)=r-k-1
with the leading coefficient
a_{r-k}\in\mathbf{Z}_{(p)}^{\times}
by Lemma
3.2.1
, we have

\displaystyle p^{i+r-k}\Theta\frac{x^{2k-r}}{d_{k}(x)}P_{r-k,i+r-2k}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)

\displaystyle\equiv p^{i+r-k}\Theta P_{r-k,i+r-2k}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)\pmod{p^{2k-r+1}\Theta\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}

\displaystyle\equiv\begin{cases}p^{2k-r+1}\Theta a_{r-k}v^{r-k-1}\pmod{p^{2k-r+1}\Theta\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}&\mbox{if }i=2k-r;\\
0\pmod{p^{2k-r+1}\Theta\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}&\mbox{otherwise}\end{cases}

and

\displaystyle p^{i+r-k}\frac{x^{2k-r+1}}{d_{k}(x)}P_{r-k,i+r-2k}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)

\displaystyle\equiv p^{i+r-k}xP_{r-k,i+r-2k}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)\pmod{p^{2k-r+1}x\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}

\displaystyle\equiv\begin{cases}p^{2k-r+1}xa_{r-k}v^{r-k-1}\pmod{p^{2k-r+1}x\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}&\mbox{if }i=2k-r;\\
0\pmod{p^{2k-r+1}x\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}&\mbox{otherwise,}\end{cases}

where the first congruence in each case is due to (
4.2.15
). Moreover, as
\deg_{T}P_{k,i,s}(T)\leq k-1
for
0\leq s\leq 2k-r-1
by Lemma
3.1.14
we have

\displaystyle p^{i+r-k}\Theta\frac{x^{s}}{d_{k}(x)}P_{k,i,s}\Big(\frac{v}{p}\Big)\equiv p^{i+r-k}\frac{\Theta}{x^{2k-r-s}}P_{k,i,s}\Big(\frac{v}{p}\Big)\equiv 0\pmod{\frac{\Theta}{x}\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}.

Finally, by Lemma
3.2.3
we have

\displaystyle Q_{k,i}(T,x)-\frac{x^{2k-r+1}}{d_{k}(x)}P_{r-k,i+r-2k}(T,0)=\sum_{s=0}^{2k-r}\frac{x^{s}}{d_{k}(x)}Q_{k,i,s}(T)\in\mathcal{O}[T],

as
v_{p}(x^{s}/d_{k}(x))\geq 0
for
s\leq 2k-r
, which is a monic polynomial of degree
i+r-k
by Lemma
3.1.14
, and so

\displaystyle p^{i+r-k}\sum_{s=0}^{2k-r}\frac{x^{s}}{d_{k}(x)}Q_{k,i,s}\Big(\frac{v}{p}\Big)\equiv v^{i+r-k}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}.

Hence, under the assumption
v_{p}(x)\leq v_{p}(\Theta)
, we have
F^{\prime}_{k,i}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
for
2k-r\leq i\leq k-1
if and only if
v_{p}(x)\geq-2k+r-1
. It is also immediate that if
v_{p}(x)\leq v_{p}(\Theta)
and
v_{p}(x)\geq-2k+r-1
then
\overline{F}^{\prime}_{k,i}
for
2k-r\leq i\leq k-1
is computed as in the statement of Lemma
4.2.17
.
∎

Lemma 4.2.18
.

Keep the assumption of Proposition
4.2.13
. Then
F^{\prime}_{k,i}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
for
k\leq i\leq r-1
if and only if

\displaystyle v_{p}(x)\geq-2k+r-1\quad\mbox{ and }\quad v_{p}(\Theta)-2v_{p}(x)\leq 2k-r+1,

in which case

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{k,k}

\displaystyle\equiv\big(v^{k}-p^{2k-r+1}xa_{r-k}v^{r-k-1}\big)E^{\prime}_{1}-\frac{p^{2k-r+1}x^{2}}{\Theta}a_{r-k}v^{r-k-1}E^{\prime}_{2}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}};

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{k,i}

\displaystyle\equiv v^{i}E^{\prime}_{1}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}}\,\,\mbox{ if }k+1\leq i\leq r-1.

Proof.

Assume that
k\leq i\leq r-1
. By Lemma
3.2.3
we have

\displaystyle[F^{\prime}_{k,i}]_{E^{\prime}_{1}}

\displaystyle=-p^{i}\frac{x^{2k-r+1}}{d_{k}(x)}P_{r-k,i-k}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)+p^{i}\sum_{s=0}^{2k-r}\frac{x^{s}}{d_{k}(x)}P_{k,i,s}\Big(\frac{v}{p}\Big);

\displaystyle[F^{\prime}_{k,i}]_{E^{\prime}_{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{p^{i}}{\Theta}\frac{x^{2k-r+2}}{d_{k}(x)}P_{r-k,i-k}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)+\frac{p^{i}}{\Theta}\sum_{s=0}^{2k-r+1}\frac{x^{s}}{d_{k}(x)}Q_{k,i,s}\Big(\frac{v}{p}\Big).

By Lemma
3.1.14

\deg_{T}P_{r-k,j-k}(T,0)\leq r-k-1
for
k\leq j\leq r-1
and by Lemma
3.2.1

\deg_{T}P_{r-k,0}(T,0)=r-k-1
with the leading coefficient
a_{r-k}\in\mathbf{Z}_{(p)}^{\times}
, and so we have

\displaystyle p^{i}\frac{x^{2k-r+1}}{d_{k}(x)}P_{r-k,i-k}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)

\displaystyle\equiv p^{i-r+k+1}xp^{r-k-1}P_{r-k,i-k}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)\pmod{p^{2k-r+1}x\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}

\displaystyle\equiv\begin{cases}p^{2k-r+1}xa_{r-k}v^{r-k-1}\pmod{p^{2k-r+1}x\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}&\mbox{if }i=k;\\
0\pmod{p^{2k-r+1}x\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}&\mbox{otherwise}\end{cases}

and

\displaystyle\frac{p^{i}}{\Theta}\frac{x^{2k-r+2}}{d_{k}(x)}P_{r-k,i-k}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{p^{i-r+k+1}x^{2}}{\Theta}p^{r-k-1}P_{r-k,i-k}\Big(\frac{v}{p},0\Big)\pmod{\frac{p^{2k-r+1}x^{2}}{\Theta}\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}

\displaystyle\equiv\begin{cases}\frac{p^{2k-r+1}x^{2}}{\Theta}a_{r-k}v^{r-k-1}\pmod{\frac{p^{2k-r+1}x^{2}}{\Theta}\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}&\mbox{if }i=k;\\
0\pmod{\frac{p^{2k-r+1}x^{2}}{\Theta}\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}&\mbox{otherwise,}\end{cases}

where the first congruence in each case is due to (
4.2.15
). Moreover, by Lemma
3.2.3
we have

\displaystyle P_{k,i}(T,x)+\frac{x^{2k-r+1}}{d_{k}(x)}P_{r-k,i-k}(T,0)=\sum_{s=0}^{2k-r}\frac{x^{s}}{d_{k}(x)}P_{k,i,s}(T)\in\mathcal{O}[T],

as
v_{p}(x^{s}/d_{k}(x))\geq 0
for
s\leq 2k-r
, which is a monic polynomial of degree
i
by Lemma
3.1.14
, and so

\displaystyle p^{i}\sum_{s=0}^{2k-r}\frac{x^{s}}{d_{k}(x)}P_{k,i,s}\Big(\frac{v}{p}\Big)\equiv v^{i}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}.

Finally, we have
\deg Q_{k,i,s}(T)\leq r-k-1
by Lemma
3.1.14
, and so if
s\leq 2k-r+1
then we have

\displaystyle\frac{p^{i}}{\Theta}\frac{x^{s}}{d_{k}(x)}Q_{k,i,s}\Big(\frac{v}{p}\Big)\equiv\frac{p^{i}}{\Theta}\frac{1}{x^{2k-r-2}}Q_{k,i,s}\Big(\frac{v}{p}\Big)\equiv 0\pmod{\frac{p^{2k-r+1}x^{2}}{\Theta}\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}.

Hence, we have
F^{\prime}_{k,i}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
for
k\leq i\leq r-1
if and only if
v_{p}(x)\geq-2k+r-1
and
v_{p}(X)-2v_{p}(x)\leq 2k-r+1
. It is also immediate that if
v_{p}(x)\geq-2k+r-1
and
v_{p}(X)-2v_{p}(x)\leq 2k-r+1
then
\overline{F}^{\prime}_{k,i}
for
k\leq i\leq r-1
is computed as in the statement of Lemma
4.2.18
.
∎

From Lemma
4.2.16
, Lemma
4.2.17
, and Lemma
4.2.18
, it is easy to see that
F^{\prime}_{k,i}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
for
0\leq i\leq r-1
if and only if the inequalities in (
4.2.14
) hold. Note that
v_{p}(\Theta)-2v_{p}(x)\leq 2k-r+1
together with
v_{p}(x)\leq v_{p}(\Theta)
implies
v_{p}(x)\geq-2k+r-1
.

Finally, we need to check that
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{k}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
is generated by
\overline{F}^{\prime}_{k,k},\,\overline{F}^{\prime}_{k,0}
over
\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}
.

Proof of Proposition
4.2.13
.

Assume that the inequalities in (
4.2.14
) hold, and note that
\overline{F}^{\prime}_{k,i}
for all
0\leq i\leq r-1
are computed in the preceding lemmas.

We first note that

(4.2.19)

\frac{\Theta}{p^{2k-r-1}x^{2}}F^{\prime}_{k,k}\equiv\frac{\Theta}{p^{2k-r-1}x^{2}}v^{k}E^{\prime}_{1}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}}

and

(4.2.20)

vF^{\prime}_{k,k}+\frac{p^{2k-r+1}x^{2}}{\Theta}a_{r-k}F^{\prime}_{k,0}\equiv v^{k+1}E^{\prime}_{1}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}}.

Hence, if
1\leq i\leq 2k-r-1
then by (
4.2.19
) we have

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{k,i}

\displaystyle\equiv v^{i}F^{\prime}_{k,0}+\frac{\Theta}{p^{2k-r-1}x^{2}}a^{\prime}_{r,k}v^{i+k-1}E^{\prime}_{1}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\equiv v^{i}F^{\prime}_{k,0}+\frac{\Theta}{p^{2k-r-1}x^{2}}a^{\prime}_{r,k}v^{i-1}F^{\prime}_{k,k}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}},

and if
i=2k-r
then by (
4.2.19
) we have

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{k,2k-r}

\displaystyle\equiv-\frac{\Theta}{x}F^{\prime}_{k,k}+\frac{\Theta}{x}v^{k}E^{\prime}_{1}+v^{k}E^{\prime}_{2}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\equiv-\frac{\Theta}{x}F^{\prime}_{k,k}+v^{2k-r}\bigg(F^{\prime}_{k,0}+\frac{\Theta}{p^{2k-r-1}x^{2}}a^{\prime}_{r,k}v^{k-1}E^{\prime}_{1}\bigg)\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\equiv-\bigg(\frac{\Theta}{x}-\frac{\Theta}{p^{2k-r-1}x^{2}}a^{\prime}_{r,k}v^{2k-r-1}\bigg)F^{\prime}_{k,k}+v^{2k-r}F^{\prime}_{k,0}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}}.

Moreover, if
2k-r+1\leq i\leq k-1
then by (
4.2.19
) and (
4.2.20
)

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{k,i}

\displaystyle\equiv v^{i}F^{\prime}_{k,0}-\frac{\Theta}{x}v^{i+r-k}E_{1}^{\prime}+\frac{\Theta}{p^{2k-r-1}x^{2}}a^{\prime}_{r,k}v^{i+k-1}E_{1}^{\prime}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\equiv v^{i}F^{\prime}_{k,0}-\frac{\Theta}{x}v^{i+r-2k}F^{\prime}_{k,k}-p^{2k-r+1}xa_{r-k}v^{i+r-2k-1}F^{\prime}_{k,0}

\displaystyle\hskip 110.00017pt+\frac{\Theta}{p^{2k-r-1}x^{2}}a^{\prime}_{r,k}v^{i-1}F_{k,k}^{\prime}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}},

and if
k+1\leq i\leq r-1
then by (
4.2.20
)

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{k,j}\equiv v^{j-k}F^{\prime}_{k,k}+\frac{p^{2k-r+1}x^{2}}{\Theta}a_{r-k}v^{j-k-1}F^{\prime}_{k,0}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}}.

Now, it is also immediate that
u^{r}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1},\,u^{r}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}\in\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{k,k},\overline{F}^{\prime}_{k,0})
, as

v^{r}E^{\prime}_{1}\equiv vF^{\prime}_{k,r-1}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}}\quad\mbox{ and }\quad v^{r}E^{\prime}_{2}\equiv vF^{\prime}_{k,k-1}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}}.

Hence, we deduce that
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{k}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{k,k},\overline{F}^{\prime}_{k,0})
. Now, by the same argument as in (
4.2.3
), we have

\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{k}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}=S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}(F^{\prime}_{k,k},F^{\prime}_{k,0})+\mathrm{Fil}^{p}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
, which completes the proof.
∎

4.2.5.
In
\mathbf{Case}~(r)

In this section of paragraph, we describe the
S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}
-generators of
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{r}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
and the
\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}
-generators of
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{r}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
when
k=r
, i.e., in the case
\mathbf{Case}~(r)
.

Proposition 4.2.21
.

Assume that
k=r
and that
v_{p}(\Theta)\leq 0
. Then
F^{\prime}_{r,i}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
for all
0\leq i\leq r-1
if and only if

(4.2.22)

v_{p}(x)\leq v_{p}(\Theta)\quad\mbox{ and }\quad v_{p}(\Theta)-2v_{p}(x)\geq r-1,

in which case

\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{r}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}=S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}(v^{r}E_{1}^{\prime},F^{\prime}_{r,0})+\mathrm{Fil}^{p}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}

and
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{r}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}(u^{r}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1},\overline{F}^{\prime}_{r,0})
with

\overline{F}^{\prime}_{r,0}=\bigg(\frac{\Theta}{x}+\frac{\Theta}{p^{r-1}x^{2}}\frac{(-1)^{r-1}}{r-1}u^{r-1}\bigg)\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}+\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}.

Note that the inequalities in (
4.2.22
) imply that
v_{p}(x)<0
.

Proof.

If
k=r
, then by Lemma
3.1.12
we have

\displaystyle[F^{\prime}_{r,i}]_{E^{\prime}_{1}}=p^{i}\Theta P_{r,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p},x\Big)=\frac{\Theta}{x}v^{i}+\sum_{s=i}^{r-2}\frac{p^{i}\Theta}{x^{r-s}}P_{r,i,s}\Big(\frac{v}{p}\Big)\,\mbox{ and }\,[F^{\prime}_{r,i}]_{E^{\prime}_{2}}=p^{i}Q_{r,i}\Big(\frac{v}{p}\Big)=v^{i}.

We first claim that if
[F^{\prime}_{r,i}]_{E^{\prime}_{1}}\in S_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}^{\prime}
for all
0\leq i\leq r-1
then
v_{p}(x)<0
. Indeed, as
P_{r,r-2,r-2}(T)=-T^{r-1}
by Lemma
3.1.12
, we have

\displaystyle[F^{\prime}_{r,r-2}]_{E^{\prime}_{1}}=\frac{\Theta}{x}v^{r-2}-\frac{\Theta}{px^{2}}v^{r-1}.

Thus, if
[F^{\prime}_{r,r-2}]_{E^{\prime}_{1}}\in S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}
, then
v_{p}(\Theta/px^{2})\geq 0
, which gives

\displaystyle 2v_{p}(x)\leq v_{p}(\Theta)-1\leq-1<0

and so
v_{p}(x)<0
.

Note that the following three are equivalent:

\circ

\frac{p^{i}\Theta}{x^{r-s}}P_{r,i,s}\big(\frac{v}{p}\big)\in S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}
for all
0\leq i\leq s\leq r-2
;

\circ

\frac{\Theta}{x^{r-s}}P_{r,0,s}\big(\frac{v}{p}\big)\in S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}
all
0\leq s\leq r-2
;

\circ

\frac{\Theta}{x^{2}}P_{r,0,r-2}\big(\frac{v}{p}\big)\in S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}
,

where the first two items are equivalent as
\deg_{T}P_{r,i,s}(T)\leq r-1
for all
0\leq i\leq s\leq r-2
by Lemma
3.1.12
, and the last two items are equivalent as
v_{p}(x)<0
.

Now, as
P_{r,0,r-2}(T)=-[Tf_{r}(T)]^{(r-1)}_{T}
by Lemma
3.1.12
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\Theta}{x^{2}}P_{r,0,r-2}\Big(\frac{v}{p}\Big)\equiv\frac{\Theta}{p^{r-1}x^{2}}\frac{(-1)^{r-1}}{r-1}v^{r-1}\pmod{\frac{\Theta}{p^{r-1}x^{2}}\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}.

Hence,
F^{\prime}_{r,i}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
for all
0\leq i\leq r-1
if and only if

\displaystyle v_{p}(x)\leq v_{p}(\Theta)\quad\mbox{ and }\quad v_{p}(\Theta)-2v_{p}(x)\geq r-1,

in which case we have

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{r,0}

\displaystyle\equiv\bigg(\frac{\Theta}{x}+\frac{\Theta}{p^{r-1}x^{2}}\frac{(-1)^{r-1}}{r-1}v^{r-1}\bigg)E^{\prime}_{1}+E^{\prime}_{2}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}};

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{r,i}

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{\Theta}{x}v^{i}E^{\prime}_{1}+v^{i}E^{\prime}_{2}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}}\,\,\mbox{ if }0<i\leq r-1.

We note that

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{r,i}

\displaystyle\equiv v^{i}F^{\prime}_{r,0}-\frac{\Theta}{p^{r-1}x^{2}}\frac{(-1)^{r-1}}{r-1}v^{i-1}(v^{r}E^{\prime}_{1})\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}}\,\,\mbox{ if }0<i\leq r-1;

\displaystyle v^{r}E^{\prime}_{2}

\displaystyle\equiv v^{r}F^{\prime}_{r,0}-\bigg(\frac{\Theta}{x}+\frac{\Theta}{p^{r-1}x^{2}}\frac{(-1)^{r-1}}{r-1}v^{r-1}\bigg)v^{r}E^{\prime}_{1}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}}.

Hence, we deduce that
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{r}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}(u^{r}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1},\overline{F}^{\prime}_{r,0})
. Now, by the same argument as in (
4.2.3
), we have
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{r}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}=S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}(v^{r}E^{\prime}_{1},F^{\prime}_{r,0})+\mathrm{Fil}^{p}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
, which completes the proof.
∎

4.3.
Strong divisibility

In this subsection, we prove Theorem
4.1.24
when
r^{\prime}\geq 2
and
k^{\prime}\neq\infty
. We keep the notation of §
3
, §
4.1
, and §
4.2
. In particular, we assume
r=r^{\prime}\geq 2
. Note that it is harmless to assume
r=r^{\prime}
, as one can recover the general case
r^{\prime}\leq r
by simply multiplying
c^{r-r^{\prime}}
to
\phi^{\prime}_{r}(F^{\prime}_{1}),\phi^{\prime}_{r}(F^{\prime}_{2})
.

4.3.1.
In
\mathbf{Case}~(0)

In this section of paragraph, we describe the conditions for
(\Lambda,\Theta,\Omega,x)
to be good for
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(k^{\prime})
when
k^{\prime}=\frac{1}{2}
. We also determine the corresponding
\Delta
and describe the image of
\phi^{\prime}_{r}
in
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
in this case.

Proposition 4.3.1
.

Let
k^{\prime}=\frac{1}{2}
. If the quadruple
(\Lambda,\Theta,\Omega,x)
satisfies

(i)

v_{p}(\Lambda)=r-1
and
v_{p}(\Theta\Omega^{-1})=-r+1
;

(ii)

v_{p}(\Theta)\leq v_{p}(x)
and
v_{p}(\Omega)\leq 0
,

then the quadruple is good for
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\frac{1}{2})
with respect to
\Delta(T)=\delta_{1}(T)
, in which case

\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{0}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1},\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})
where

\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1}:=\pi^{\prime}(F^{\prime}_{0,0})\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2}:=\pi^{\prime}(v^{r}E^{\prime}_{2}),

and
\phi^{\prime}_{r}:\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{0}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
is described as

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda}{p^{r-1}}P_{0,0}(-1,0)\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}+\frac{\Lambda}{p^{r-1}\Omega}P_{0,0}(-1,0)\dot{\delta}_{1}(-1)u^{p-1}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}.;

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{r}\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{\Omega}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}.

Proof.

We start the proof by noting that the inequalities in (ii) is equivalent with (
4.2.2
) under the equation (i) and that the equations in (i) and the inequalities in (ii) imply the inequalities in (
4.1.8
).

Let
k:=\lfloor k^{\prime}\rfloor=0
and
\Delta=\delta_{1}
. By Lemma
4.1.14
, it is immediate that if
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(F^{\prime}_{0,0})]_{E^{\prime}_{1}}\in S_{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}
then
\phi^{\prime}_{r}(F^{\prime}_{0,0})
can be written as

\phi^{\prime}_{r}(F^{\prime}_{0,0})\equiv\frac{\Lambda}{p^{r-1}}P_{0,0}(c,x)E^{\prime}_{1}+\frac{\Lambda}{p^{r}\Omega}\big([P_{0,0}(c,x)cf(c)]_{c}^{(r-1)}-P_{0,0}(c,x)\Delta(\gamma-1)\big)E^{\prime}_{2}

modulo
\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
. As
c-(\gamma-1)\equiv pu^{p-1}\pmod{p^{2}}
, by Lemma
3.1.12
one can readily see that
\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})
can be written as in the statement.

By Lemma
3.1.12

P_{0,0}(c,x)=1
, and so if
v_{p}(\Lambda)=r-1
then
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}}=\frac{\Lambda}{p^{r-1}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
. It is easy to see that
v_{p}(\Omega)\leq 0
and
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda}{p^{r-1}})=0
imply that
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda}{p^{r-1}\Omega})\geq 0
, and so we further have
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}\in\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}
as
\dot{\delta}_{1}(-1)=r-1\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
by Lemma
3.3.7
(i).
Moreover, it is also immediate from (
4.1.4
) to have that
\phi_{r}^{\prime}(v^{r}E^{\prime}_{2})=c^{r}\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{\Omega}E^{\prime}_{2}
, and so we have
\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})
as in the statement and if
v_{p}(\Theta\Omega^{-1})=-r+1
and
v_{p}(\Lambda)=r-1
then
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{\Omega})=0
and so
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
. Hence, we conclude that
the quadruple is good for
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\frac{1}{2})
with respect to
\Delta=\delta_{1}
.
∎

4.3.2.
In
\mathbf{Case}~(k)
with
1\leq k<r/2

In this section of paragraph, we describe the conditions for
(\Lambda,\Theta,\Omega,x)
to be good for
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(k^{\prime})
when
k^{\prime}\in\{k,k+\frac{1}{2}\}
and
k\in\mathbf{Z}
with
1\leq k<r/2
. We also determine the corresponding
\Delta
and describe the image of
\phi^{\prime}_{r}
in
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
in this case.

Proposition 4.3.2
.

Let
k\in\mathbf{Z}
with
1\leq k<r/2
, and let
k^{\prime}\in\{k,k+\frac{1}{2}\}
. If the quadruple
(\Lambda,\Theta,\Omega,x)
satisfies

(i)

\begin{cases}v_{p}(\Lambda\Theta)=k-1\mbox{ and }v_{p}(\Theta\Omega)=-r+2k-1&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}=k;\\
v_{p}(\Lambda)=r-k-1\mbox{ and }v_{p}(\Theta\Omega^{-1})=-r+2k+1&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}=k+\frac{1}{2},\end{cases}

(ii)

v_{p}(\Theta)\leq v_{p}(x)
and
-1\leq v_{p}(\Omega)\leq 0
,

then the quadruple is good for
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(k^{\prime})
with respect to
\Delta(T)=\delta_{\lceil k^{\prime}\rceil}(T)
, in which case
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{k}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1},\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})
where

\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1}:=\pi^{\prime}(F^{\prime}_{k,k})\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2}:=\pi^{\prime}(F^{\prime}_{k,0}),

and the map

\phi^{\prime}_{r}:\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{k}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
is described as follows:

\circ

if
k^{\prime}=k
then

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda}{p^{r-k-1}}P_{k,k}(-1,0)\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}+\frac{\Lambda}{p^{r-k}\Omega}P_{k,k}(-1,0)\big(\delta_{k+1}(-1)-\delta_{k}(-1)\big)\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2};

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{k-1}}P_{k,0}(-1,0)\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}+\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{k-1}\Omega}P_{k,0}(-1,0)\dot{\delta}_{k}(-1)u^{p-1}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2},

\circ

if
k^{\prime}=k+\frac{1}{2}
then

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda}{p^{r-k-1}}P_{k,k}(-1,0)\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}+\frac{\Lambda}{p^{r-k-1}\Omega}P_{k,k}(-1,0)\dot{\delta}_{k+1}(-1)u^{p-1}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2};

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{k-1}}P_{k,0}(-1,0)\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}+\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{k}\Omega}P_{k,0}(-1,0)\big(\delta_{k}(-1)-\delta_{k+1}(-1)\big)\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}.

Proof.

We start the proof by noting that the inequalities in (ii) imply the ones in (
4.2.5
), and that the equations in (i) and the inequalities in (ii) imply the inequalities in (
4.1.8
).

Assume first that
k^{\prime}=k
, and let
\Delta=\delta_{k}
. Then we have
P_{k,0}(c,x)=P_{k,0}(c,0)
by Lemma
3.2.1
(iii), and
P_{k,0}(-1,0)\in\mathbf{Z}_{(p)}^{\times}
by Lemma
3.3.2
(i), and so if
v_{p}(\Lambda\Theta)=k-1
then
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}_{1}^{\prime}}=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{k-1}}P_{k,0}(-1,0)\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
. As
c-(\gamma-1)\equiv pu^{p-1}\pmod{p^{2}}
, by Lemma
3.1.14
(i) together with Lemma
3.2.2
(i) one can readily see that from (
4.1.16
)

\displaystyle[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{k}\Omega}\big(Q_{k,0}(c,0)-P_{k,0}(c,0)\delta_{k}(\gamma-1)\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{k}\Omega}P_{k,0}(c,0)\big(\delta_{k}(c)-\delta_{k}(\gamma-1)\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{k-1}\Omega}P_{k,0}(-1,0)\dot{\delta}_{k}(-1)u^{p-1}.

Hence,
\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})
can be written as in the statement, and we have
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}_{2}^{\prime}}\in u^{p-1}S_{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}
, by Lemma
3.3.7
together with Lemma
3.3.2
, as
v_{p}(\Lambda\Theta\Omega^{-1})\geq k-1
.

To satisfy (
4.1.12
), we should have
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}_{2}^{\prime}}\in(\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime})^{\times}
as
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}_{2}^{\prime}}\in u^{p-1}\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}
. However, by Lemma
3.1.14
(i) together with Lemma
3.2.2
(i) one can readily see that from (
4.1.15
)

\displaystyle[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda}{p^{r-k}\Omega}\left(Q_{k,k}(c,0)-P_{k,k}(c,0)\delta_{k}(\gamma-1)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda}{p^{r-k}\Omega}P_{k,k}(c,0)\left(\delta_{k+1}(c)-\delta_{k}(\gamma-1)\right).

Hence,
\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})
can be written as in the statement, and if
v_{p}(\Lambda\Theta)=k-1
and
v_{p}(\Theta\Omega)=-r+2k-1
then
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda}{\Omega})=v_{p}(\Lambda\Theta)-v_{p}(\Theta\Omega)=r-k
and so we have
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}_{2}^{\prime}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
, by Lemma
3.3.7
(i) together with Lemma
3.3.2
(ii). Moreover, we have
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}}\in\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}
as
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda}{p^{r-k-1}})=v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda}{p^{r-k}\Omega})+v_{p}(\Omega)+1\geq 0
.

Assume now that
k^{\prime}=k+\frac{1}{2}
, and let
\Delta=\delta_{k+1}
. Then we have
P_{k,k}(c,x)=P_{k,k}(c,0)
by Lemma
3.2.2
(i), and
P_{k,k}(-1,0)\in\mathbf{Z}_{(p)}^{\times}
by Lemma
3.3.2
(ii), and so if
v_{p}(\Lambda)=r-k-1
then
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}_{1}^{\prime}}=\frac{\Lambda}{p^{r-k-1}}P_{k,k}(-1,0)\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
. As
c-(\gamma-1)\equiv pu^{p-1}\pmod{p^{2}}
, by Lemma
3.1.14
(i) together with Lemma
3.2.2
(i) one can readily see that from (
4.1.15
)

\displaystyle[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda}{p^{r-k}\Omega}\left(Q_{k,k}(c,0)-P_{k,k}(c,0)\delta_{k+1}(\gamma-1)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda}{p^{r-k}\Omega}P_{k,k}(c,0)\left(\delta_{k+1}(c)-\delta_{k+1}(\gamma-1)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda}{p^{r-k-1}\Omega}P_{k,k}(c,0)\dot{\delta}_{k+1}(-1)u^{p-1}.

Hence,
\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})
can be written as in the statement, and we have
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}_{2}^{\prime}}\in u^{p-1}S_{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}
, by Lemma
3.3.7
(i) together with Lemma
3.3.2
(ii), as
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda}{p^{r-k-1}\Omega})=v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda}{p^{r-k-1}})-v_{p}(\Omega)\geq 0
.

To satisfy (
4.1.12
), we should have
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}_{2}^{\prime}}\in(\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime})^{\times}
as
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}_{2}^{\prime}}\in u^{p-1}\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}
. However, by Lemma
3.1.14
(i) together with Lemma
3.2.2
(i) one can readily see that from (
4.1.16
)

\displaystyle[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{k}\Omega}\big(Q_{k,0}(c,0)-P_{k,0}(c,0)\delta_{k+1}(\gamma-1)\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{k}\Omega}P_{k,0}(c,0)\big(\delta_{k}(c)-\delta_{k+1}(\gamma-1)\big).

Hence,
\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})
can be written as in the statement, and if
v_{p}(\Lambda)=r-k-1
and
v_{p}(\Theta\Omega^{-1})=-r+2k+1
then
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{\Omega})=v_{p}(\Lambda)+v_{p}(\Theta\Omega^{-1})=k
and so we have
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}_{2}^{\prime}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
, by Lemma
3.3.2
(i) together with Lemma
3.3.4
(ii). Moreover, we have
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}}\in\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}
as
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{k-1}})=v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{k}\Omega})+v_{p}(\Omega)+1\geq 0
. This completes the proof.
∎

4.3.3.
In
\mathbf{Case}~(m)
with
r=2m

In this section of paragraph, we describe the conditions for
(\Lambda,\Omega,\Theta,x)
to be good for
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(k^{\prime})
when
k^{\prime}\in\{m,m+\frac{1}{2}\}
and
r=2m
. We also determine the corresponding
\Delta
and describe the image of
\phi^{\prime}_{r}
in
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
in this case.

Proposition 4.3.3
.

Let
k=m
with
r=2m
, let
k^{\prime}\in\{m,m+\frac{1}{2}\}
, and assume that
v_{p}(\Theta)\leq 0
for
k^{\prime}=m+\frac{1}{2}
. If the quadruple
(\Lambda,\Theta,\Omega,x)
satisfies

(i)

\begin{cases}v_{p}(\Lambda\Theta)=m-1\mbox{ and }v_{p}(\Theta\Omega)=-1&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}=m;\\
v_{p}(\Lambda x)=m-1\mbox{ and }v_{p}(\Theta\Omega^{-1}x^{-2})=1&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}=m+\frac{1}{2},\end{cases}

(ii)

\begin{cases}v_{p}(\Theta)\leq v_{p}(x)\mbox{ and }-1\leq v_{p}(\Omega)\leq 0&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}=m;\\
v_{p}(\Theta)\geq v_{p}(x)<0\mbox{ and }v_{p}(\Omega)\leq 0&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}=m+\frac{1}{2},\end{cases}

then the quadruple is good for
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(k^{\prime})
with respect to

\Delta(T)=\begin{cases}\delta_{m}(T)&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}=m;\\
\delta_{m+1}(T,x)&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}=m+\frac{1}{2},\end{cases}

in which case

\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{m}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1},\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})
where

\begin{cases}\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1}:=\pi^{\prime}\big(F^{\prime}_{m,m}+\frac{x}{\Theta}F^{\prime}_{m,0}\big)\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2}:=\pi^{\prime}(F^{\prime}_{m,0})&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}=m;\\
\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1}:=\pi^{\prime}(F^{\prime}_{m,m})\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2}:=\pi^{\prime}\big(\frac{\Theta}{x}F^{\prime}_{m,m}+F^{\prime}_{m,0}\big)&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}=m+\frac{1}{2},\end{cases}

and the map
\phi^{\prime}_{r}:\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{m}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
is described as follows:

\circ

if
k^{\prime}=m
then

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda}{p^{m-1}}P_{m,m}(-1,0)\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}-\frac{\Lambda}{p^{m}\Omega}\frac{(-1)^{r}}{P_{m,0}(-1,0)}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2};

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{m-1}}P_{m,0}(-1,0)\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}+\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{m-1}\Omega}P_{m,0}(-1,0)\dot{\delta}_{m}(-1)u^{p-1}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2},

\circ

if
k^{\prime}=m+\frac{1}{2}
then

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{m-1}}P_{m,0}(-1,0)\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}-\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{m-1}\Omega}P_{m,0}(-1,0)\dot{\delta}_{m}(-1)u^{p-1}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2};

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{m}\Omega}\frac{(-1)^{r}}{P_{m,0}(-1,0)}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}.

Proof.

We start the proof by noting that the inequalities in (ii) imply the ones in (
4.2.10
) under the equations in (i), and that the equations in (i) and the inequalities in (ii) imply the inequalities in (
4.1.8
).

Assume first that
k^{\prime}=m
, and let
\Delta=\delta_{m}
. Then we have
P_{m,0}(c,x)=P_{m,0}(c,0)
by Lemma
3.2.1
(iii), and
P_{m,0}(-1,0)\in\mathbf{Z}_{(p)}^{\times}
by Lemma
3.3.2
(i), and so if
v_{p}(\Lambda\Theta)=m-1
then
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}_{1}^{\prime}}=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{m-1}}P_{m,0}(-1,0)\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
. As
c-(\gamma-1)\equiv pu^{p-1}\pmod{p^{2}}
, by Lemma
3.1.14
(i) together with Lemma
3.2.2
(i) one can readily see that from (
4.1.16
)

(4.3.4)

\displaystyle\begin{split}[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}&=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{m}\Omega}\big(Q_{m,0}(c,0)-P_{m,0}(c,0)\delta_{m}(\gamma-1)\big)\\
&=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{m}\Omega}P_{m,0}(c,0)\big(\delta_{m}(c)-\delta_{m}(\gamma-1)\big)\\
&=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{m-1}\Omega}P_{m,0}(-1,0)\dot{\delta}_{m}(-1)u^{p-1}.\end{split}

Hence,
\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})
can be written as in the statement, and we have
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}_{2}^{\prime}}\in u^{p-1}\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}
, by Lemma
3.3.7
together with Lemma
3.3.2
, as
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{m-1}\Omega})=v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{m-1}})-v_{p}(\Omega)\geq 0
.

To satisfy (
4.1.12
), we should have
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}_{2}^{\prime}}\in(\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime})^{\times}
as
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}_{2}^{\prime}}\in u^{p-1}\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}
. However, one can readily see that from (
4.1.15
)

\displaystyle[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{m,m})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda}{p^{m}\Omega}P_{m,m}(c,x)\big(\delta_{m+1}(c,x)-\delta_{m}(\gamma-1)\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda}{p^{m}\Omega}P_{m,m}(c,x)\left[\big(\delta_{m+1}(c,x)-\delta_{m}(c)\big)+\big(\delta_{m}(c)-\delta_{m}(\gamma-1)\big)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda}{p^{m}\Omega}\frac{-c^{r}}{P_{m,0}(c,0)}+\frac{\Lambda}{p^{m}\Omega}\big(P_{m,m}(c,0)-xP_{m,0}(c,0)\big)\big(\delta_{m}(c)-\delta_{m}(\gamma-1)\big)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Lambda}{p^{m}\Omega}\frac{c^{r}}{P_{m,0}(c,0)}+\frac{\Lambda}{p^{m-1}\Omega}\big(P_{m,m}(c,0)-xP_{m,0}(c,0)\big)\dot{\delta}_{m}(-1)u^{p-1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Lambda}{p^{m}\Omega}\frac{c^{r}}{P_{m,0}(c,0)}-\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{m-1}\Omega}P_{m,0}(c,0)\dot{\delta}_{m}(-1)u^{p-1},

where the first equality is due to Lemma
3.1.14
(i), Lemma
3.2.1
, and Lemma
3.2.2
(ii), the third equality is due to Lemma
3.3.4
(iii) and Lemma
3.2.2
(ii), the forth equality is due to
c-(\gamma-1)\equiv pu^{p-1}\pmod{p^{2}}
, and the last equality is due to
v_{p}(\Lambda\Omega^{-1})=v_{p}(\Lambda\Theta)-v_{p}(\Theta\Omega)=m
. Hence, together with (
4.3.4
) it is immediate that
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}
can be written as in the statement, and if
v_{p}(\Lambda\Theta)=m-1
and
v_{p}(\Theta\Omega)=-1
then we have
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}_{2}^{\prime}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
, by Lemma
3.3.2
(i). Moreover, by Lemma
3.2.2
and Lemma
3.2.1
(iii) we have

\displaystyle[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}}=\frac{\Lambda}{p^{m-1}}P_{m,m}(c,x)+\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{m-1}}P_{m,0}(c,x)=\frac{\Lambda}{p^{m-1}}P_{m,m}(c,0),

and so
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}}=\frac{\Lambda}{p^{m-1}}P_{m,m}(-1,0)\in\mathbf{F}
as
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda}{p^{m-1}})=v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{m-1}})-v_{p}(\Theta\Omega)+v_{p}(\Omega)\geq-(-1)+(-1)=0
.

Assume now that
k^{\prime}=m+\frac{1}{2}
, and let
\Delta(T)=\delta_{m+1}(T,x)
. Then we have
P_{m,m}(c,x)=P_{m,m}(c,0)-xP_{m,0}(c,0)
by Lemma
3.2.2
(ii), and
P_{m,0}(-1,0)\in\mathbf{Z}_{(p)}^{\times}
by Lemma
3.3.2
(i), and so if
v_{p}(\Lambda x)=m-1
and
v_{p}(x)<0
then
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}_{1}^{\prime}}=-\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{m-1}}P_{m,0}(-1,0)\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
. Moreover, one can readily see that from (
4.1.15
)

(4.3.5)

\displaystyle\begin{split}[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}&=\frac{\Lambda}{p^{m}\Omega}P_{m,m}(c,x)\left(\delta_{m+1}(c,x)-\delta_{m+1}(\gamma-1,x)\right)\\
&=\frac{\Lambda}{p^{m}\Omega}(P_{m,m}(c,0)-xP_{m,0}(c,0))\left(\delta_{m+1}(c,x)-\delta_{m+1}(\gamma-1,x)\right)\\
&=-\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{m-1}\Omega}P_{m,0}(c,0)\dot{\delta}_{m+1}(-1,x)u^{p-1}\\
&=-\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{m-1}\Omega}P_{m,0}(c,0)\dot{\delta}_{m}(-1)u^{p-1},\end{split}

where the first equality is due to Lemma
3.1.14
(i), Lemma
3.2.1
, and Lemma
3.2.2
(ii), the second equality is due to Lemma
3.2.2
(ii), and the third equality is due to
c-(\gamma-1)\equiv pu^{p-1}\pmod{p^{2}}
and
v_{p}(x)<0
, and the last equality is due to Lemma
3.3.4
(iii) and
v_{p}(x)<0
. Hence,
\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})
can be written as in the statement, and we have
\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})_{\overline{E}_{2}^{\prime}}\in u^{p-1}\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}
, by Lemma
3.3.4
(ii) and Lemma
3.3.2
(i), as
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{m-1}\Omega})=v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{m-1}})-v_{p}(\Omega)\geq 0
.

To satisfy (
4.1.12
), we should have
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}_{2}^{\prime}}\in(\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime})^{\times}
as
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}_{2}^{\prime}}\in u^{p-1}\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}
. However, one can readily see that from (
4.1.16
)

\displaystyle[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{m,0})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{m}\Omega}P_{m,0}(c,0)\big(\delta_{m}(c)-\delta_{m+1}(\gamma-1,x)\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{m}\Omega}P_{m,0}(c,0)\big[\big(\delta_{m}(c)-\delta_{m+1}(c,x)\big)+\big(\delta_{m+1}(c,x)-\delta_{m+1}(\gamma-1,x)\big)\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{m}\Omega}\frac{c^{r}}{P_{m,m}(c,x)}+\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{m-1}\Omega}P_{m,0}(c,0)\dot{\delta}_{m+1}(-1,x)u^{p-1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{m}\Omega}\frac{c^{r}}{P_{m,0}(c,0)}+\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{m-1}\Omega}P_{m,0}(c,0)\dot{\delta}_{m}(-1)u^{p-1},

where the first equality is due to Lemma
3.1.14
and Lemma
3.2.1
(iii), and the third equality is due to Lemma
3.3.4
(iii) and
c-(\gamma-1)\equiv pu^{p-1}\pmod{p^{2}}
