, and the last equality is due to Lemma
3.2.2
and
v_{p}(x)<0
. Hence, together with (
4.3.5
) it is immediate that
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}
can be written as in the statement, and if

v_{p}(\Lambda\Theta\Omega^{-1}x^{-1})=v_{p}(\Lambda x)+v_{p}(\Theta\Omega^{-1}x^{-2})=m
then we have
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}_{2}^{\prime}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
, by Lemma
3.3.2
(i). Moreover, by Lemma
3.2.2
and Lemma
3.2.1
(iii) we have

\displaystyle[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}}=\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{m-1}}P_{m,m}(c,x)+\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{m-1}}P_{m,0}(c,x)=\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{m-1}}P_{m,m}(c,0),

and so
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}}=\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{m-1}}P_{m,m}(-1,0)=0
as
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{m-1}})=v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{m-1}})+v_{p}(\Theta x^{-1})-v_{p}(x)>0
. This completes the proof.
∎

4.3.4.
In
\mathbf{Case}~(m+1)
with
m+1<r

In this section of paragraph, we describe the conditions for
(\Lambda,\Theta,\Omega,x)
to be good for
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(k^{\prime})
when
k^{\prime}\in\{m+1,m+1+\frac{1}{2}\}
and
r\geq 3
. We also determine the corresponding
\Delta
and describe the image of
\phi^{\prime}_{r}
in
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
in this case.

For
0\leq k\leq r
, we define

(4.3.6)

b_{r,k}(T):=-\frac{[P_{m+1,m+1,1}(T)Tf_{r}(T)]_{T}^{(r-1)}-P_{m+1,m+1,1}(T)\delta_{m}(T)}{P_{m,0}(T,0)}

if
(r,k)=(2m+1,m+2)
, and
b_{r,k}(T):=0
otherwise. Note that an identity for
b_{2m+1,m+2}
has been illustrated in Lemma
3.3.11
.

Proposition 4.3.7
.

Let
k=m+1
with
k<r
, let
k^{\prime}\in\{k,k+\frac{1}{2}\}
, and assume that
v_{p}(\Theta)\leq 0
and
v_{p}(x)<0
.

If the quadruple
(\Lambda,\Theta,\Omega,x)
satisfies

(i)

\begin{cases}v_{p}(\Lambda\Theta x^{-1})=m\mbox{ and }v_{p}(\Theta\Omega x^{-2})=-r+2m+1&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}=m+1;\\
v_{p}(\Lambda x)=r-m-2\mbox{ and }v_{p}(\Theta\Omega^{-1}x^{-2})=-r+2m+3&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}=m+1+\frac{1}{2},\end{cases}

(ii)

v_{p}(\Theta)\geq v_{p}(x)
and

-1\leq v_{p}(\Omega)\leq 0
,

then the quadruple is good for
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(k^{\prime})
with respect to

\Delta(T)=\begin{cases}\delta_{m+1}(T,x)&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}=m+1;\\
\delta_{r-m-1}(T)&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}=m+1+\frac{1}{2},\end{cases}

in which case
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{m+1}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1},\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})
where

\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1}:=\pi^{\prime}(F^{\prime}_{m+1,m+1})\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2}:=\pi^{\prime}(F^{\prime}_{m+1,0}),

and the map
\phi^{\prime}_{r}:\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{m+1}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
is described as follows:

\circ

if
k^{\prime}=m+1
then

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{r-m-2}}P_{r-m-1,0}(-1,0)\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt-\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{r-m-1}\Omega}P_{r-m-1,0}(-1,0)\big(\delta_{r-m-1}(-1)-\delta_{m+1}(-1,x)\big)\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2};

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{m}}P_{r-m-1,r-m-1}(-1,0)\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}

\displaystyle\hskip 30.00005pt+\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{m}\Omega}P_{r-m-1,r-m-1}(-1,0)\dot{\delta}_{r-m}(-1)u^{p-1}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2},

\circ

if
k^{\prime}=m+1+\frac{1}{2}
then

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{r-m-2}}P_{r-m-1,0}(-1,0)\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt-\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{r-m-2}\Omega}P_{r-m-1,0}(-1,0)\Big(\dot{\delta}_{r-m-1}(-1)u^{p-1}+\frac{b_{r,m+2}(-1)}{px}\Big)\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2};

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{m}}P_{r-m-1,r-m-1}(-1,0)\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{m+1}\Omega}P_{r-m-1,r-m-1}(-1,0)\big(\delta_{r-m}(-1)-\delta_{r-m-1}(-1)\big)\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}.

Proof.

We start the proof by noting that the inequalities in (ii) imply the ones in (
4.2.14
) for
k=m+1
under the equations in (i), and that the equations in (i) and the inequalities in (ii) imply the inequalities in (
4.1.8
).

We let
t=2(m+1)-r
for brevity. Assume first that
k^{\prime}=m+1
, and let
\Delta(T)=\delta_{m+1}(T,x)
. Then we have

(4.3.8)

P_{m+1,0}(T,x)=\frac{\tilde{P}_{m+1,0}(T,x)}{d_{m+1}(x)}\equiv x^{-1}P_{r-m-1,r-m-1}(T,0)\pmod{x^{-1}\mathfrak{m}}

by Lemma
3.2.3
and Lemma
3.3.2
(ii), and so by Lemma
3.3.2
if
v_{p}(\Lambda\Theta x^{-1})=m
and
v_{p}(x)<0
then

\displaystyle[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}}=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{m}}\frac{\tilde{P}_{m+1,0}(c,x)}{d_{m+1}(x)}=\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{m}}P_{r-m-1,r-m-1}(-1,0)\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}.

Moreover, one can readily see that from (
4.1.16
) together with (
4.3.8
)

\displaystyle[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{m+1}\Omega}P_{m+1,0}(c,x)\big(\delta_{m+1}(c,x)-\delta_{m+1}(\gamma-1,x)\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{m+1}\Omega}P_{r-m-1,r-m-1}(c,0)\big(\delta_{m+1}(c,x)-\delta_{m+1}(\gamma-1,x)\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{m}\Omega}P_{r-m-1,r-m-1}(-1,0)\dot{\delta}_{m+1}(-1,x)u^{p-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{m}\Omega}P_{r-m-1,r-m-1}(-1,0)\dot{\delta}_{r-m}(-1)u^{p-1},

where the third equality is due to
c-(\gamma-1)\equiv pu^{p-1}\pmod{p^{2}}
and the last equality is due to Lemma
3.3.7
(iv). Hence,
\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})
can be written as in the statement, and we have
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}_{2}^{\prime}}\in u^{p-1}\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}
, by Lemma
3.3.2
and Lemma
3.3.4
, as
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{m}\Omega})=v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{m}})-v_{p}(\Omega)\geq 0
.

To satisfy (
4.1.12
), we should have
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}_{2}^{\prime}}\in(\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime})^{\times}
as
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}_{2}^{\prime}}\in u^{p-1}\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}
. However, we have

(4.3.9)

P_{m+1,m+1}(c,x)=\frac{\tilde{P}_{m+1,m+1}(c,x)}{d_{m+1}(x)}\equiv\\
-xP_{r-m-1,0}(c,0)+\frac{1}{x^{t}}\sum_{s=0}^{t}x^{s}P_{m+1,m+1,s}(c)\pmod{x\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}

by Lemma
3.2.3
and Lemma
3.3.2
(i), and so one can readily see that from (
4.1.15
)

\displaystyle[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{r-m-1}\Omega}\big([P_{r-m-1,0}(c,0)cf(c)]_{c}^{(r-1)}-P_{r-m-1,0}(c,0)\delta_{m+1}(\gamma-1,x)\big)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{r-m-1}\Omega}P_{r-m-1,0}(-1,0)\big(\delta_{r-m-1}(-1)-\delta_{m+1}(-1,x)\big).

As we have

\displaystyle P_{r-m-1,0}(-1,0)\big(\delta_{r-m-1}(-1)-\delta_{m+1}(-1,x)\big)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\frac{(-1)^{r}}{P_{m,m}(-1,0)}&\mbox{if }r=2m;\\
P_{m-1,0}(-1,0)\big(\delta_{m-1}(-1)-\delta_{m}(-1)\big)&\mbox{if }r=2m+1\end{cases}

by Lemma
3.3.4
(iii), if
v_{p}(\Lambda\Theta x^{-1})=m
and
v_{p}(\Theta\Omega x^{-2})=-r+2m+1
then
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda x}{\Omega})=v_{p}(\Lambda\Theta x^{-1})-v_{p}(\Theta\Omega x^{-2})=r-m-1
and so
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
by Lemma
3.3.2
and Lemma
3.3.4
(i). Moreover, we have

\displaystyle[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}}=\frac{\Lambda}{p^{r-m-2}}P_{m+1,m+1}(c,x)=-\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{r-m-2}}P_{r-m-1,0}(-1,0)

and so by Lemma
3.3.2
we have
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}}\in\mathbf{F}
as
v_{p}(\Lambda x)=v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda x}{\Omega})+v_{p}(\Omega)\geq(r-m-1)+(-1)=r-m-2
.

Assume now that
k^{\prime}=m+1+\frac{1}{2}
, and let
\Delta(T)=\delta_{r-m-1}(T)
.
Then by (
4.3.9
) and Lemma
3.3.2
, if
v_{p}(\Lambda x)=r-m-2
and
v_{p}(x)<0
then

\displaystyle[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}}=\frac{\Lambda}{p^{r-m-2}}\frac{\tilde{P}_{m+1,m+1}(c,x)}{d_{m+1}(x)}=-\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{r-m-2}}P_{r-m-1,0}(-1,0)\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}

Moreover, one can readily see that from (
4.1.15
)

\displaystyle[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{r-m-1}\Omega}\big([P_{r-m-1,r-m-1}(c,0)cf(c)]_{c}^{(r-1)}-P_{r-m-1,r-m-1}(c,0)\delta_{r-m-1}(\gamma-1)\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\frac{\Lambda}{p^{r-m-1}\Omega}\sum_{s=0}^{t}\frac{x^{s}}{x^{t}}\big([P_{m+1,m+1,s}(c)cf(c)]_{c}^{(r-1)}-P_{m+1,m+1,s}(c)\delta_{r-k+1}(\gamma-1)\big)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{r-m-1}\Omega}P_{r-m-1,r-m-1}(c,0)\big(\delta_{r-m-1}(c)-\delta_{r-m-1}(\gamma-1)\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\frac{\Lambda}{p^{r-m-1}\Omega}\big([P_{m+1,m+1,t}(c)cf(c)]_{c}^{(r-1)}-P_{m+1,m+1,t}(c)\delta_{r-m-1}(\gamma-1)\big),

where the first equality is due to (
4.3.9
), the second equality is due to Lemma
3.2.3
, and the third equality is due to

v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda x^{-s}}{\Omega})=v_{p}(\Lambda x)+\frac{1}{2}v_{p}(\Theta\Omega^{-1}x^{-2})-\frac{1}{2}\big(v_{p}(\Theta)+v_{p}(\Omega)\big)+v_{p}(x^{-s})\geq(r-m-2)+\frac{1}{2}(t+3)+v_{p}(x^{-s})=\frac{r-1}{2}+v_{p}(x^{-s})>r-m-1
if
s\geq 1
. Furthermore, we see that
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda}{\Omega})>r-m-1
unless
r=2m+1
. Hence, we have

\displaystyle[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{r-m-1}\Omega}P_{r-m-1,0}(-1,0)\big(\delta_{r-m-1}(c)-\delta_{r-m-1}(\gamma-1)\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 30.00005pt-\frac{\Lambda}{p^{r-m-1}\Omega}P_{r-m-1,0}(-1,0)b_{r,m+2}(-1)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{r-m-2}\Omega}P_{r-m-1,0}(-1,0)\dot{\delta}_{r-m-1}(-1)u^{p-1}

\displaystyle\hskip 30.00005pt-\frac{\Lambda}{p^{r-m-1}\Omega}P_{r-m-1,0}(-1,0)b_{r,m+2}(-1)

where the last equality is due to
c-(\gamma-1)\equiv pu^{p-1}\pmod{p^{2}}
.
As we already know that
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda}{\Omega})\geq r-m-1
if
r=2m+1
and as
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{r-m-2}\Omega})=v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{r-m-2}})-v_{p}(\Omega)\geq 0
, we see that
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}_{2}^{\prime}}\in\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}
.

To satisfy (
4.1.12
), we should have
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}_{2}^{\prime}}\in(\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime})^{\times}
as
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}_{2}^{\prime}}\in u^{p-1}\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}
. However, by (
4.3.8
) one can readily see that from (
4.1.16
)

\displaystyle[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{m+1}\Omega}\big([P_{r-m-1,r-m-1}(c,0)cf(c)]_{c}^{(r-1)}-P_{r-m-1,r-m-1}(c,0)\delta_{r-m-1}(\gamma-1)\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{m+1}\Omega}P_{r-m-1,r-m-1}(c,0)\big(\delta_{r-m}(c)-\delta_{r-m-1}(\gamma-1)\big)

and so by Lemma
3.3.2
and Lemma
3.3.4
if
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{\Omega})=v_{p}(\Lambda x)+v_{p}(\Theta\Omega^{-1}x^{-2})=(r-m-2)+(-r+2m+3)=m+1
then
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
. Moreover, by (
4.3.8
) we have

[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}}=\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{m}}P_{r-m-1,r-m-1}(c,0),

and so
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}}\in\mathbf{F}
by Lemma
3.3.2
as
v_{p}(\Lambda\Theta x^{-1})=v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{\Omega})+v_{p}(\Omega)\geq(m+1)+(-1)=m
. This completes the proof.
∎

4.3.5.
In
\mathbf{Case}~(k)
with
m+2\leq k<r

In this section of paragraph, we describe the conditions for
(\Lambda,\Theta,\Omega,x)
to be good for
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(k^{\prime})
when
k^{\prime}\in\{k,k+\frac{1}{2}\}
and
k\in\mathbf{Z}
with
m+2\leq k<r
. We also determine the corresponding
\Delta
and describe the image of
\phi^{\prime}_{r}
in
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
in this case.

Recall that for
0\leq k\leq r
we have

b_{r,k}(T):=\frac{[P_{m+2,0,1}(T)Tf_{r}(T)]_{T}^{(r-1)}-P_{m+2,0,1}(T)\delta_{m}(T)}{P_{m-1,m-1}(T,0)}

if
(r,k)=(2m+1,m+2)
, and
b_{r,k}(T)=0
otherwise. Note that an identity for
b_{2m+1,m+2}
has been illustrated in Lemma
3.3.11
.

Proposition 4.3.10
.

Let
k\in\mathbf{Z}
with
m+2\leq k<r
, let
k^{\prime}\in\{k,k+\frac{1}{2}\}
, and assume that
v_{p}(\Theta)\leq 0
. If the quadruple
(\Lambda,\Theta,\Omega,x)
satisfies

(i)

\begin{cases}v_{p}(\Lambda\Theta x^{-1})=k-1\mbox{ and }v_{p}(\Theta\Omega x^{-2})=-r+2k-1&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}=k;\\
v_{p}(\Lambda x)=r-k-1\mbox{ and }v_{p}(\Theta\Omega^{-1}x^{-2})=-r+2k+1&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}=k+\frac{1}{2},\end{cases}

(ii)

v_{p}(\Theta)\geq v_{p}(x)
and
-1\leq v_{p}(\Omega)\leq 0
,

then the quadruple is good for
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(k^{\prime})
with respect to
\Delta(T)=\delta_{r+1-\lceil k^{\prime}\rceil}(T)
, in which case

\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{m+2}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1},\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})
where

\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1}:=\pi^{\prime}(F^{\prime}_{k,k})\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2}=\pi^{\prime}(F^{\prime}_{k,0}),

and the map
\phi^{\prime}_{r}:\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{m+2}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
is described as follows:

\circ

if
k^{\prime}=k
then

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{r-k-1}}P_{r-k,0}(-1,0)\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}-\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{r-k}\Omega}P_{r-k,0}(-1,0)\big(\delta_{r-k}(-1)-\delta_{r-k+1}(-1)\big)\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2};

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{k-1}}P_{r-k,r-k}(-1,0)\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}

\displaystyle\hskip 30.00005pt+\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{k-1}\Omega}P_{r-k,r-k}(-1,0)\Big(\dot{\delta}_{r-k+1}(-1)u^{p-1}+\frac{b_{r,k}(-1)}{px}\Big)\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2},

\circ

if
k^{\prime}=k+\frac{1}{2}
then

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{r-k-1}}P_{r-k,0}(-1,0)\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}-\frac{\Omega x}{p^{r-k-1}}P_{r-k,0}(-1,0)\dot{\delta}_{r-k}(-1)u^{p-1}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2};

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{k-1}}P_{r-k,r-k}(-1,0)\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}

\displaystyle\hskip 30.00005pt+\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{k}\Omega}P_{r-k,r-k}(-1,0)\big(\delta_{r-k+1}(-1)-\delta_{r-k}(-1)\big)\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}.

Note that from Proposition
4.3.10
one can readily observe that

\circ

[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}_{1}^{\prime}}\cdot[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}_{2}^{\prime}}=0
if
k^{\prime}=k
;

\circ

the second inequality in (ii) together with
v_{p}(\Theta)\leq 0
implies
v_{p}(x)<0
.

Proof.

We start the proof by noting that the inequalities in (ii) imply the ones in (
4.2.14
) for
m+2\leq k\leq r-1
under the equations in (i), and that the equations in (i) and the inequalities in (ii) imply the inequalities in (
4.1.8
).

We let
t=2k-r
for brevity. Assume first that
k^{\prime}=k
, and let
\Delta(T)=\delta_{r+1-k}(T)
. Then we have

(4.3.11)

P_{k,0}(T,x)=\frac{\tilde{P}_{k,0}(T,x)}{d_{k}(x)}\equiv\frac{1}{x^{t}}\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}x^{s}P_{k,0,s}(T)\pmod{\mathfrak{m}}

by Lemma
3.2.3
together with
v_{p}(x)<0
, and so by Lemma
3.3.2
if
v_{p}(\Lambda\Theta x^{-1})=k-1
and
v_{p}(x)<0
then

\displaystyle[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}}=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{k-1}}\frac{\tilde{P}_{k,0}(c,x)}{d_{k}(x)}=\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{k-1}}P_{r-k,r-k}(-1,0)\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}.

Moreover, one can readily see that from (
4.1.16
)

\displaystyle[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{k}\Omega}\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\frac{x^{s}}{x^{t}}\big([P_{k,0,s}(c)cf(c)]_{c}^{(r-1)}-P_{k,0,s}(c)\delta_{r-k+1}(\gamma-1)\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{k}\Omega}\big([P_{r-k,r-k}(c,0)cf(c)]_{c}^{(r-1)}-P_{r-k,r-k}(c,0)\delta_{r-k+1}(\gamma-1)\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{k}\Omega}\sum_{s=0}^{t-2}\frac{x^{s}}{x^{t}}\big([P_{k,0,s}(c)cf(c)]_{c}^{(r-1)}-P_{k,0,s}(c)\delta_{r-k+1}(\gamma-1)\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{k}\Omega}P_{r-k,r-k}(c,0)\big(\delta_{r-k+1}(c)-\delta_{r-k+1}(\gamma-1)\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-2}}{p^{k}\Omega}\big([P_{k,0,t-2}(c)cf(c)]_{c}^{(r-1)}-P_{k,0,t-2}(c)\delta_{r-k+1}(\gamma-1)\big),

where the first equality is due to (
4.3.11
), the second equality is due to Lemma
3.2.3
, and the third equality is due to
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-s}}{\Omega})=v_{p}(\Lambda\Theta x^{-1})+\frac{1}{2}v_{p}(\Theta\Omega x^{-2})-\frac{1}{2}\big(v_{p}(\Theta)+3v_{p}(\Omega)\big)+v_{p}(x^{-(s-2)})\geq(k-1)-\frac{1}{2}(t-1)+v_{p}(x^{-(s-2)})=k+\frac{1}{2}(t-3)+v_{p}(x^{-(s-2)})>k
if
s\geq 3
. Furthermore, we see that
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-2}}{\Omega})>k
unless
(r,k)=(2m+1,m+2)
. Hence, we have

\displaystyle[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{k}\Omega}P_{r-k,r-k}(c,0)\big(\delta_{r-k+1}(c)-\delta_{r-k+1}(\gamma-1)\big)+\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-2}}{p^{k}\Omega}P_{r-k,r-k}(c,0)b_{r,k}(-1)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{k-1}\Omega}P_{r-k,r-k}(-1,0)\dot{\delta}_{r-k+1}(-1)u^{p-1}+\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-2}}{p^{k}\Omega}P_{r-k,r-k}(-1,0)b_{r,k}(-1)

where the second equality is due to
c-(\gamma-1)\equiv pu^{p-1}\pmod{p^{2}}
. As we already know that
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-2}}{\Omega})\geq k
if
(r,k)=(2m+1,m+2)
and as
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{k-1}\Omega})=v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{k-1}})-v_{p}(\Omega)\geq 0
, we see that
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}\in\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}
.

We now consider
\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})
. By Lemma
3.2.3
and Lemma
3.3.2
(i) we have

(4.3.12)

P_{k,k}(c,x)=\frac{\tilde{P}_{k,k}(c,x)}{d_{k}(x)}\equiv-xP_{r-k,0}(c,0)\pmod{x\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}.

Hence, from (
4.1.15
) we have

\displaystyle[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}}=\frac{\Lambda}{p^{r-k-1}}P_{k,k}(c,x)=-\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{r-k-1}}P_{r-k,0}(-1,0)

and so by Lemma
3.3.2
we have
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}}\in\mathbf{F}
as
v_{p}(\Lambda x)=v_{p}(\Lambda\Theta x^{-1})-v_{p}(\Theta\Omega x^{-2})+v_{p}(\Omega)\geq(k-1)-(-r+2k-1)+(-1)=r-k-1
.

It is easy to see that
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}}\cdot[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}\in\mathbf{F}
by observing the valuation of the coefficients, so that we should have
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}_{2}^{\prime}}\in(\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime})^{\times}
to satisfy (
4.1.12
).
From (
4.1.15
) together with (
4.3.12
), we have

\displaystyle[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{r-k}\Omega}\big([P_{r-k,0}(c,0)cf(c)]_{c}^{(r-1)}-P_{r-k,0}(c,0)\delta_{r+1-k}(\gamma-1)\big)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{r-k}\Omega}P_{r-k,0}(-1,0)\big(\delta_{r-k}(-1)-\delta_{r+1-k}(-1)\big),

and so if
v_{p}(\Lambda\Theta x^{-1})=k-1
and
v_{p}(\Theta\Omega x^{-2})=-r+2k-1
then
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda x}{\Omega})=(k-1)-(-r+2k-1)=r-k
and so
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
, by Lemma
3.3.4
(i) and Lemma
3.3.2
(i).

Assume now that
k^{\prime}=k+\frac{1}{2}
, and let
\Delta(T)=\delta_{r-k}(T)
. Then by (
4.3.12
) together with Lemma
3.3.2
we have

[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}}=-\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{r-k-1}}P_{r-k,0}(-1,0)\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}

as
v_{p}(\Lambda x)=r-k-1
. Moreover, by (
4.3.12
) we have

\displaystyle[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{r-k}\Omega}\big([P_{r-k,0}(c,0)cf(c)]_{c}^{(r-1)}-P_{r-k,0}(c,0)\delta_{r-k}(\gamma-1)\big)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{r-k}\Omega}P_{r-k,0}(-1,0)\big(\delta_{r-k}(c)-\delta_{r-k}(\gamma-1)\big)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Lambda x}{p^{r-k-1}\Omega}P_{r-k,0}(-1,0)\dot{\delta}_{r-k}(-1)u^{p-1}

where the last equality is due to
c-(\gamma-1)\equiv pu^{p-1}\pmod{p^{2}}
. Hence, we have
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}\in\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}
as
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda x}{\Omega})=v_{p}(\Lambda x)-v_{p}(\Omega)\geq r-k-1
.

To satisfy (
4.1.12
), we should have
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}_{2}^{\prime}}\in(\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime})^{\times}
as
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}_{2}^{\prime}}\in u^{p-1}\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}
. However, one can readily see that from (
4.1.16
)

\displaystyle[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{k}\Omega}\big([P_{r-k,r-k}(c,0)cf(c)]_{c}^{(r-1)}-P_{r-k,r-k}(c,0)\delta_{r-k}(\gamma-1)\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{k}\Omega}P_{r-k,r-k}(-1,0)\big(\delta_{r-k+1}(-1)-\delta_{r-k}(-1)\big)

where the first equality is due to (
4.3.11
) and
v_{p}(x)<0
together with Lemma
3.2.3
and the second equality is due to Lemma
3.1.14
. Hence, if
v_{p}(\Lambda x)=r-k-1
and
v_{p}(\Theta\Omega^{-1}x^{-2})=-r+2k+1
then we have
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{\Omega})=v_{p}(\Lambda x)+v_{p}(\Theta\Omega^{-1}x^{-2})=k
and so
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
. Moreover, by (
4.3.11
) we have

\displaystyle[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}}=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{k-1}}\frac{\tilde{P}_{k,0}(c,x)}{d_{k}(x)}=\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{k-1}}P_{r-k,r-k}(-1,0)\in\mathbf{F}

as
v_{p}(\Lambda\Theta x^{-1})=v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{\Omega})+v_{p}(\Omega)\geq k-1
. This completes the proof.
∎

4.3.6.
In
\mathbf{Case}~(r)

In this section of paragraph, we describe the conditions for
(\Lambda,\Theta,\Omega,x)
to be good for
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(k^{\prime})
when
k^{\prime}=r
. We also determine the corresponding
\Delta
and describe the image of
\phi^{\prime}_{r}
in
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
in this case.

Recall that
b_{r,k}
has been defined in (
4.3.6
).

Proposition 4.3.13
.

Let
k=r\geq 2
,
k^{\prime}\in\{r,r+\frac{1}{2}\}
, and assume that
v_{p}(\Theta)\leq 0
.

If the quadruple
(\Lambda,\Theta,\Omega,x)
satisfies

(i)

\begin{cases}v_{p}(\Lambda\Theta x^{-1})=r-1\mbox{ and }v_{p}(\Theta\Omega x^{-2})=r-1&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}=r;\\
v_{p}(\Lambda)=-1\mbox{ and }v_{p}(\Theta\Omega^{-1})=r+1&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}=r+\frac{1}{2},\end{cases}

(ii)

\begin{cases}v_{p}(x)\leq v_{p}(\Theta)\leq v_{p}(x)+r\mbox{ and }v_{p}(\Omega)\leq 0&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}=r;\\
v_{p}(\Theta)\geq v_{p}(x)+r&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}=r+\frac{1}{2},\end{cases}

then the quadruple is good for
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(k^{\prime})
with respect to

\Delta(T)=\begin{cases}\delta_{1}(T)&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}=r\geq 3;\\
\delta_{2}(T,x)&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}=r=2;\\
\delta_{0}(T,x)&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}=r+\frac{1}{2},\end{cases}

in which case
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{r}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1},\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})
where

\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1}:=\pi^{\prime}(v^{r}E^{\prime}_{1})\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2}:=\pi^{\prime}(F^{\prime}_{r,0}),

and the map
\phi^{\prime}_{r}:\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{r}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
is described as follows:

\circ

if
k^{\prime}=r
then

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{r}p\Lambda\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}+(-1)^{r+1}\frac{\Lambda x}{\Omega}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2};

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{r-1}x}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}+\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{r-1}\Omega x}\Big(\dot{\delta}(-1)u^{p-1}-\frac{b_{r,r}(-1)}{px}\Big)\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2},

\circ

if
k^{\prime}=r+\frac{1}{2}
then

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{r}p\Lambda\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1};

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{r-1}x}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}+\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{r}\Omega}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}.

Note that from Proposition
4.3.13
one can readily observe the following:

\circ

[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}_{1}^{\prime}}\cdot[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}_{2}^{\prime}}=0
if
k^{\prime}=r
;

\circ

the second inequality in (ii) together with
v_{p}(\Theta)\leq 0
implies
v_{p}(x)<0
;

\circ

b_{r,r}\neq 0
if and only if
r=3
, by definition.

Proof.

We start the proof by noting that the inequalities in (ii) implies the ones in (
4.2.22
) (including
v_{p}(x)<0
) under the equations in (i), and that the equations in (i) and the inequalities in (ii) imply the inequalities in (
4.1.8
), including
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})\leq 0
if
k^{\prime}=r
.

Assume first that
k^{\prime}=r>1
, and let
\Delta(T)=\delta_{1}(T)
if
r\geq 3
and
\Delta(T)=\delta_{2}(T,x)
if
r=2
. By Lemma
3.1.12
, we have
P_{r,0}(c,x)\equiv x^{-1}\pmod{x^{-1}\mathfrak{m}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}}
as
v_{p}(x)<0
, and so if
v_{p}(\Lambda\Theta x^{-1})=r-1
then

[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}}=\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{r-1}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
.

To compute
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}
we separate it into two cases:
r=2
and
r\geq 3
. If
r=2
then from (
4.1.16
) we have

\displaystyle[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{r}\Omega}P_{2,0}(c,x)\big(\delta_{2}(c,x)-\delta_{2}(\gamma-1,x)\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{r}\Omega}\big(\delta_{2}(c,x)-\delta_{2}(\gamma-1,x)\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{r-1}\Omega}\dot{\delta}_{2}(-1,x)u^{p-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{r-1}\Omega}\dot{\delta}_{1}(-1)u^{p-1},

where the second equality is due to Lemma
3.1.12
and
v_{p}(x)<0
, the third equality is due to
c-(\gamma-1)\equiv pu^{p-1}\pmod{p^{2}}
, and the last equality is due to Lemma
3.3.7
(iv). Hence,
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}\in\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}
as
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{r-1}\Omega})=v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{r-1}})-v_{p}(\Omega)\geq 0
.
If
r\geq 3
then from (
4.1.16
) together with
v_{p}(x)<0
and
P_{r,0,s}(T)=[(-Tf_{r}(T))^{r-1-s}]_{T}^{(r-1)}
we have

\displaystyle[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{r}\Omega}x^{-r}\sum_{s=0}^{r-1}x^{s}\big([P_{r,0,s}(c)cf(c)]_{c}^{(r-1)}-P_{r,0,s}(c)\delta_{1}(\gamma-1)\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{r}\Omega}x^{-r}\sum_{s=0}^{r-1}x^{s}\big(-P_{r,0,s-1}(c)-P_{r,0,s}(c)\delta_{1}(\gamma-1)\big),

where the first equality is due to Lemma
3.1.12
and the second equality is due to the identity
[P_{r,0,s}(c)cf(c)]_{c}^{(r-1)}=-P_{r,0,s-1}(c)
. As
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-s}}{\Omega})=v_{p}(\Lambda\Theta x^{-1})+\frac{1}{2}v_{p}(\Theta\Omega x^{-2})-\frac{1}{2}\big(v_{p}(\Theta)+3v_{p}(\Omega)\big)+v_{p}(x^{-(s-2)})\geq\frac{3}{2}(r-1)+v_{p}(x^{-(s-2)})=r+\frac{1}{2}(r-3)+v_{p}(x^{-(s-2)})>r
if
s\geq 3
, we further have

\displaystyle[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{r}\Omega}\left[x^{-1}\big(\delta_{1}(c)-\delta_{1}(\gamma-1)\big)+x^{-2}(-P_{r,0,r-3}(c)+\delta_{1}(c)\delta_{1}(\gamma-1))\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{r}\Omega}\big[px^{-1}\dot{\delta}_{1}(-1)u^{p-1}-x^{-2}\big(P_{r,0,r-3}(-1)-\delta_{1}(-1)^{2}\big)\big],

where the first equality is due to
P_{r,0,r-2}(c)=-\delta_{1}(c)
and
P_{r,0,r-1}(c)=1
and the second equality is due to
c-(\gamma-1)\equiv pu^{p-1}\pmod{p^{2}}
.
Hence,
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}\in\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}
as

v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{r-1}\Omega})=v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{r-1}})-v_{p}(\Omega)\geq 0
and

v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-2}}{\Omega})\geq r+\frac{1}{2}(r-3)\geq r
. Moreover,

\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{r}\Omega}x^{-2}\big(P_{r,0,r-3}(-1)-\delta_{1}(-1)^{2}\big)=0

if
r>3
, as
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-2}}{\Omega})>r
if
r>3
, and it is easy to see that
b_{r,r}(-1)=P_{r,0,r-3}(-1)-\delta_{1}(-1)^{2}
if
r=3
.

We now compute
\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})
. From (
4.1.4
), we have

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})=c^{r}\left(p\Lambda E^{\prime}_{1}-\frac{\Lambda}{\Omega}\big(x+\delta(\gamma-1)\big)E^{\prime}_{2}\right)=(-1)^{r}p\Lambda E^{\prime}_{1}-(-1)^{r}\frac{\Lambda x}{\Omega}E^{\prime}_{2},

as
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda x}{\Omega})=v_{p}(\Lambda\Theta x^{-1})-v_{p}(\Omega\Theta x^{-2})=0
and
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda}{\Omega})=-v_{p}(x)>0
. It is easy to see that
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}}\cdot[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}=0
by observing the valuation of the coefficient.
Hence, to satisfy (
4.1.12
), we should have
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}_{2}^{\prime}}\in(\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime})^{\times}
, and it holds as
v_{p}(\Lambda\Omega^{-1}x)=0
. Moreover, we have
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}_{1}^{\prime}}\in\mathbf{F}
as
v_{p}(\Lambda)=v_{p}(\Lambda\Theta x^{-1})-v_{p}(\Theta x^{-1})\geq(r-1)-r=-1
.

Assume now that
k^{\prime}=r+\frac{1}{2}
, and let
\Delta(T)=\delta_{0}(T,x)
. Then by (
4.1.4
) we have

[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}}=(-1)^{r}p\Lambda\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}

as
v_{p}(\Lambda)=-1
. Moreover, by (
4.1.4
) we have

\displaystyle[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}

\displaystyle=-(-1)^{r}\frac{\Lambda}{\Omega}\big(x+\delta_{0}(\gamma-1,x)\big)

\displaystyle=-(-1)^{r}\frac{\Lambda}{\Omega}\big(x+(-x)\big)

\displaystyle=0

Hence, we have
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}\in\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}
.

To satisfy (
4.1.12
), we should have
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}_{2}^{\prime}}\in(\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime})^{\times}
as
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}_{2}^{\prime}}\in\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}
. However, one can readily see that from (
4.1.16
)

\displaystyle[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{r}\Omega}\big([P_{0,0}(c,0)cf(c)]_{c}^{(r-1)}-P_{0,0}(c,0)\delta_{0}(\gamma-1,x)\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{r}\Omega}(-x)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{r}\Omega}

where the first equality is due to Lemma
3.1.12
and
v_{p}(x)<0
, and the second equality is due to
P_{0,0}(c,0)=1
and
v_{p}(x)<0
. Hence, if
v_{p}(\Lambda)=-1
and
v_{p}(\Theta\Omega^{-1})=r+1
then we have
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{\Omega})=v_{p}(\Lambda)+v_{p}(\Theta\Omega^{-1})=r
and so
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
. Moreover, we have

[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}}=\frac{\Lambda\Theta x^{-1}}{p^{r-1}}\in\mathbf{F}

as
v_{p}(\Lambda\Theta x^{-1})=-1+v_{p}(\Theta x^{-1})\geq r-1
. This completes the proof.
∎

4.4.
The exceptional cases

In this subsection, we prove Theorem
4.1.10
and Theorem
4.1.24
for two exceptional cases:
r^{\prime}=1
and
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\infty)
.

4.4.1.
When
r^{\prime}=1

In this section of paragraph, we describe the filtration and the conditions for the strong divisibility when
r^{\prime}=1
. Recall that we set
\delta_{1}(T,x)=0
and
\delta_{0}(T,x)=-x
if
r^{\prime}=1
. Without loss of generality, we may assume
r=r^{\prime}
, and we do so.

Set
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}_{k}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}:=\mathrm{Fil}^{1;1}_{k}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
for brevity. It is clear from (
4.1.3
) that

(4.4.1)

\begin{cases}F^{\prime}_{0,0}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}\,\,\mbox{ if and only if }\,\,v_{p}(\Theta)\leq v_{p}(x);\\
F^{\prime}_{1,0}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}\,\,\mbox{ if and only if }\,\,v_{p}(\Theta)\geq v_{p}(x),\end{cases}

in which case we have

(4.4.2)

\mathrm{Fil}^{1}_{k}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}=\begin{cases}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}(F^{\prime}_{0,0},vE^{\prime}_{2})+\mathrm{Fil}^{p}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}\cdot\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}&\mbox{ if }k=0;\\
S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}(vE^{\prime}_{1},F^{\prime}_{1,0})+\mathrm{Fil}^{p}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}\cdot\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}&\mbox{ if }k=1.\end{cases}

We first treat the case
\mathbf{Case}~(0)
.

Proposition 4.4.3
.

Let
k^{\prime}=\frac{1}{2}
. If the quadruple
(\Lambda,\Theta,\Omega,x)
satisfies

(i)

v_{p}(\Lambda)=0
and
v_{p}(\Theta\Omega^{-1})=0
;

(ii)

v_{p}(\Theta)\leq v_{p}(x)
and
v_{p}(\Omega)\leq 0
,

then the quadruple is good for
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\frac{1}{2})
with respect to
\Delta(T)=\delta_{1}(T)
, in which case
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}_{0}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1},\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})
with

\displaystyle\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1}:=\pi^{\prime}(F^{\prime}_{0,0})\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2}:=\pi^{\prime}(vE^{\prime}_{2})

and the map
\phi^{\prime}_{1}:\mathrm{Fil}^{1}_{0}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
is described as

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}_{1}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})=\Lambda\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\phi^{\prime}_{1}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})=-\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{\Omega}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}.

Proof.

We start the proof by noting that the equations in (i) and the inequalities in (ii) imply the inequalities in (
4.1.8
), and that the first inequality in (ii) implies the one in (
4.4.1
).

By (
4.1.4
), it is tedious to see that

\phi^{\prime}_{1}(F^{\prime}_{0,0})\equiv\frac{1}{p}\bigg(\bigg(p\Lambda E^{\prime}_{1}-\frac{\Lambda x}{\Omega}E^{\prime}_{2}\bigg)+\frac{x}{\Theta}\bigg(\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{\Omega}E^{\prime}_{2}\bigg)\bigg)=\Lambda E^{\prime}_{1}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}},

as
\delta=0
and (
4.1.9
). Hence, if
v_{p}(\Lambda)=0
then
[\phi^{\prime}_{1}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
.

As
[\phi^{\prime}_{1}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}=0
, we should have
[\phi^{\prime}_{1}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}\in(\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}})^{\times}
. But by (
4.1.4
)
\phi^{\prime}_{1}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{\Omega}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}
and so if
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{\Omega})=v_{p}(\Lambda)+v_{p}(\Theta\Omega^{-1})=0
then we have
[\phi^{\prime}_{1}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
, which completes the proof.
∎

We now treat the case
\mathbf{Case}~(1)
.

Proposition 4.4.4
.

Let
k^{\prime}\in\{1,\frac{3}{2}\}
, and assume that
v_{p}(\Theta)\leq 0
. If the quadruple
(\Lambda,\Theta,\Omega,x)
satisfies

(i)

\begin{cases}v_{p}(\Lambda\Theta x^{-1})=0\mbox{ and }v_{p}(\Theta\Omega x^{-2})=0&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}=1;\\
v_{p}(\Lambda)=-1\mbox{ and }v_{p}(\Theta\Omega^{-1})=2&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}=\frac{3}{2},\end{cases}

(ii)

\begin{cases}v_{p}(x)\leq v_{p}(\Theta)\leq v_{p}(x)+1&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}=1;\\
v_{p}(\Theta)\geq v_{p}(x)+1&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}=\frac{3}{2},\end{cases}

then the quadruple is good for
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(k^{\prime})
with respect to

\Delta(T)=\begin{cases}\delta_{1}(T)&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}=1;\\
\delta_{0}(T,x)&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}=\frac{3}{2},\end{cases}

in which case
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}_{1}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1},\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})
with

\displaystyle\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1}:=\pi^{\prime}(vE^{\prime}_{1})\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2}:=\pi^{\prime}(F^{\prime}_{1,0})

and the map
\phi^{\prime}_{1}:\mathrm{Fil}^{1}_{1}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
is described as follows:

\circ

if
k^{\prime}=1
then

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}_{1}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})=-p\Lambda\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}+\frac{\Lambda x}{\Omega}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\phi^{\prime}_{1}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{x}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1},

\circ

if
k^{\prime}=\frac{3}{2}
then

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}_{1}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})=-p\Lambda\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\phi^{\prime}_{1}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{x}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}+\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p\Omega}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}.

Proof.

Assume first that
k^{\prime}=1
. We start the proof by noting that the equations in (i) and the inequalities in (ii) imply the inequalities in (
4.1.8
), and that the first inequality in (ii) implies the one in (
4.4.1
).

By (
4.1.4
) it is tedious that

\phi^{\prime}_{1}(F^{\prime}_{1,0})\equiv\frac{1}{p}\bigg(\frac{\Theta}{x}\bigg(p\Lambda E^{\prime}_{1}-\frac{\Lambda x}{\Omega}E^{\prime}_{2}\bigg)-\bigg(\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{\Omega}E^{\prime}_{2}\bigg)\bigg)\equiv\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{x}E^{\prime}_{1}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}},

as
\Delta=0
and (
4.1.9
). Hence, if
v_{p}(\Lambda\Theta x^{-1})=0
then
[\phi^{\prime}_{1}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
.

As
[\phi^{\prime}_{1}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}=0
, we should have
[\phi^{\prime}_{1}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}\in(\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}})^{\times}
. But by (
4.1.4
)

\phi^{\prime}_{1}(vE^{\prime}_{1})\equiv c\bigg(p\Lambda E^{\prime}_{1}-\frac{\Lambda x}{\Omega}E^{\prime}_{2}\bigg)\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}},

as
\Delta=0
and (
4.1.9
), and so if
v_{p}(\Lambda\Theta x^{-1})=v_{p}(\Theta\Omega x^{-2})=0
then we have

v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda x}{\Omega})=v_{p}(\Lambda\Theta x^{-1})-v_{p}(\Theta\Omega x^{-2})=0
and so
[\phi^{\prime}_{1}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
. Moreover, we also have
[\phi^{\prime}_{1}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}}\in\mathbf{F}
as
v_{p}(\Lambda)\geq-1
.

Now assume
k^{\prime}=\frac{3}{2}
. We note that the equations in (i), the inequalities in (ii) and the fact that
\Delta=\delta_{0}=-x
imply the inequalities in (
4.1.8
), and that the inequality in (ii) implies the one in (
4.4.1
).

By (
4.1.4
)

\phi^{\prime}_{1}(vE^{\prime}_{1})\equiv cp\Lambda E^{\prime}_{1}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}},

as
\Delta=-x
and (
4.1.9
). Hence, if
v_{p}(\Lambda)=-1
then
[\phi^{\prime}_{1}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
.

As
[\phi^{\prime}_{1}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}=0
, we should have
[\phi^{\prime}_{1}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}\in(\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}})^{\times}
. But by (
4.1.4
) it is tedious that

\phi^{\prime}_{1}(F^{\prime}_{1,0})\equiv\frac{1}{p}\bigg(\frac{\Theta}{x}(p\Lambda E^{\prime}_{1})-\bigg(\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{\Omega}E^{\prime}_{2}\bigg)\bigg)\equiv\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{x}E^{\prime}_{1}-\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p\Omega}E^{\prime}_{2}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}},

as
\Delta=-x
and (
4.1.9
), and so if
v_{p}(\Lambda)=-1
and
v_{p}(\Theta\Omega^{-1})=2
then we have
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{\Omega})=v_{p}(\Lambda)+v_{p}(\Theta\Omega^{-1})=1
and so
[\phi^{\prime}_{1}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
. Moreover, we also have
[\phi^{\prime}_{1}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}}\in\mathbf{F}
as
v_{p}(\Lambda\Theta x^{-1})=v_{p}(\Theta x^{-1})-1\geq 0
. This completes the proof.
∎

4.4.2.
In
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\infty)

In this section of paragraph, we describe the filtration and the conditions for the strong divisibility when
k^{\prime}=\infty
and
1\leq r^{\prime}\leq r<p-1
. Recall that we set
\delta_{0}(T,x)=-x
. Without loss of generality, we may assume
r=r
, and we do so.

Set
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{\infty}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}:=\mathrm{Fil}^{r;r^{\prime}}_{\infty}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
for brevity. It is clear from (
4.1.3
) that

(4.4.5)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{\infty}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}&=S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}\big(E^{\prime}_{2}\big)+\mathrm{Fil}^{r}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}\cdot\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}\\
&=S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}\big(v^{r}E^{\prime}_{1},E^{\prime}_{2}\big)+\mathrm{Fil}^{p}S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}\cdot\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}.\end{split}

Proposition 4.4.6
.

Let
k^{\prime}=\infty
. If the quadruple
(\Lambda,\Theta,\Omega,x)
satisfies

(i)

v_{p}(\Lambda)=-1
and
v_{p}(\Theta\Omega^{-1})=r+1
;

(ii)

v_{p}(\Theta)\leq 0
,

then the quadruple is good for
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\infty)
with respect to
\Delta(T)=\delta_{0}(T,x)
, in which case

\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{\infty}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1},\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})
where

\displaystyle\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1}:=\pi^{\prime}(v^{r}E^{\prime}_{1})\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2}:=\pi^{\prime}(E^{\prime}_{2})

and the map
\phi^{\prime}_{r}:\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{\infty}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
is described as

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})=(-1)^{r}p\Lambda\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})=\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{p^{r}\Omega}\overline{E}^{\prime}_{2}.

Proof.

We start the proof by noting that the equations in (i) and the inequalities in (ii) imply the inequalities in (
4.1.8
).

It is clear that
\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})
is computed as in the statement, by (
4.1.4
). Hence, if
v_{p}(\Lambda)=-1
and
v_{p}(\Theta\Omega^{-1})=r+1
then we have
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda\Theta}{\Omega})=v_{p}(\Lambda)+v_{p}(\Theta\Omega^{-1})=r
and so
[\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})]_{\overline{E}_{2}^{\prime}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
. From (
4.1.4
), it is also clear from (
4.1.9
) that we have

\phi^{\prime}(E^{\prime}_{1})\equiv p\Lambda E^{\prime}_{1}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}}

as
v_{p}(\frac{\Lambda}{\Omega})\geq r-v_{p}(\Theta)\geq r
and
\Delta=-x
.
Hence, if
v_{p}(\Lambda)=-1
then
\phi^{\prime}_{r}(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})=(-1)^{r}p\Lambda\overline{E}^{\prime}_{1}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
. This completes the proof.
∎

5.
Main results

In this section, we state and prove the main results on Galois stable lattices. We also summarize the mod-
p
reduction of the pseudo-strongly divisible modules.

5.1.
Typical examples of pseudo-strongly divisible modules

In this subsection, we show that certain choice of
S_{\mathcal{O}}
-generators in
\mathcal{D}:=\mathcal{D}(D)
, where
D
is an admissible filtered
(\phi,N)
-module of rank
2
in Example
2.1.3
, gives rise to pseudo-strongly divisible modules.

Throughout this subsection, we keep the notation of Example
2.1.3
, and, in particular, let
D=D(\lambda,\vec{\mathfrak{L}})
where
\lambda\in\mathcal{O}
and
\vec{\mathfrak{L}}:=(\mathfrak{L}_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\in[\mathbb{P}^{1}(E)]^{f}
. We also set
\mathcal{D}:=\mathcal{D}(D)
. Assume that
0<r_{j}<p-1
for all
j\in\mathcal{J}
, and fix
r
with
p-1>r\geq r_{j}
for all
j\in\mathcal{J}
.

We let
E_{1},E_{2}
be the elements in
\mathcal{D}=\oplus_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\mathcal{D}^{(j)}
of the following form

(5.1.1)

\displaystyle\begin{split}E_{1}&=\vec{\theta}_{1}\bigg(\eta_{1}+\frac{\varphi(\vec{\varrho})+\vec{\Delta}^{\varphi}(\gamma-1)}{p}\eta_{2}\bigg);\\
E_{2}&=\vec{\theta}_{2}\eta_{2}\end{split}

for some
\vec{\theta}_{1},\vec{\theta}_{2},\vec{\varrho}\in(E^{\times})^{f}
, and
\vec{\Delta}(T)\in(E(T)_{\Delta})^{f}
. We write

\vec{\varrho}=(\varrho_{0},\cdots,\varrho_{f-1})\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\vec{\Delta}(T)=(\Delta^{(0)}(T),\cdots,\Delta^{(f-1)}(T)).

Similarly, we write
\vec{\theta}_{1}=(\theta_{1,0},\theta_{1,1},\cdots,\theta_{1,f-1})
and
\vec{\theta}_{2}=(\theta_{2,0},\theta_{2,1},\cdots,\theta_{2,f-1})
.
We further require that
\vec{\varrho}
satisfies

(5.1.2)

\varrho_{j}=\mathfrak{L}_{j}\mbox{ for }j\not\in I_{D}.

Remark 5.1.3
.

If
j\in I_{D}
, i.e.,
\mathfrak{L}_{j}=\infty
, then we no condition is imposed on
\varrho_{j}
, i.e., we can choose any
\varrho_{j}\in E
. By Lemma
5.1.6
and Lemma
5.1.9
,
\varrho_{j}
only appears at
\phi^{\diamond}(E_{1})e_{j+1}
(see (
5.1.7
)), if
j\in I_{D}
. By the identities (
5.1.15
) and (
5.1.16
),
\phi^{\diamond}(E_{1})
can be written as in (
5.1.17
). But remember that our choice of
\Delta^{(j)}(T)=-x_{j}
if
j\in I_{D}
, and so the term
x_{j}+\Delta^{(j)}(\gamma-1)
of
\phi^{\diamond}(E_{1})e_{j+1}
vanishes. Hence, it is free to choose any
\varrho_{j}
in
E
.

This choice of the two elements in
\mathcal{D}
induces the following additional structures:

\circ

\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond}:=S_{\mathcal{O}}(E_{1},E_{2})
is the
S_{\mathcal{O}}
-submodule of
\mathcal{D}
generated by
E_{1},E_{2}
;

\circ

the Frobenius map
\phi^{\diamond}:\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
is the restriction of
\phi
on
\mathcal{D}
;

\circ

the monodromy operator
N^{\diamond}:\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
is the restriction of
N
on
\mathcal{D}
;

\circ

\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond}:=\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathcal{D}\cap\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond}
.

The quadruple

(5.1.4)

(\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond},\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond},\phi^{\diamond},N^{\diamond})

is denoted by
\mathfrak{M}_{(\vec{\theta}_{1},\vec{\theta}_{2},\vec{\varrho},\vec{\Delta})}
.

Remark 5.1.5
.

The main reason we require that
\varrho_{j}
satisfies (
5.1.2
) is that the quantity

\tfrac{p\mathfrak{L}_{j}-\varrho_{j-1}-\Delta^{(j-1)}(-1)}{p}\quad(\mbox{resp}.\quad\tfrac{p\varrho_{j}-\varrho_{j-1}-\Delta^{(j-1)}(-1)}{p})

naturally appears in the filtration
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond}
(resp. in the formula
\phi^{\diamond}(E_{1})
) as you will see in (
5.1.10
) (resp. in (
5.1.7
)), and we wish that these two quantities are identical since the generic fiber of the strongly divisible modules is required to be isomorphic to
\mathcal{D}(D)
. It is also reasonable to expect that
\Delta^{(j)}
in (
5.1.1
) is a rational function in
\gamma-1
, as
c:=\frac{\phi(v)}{p}
is congruent to
\gamma-1
modulo
p
and
\mathfrak{M}
is generated by the image of
\phi_{r}
. If
j\in I_{D}
, whatever
\varrho_{j}
we choose in
E
, it turns out that the resulting strongly divisible modules are homothetic.

The Frobenius map
\phi^{\diamond}
and the monodromy operator
N^{\diamond}
can be described explicitly.

Lemma 5.1.6
.

(i)

The Frobenius map
\phi^{\diamond}:\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
is induced by

(5.1.7)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\phi^{\diamond}(E_{1})=&p\frac{\lambda\varphi(\vec{\theta}_{1})}{\vec{\theta}_{1}}E_{1}-\frac{\lambda\varphi(\vec{\theta}_{1})}{p\vec{\theta}_{2}}\big(p\varphi(\vec{\varrho})-\varphi^{2}(\vec{\varrho})-\vec{\Delta}^{\varphi^{2}}(-1)\big)E_{2}\\
&\qquad-\frac{\lambda\varphi(\vec{\theta}_{1})}{\vec{\theta}_{2}}\vec{\Delta}^{\varphi}(\gamma-1)E_{2}+\frac{\lambda\varphi(\vec{\theta}_{1})}{p\vec{\theta}_{2}}\big(\vec{\Delta}^{\varphi^{2}}(\phi(\gamma)-1)-\vec{\Delta}^{\varphi^{2}}(-1)\big)E_{2};\\
\phi^{\diamond}(E_{2})=&\frac{\lambda\varphi(\vec{\theta}_{2})}{\vec{\theta}_{2}}E_{2}=\frac{\lambda\varphi(\vec{\theta}_{1})}{\vec{\theta}_{1}}\varphi\left(\frac{\vec{\theta}_{2}}{\vec{\theta}_{1}}\right)\frac{\vec{\theta}_{1}}{\vec{\theta}_{2}}E_{2}.\end{split}

(ii)

The monodromy operator
N^{\diamond}:\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
is induced by

(5.1.8)

\displaystyle\begin{split}N^{\diamond}(E_{1})&=\vec{\theta}_{1}\bigg(\eta_{2}+\frac{N(\vec{\Delta}^{\varphi}(\gamma-1))}{p}\eta_{2}\bigg);\\
N^{\diamond}(E_{2})&=0.\end{split}

Proof.

The proof easily follows from tedious computation, and so we omit it.
∎

Moreover, the filtration
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond}
can be described explicitly as well.

Lemma 5.1.9
.

The
S_{\mathcal{O}}
-module
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond}
is described as

(5.1.10)

\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond}=\bigoplus_{j\in\mathcal{J}}[\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond}e_{j}]^{(r-1)}+\mathrm{Fil}^{r}S_{\mathcal{O}}\cdot\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond}

where for each
j\in\mathcal{J}

(i)

if
\mathfrak{L}_{j}=\infty
then

[\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond}e_{j}]^{(r-1)}=\big\{v^{r-r_{j}}C(v)E_{2}e_{j}\mid C(T)\in\mathcal{O}[T]^{(r_{j}-1)}\big\}

(ii)

if
\mathfrak{L}_{j}\neq\infty
then

[\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond}e_{j}]^{(r-1)}
consists of the elements of the form

v^{r-r_{j}}[C(v)\tfrac{\theta_{2,j}}{\theta_{1,j}}E_{1}+B(v)E_{2}]e_{j}

for the polynomials
C(T),B(T)\in\mathcal{O}[T]^{(r_{j}-1)}
satisfying
C(T)\tfrac{\theta_{2,j}}{\theta_{1,j}}\in\mathcal{O}[T]
and

(5.1.11)

B(T)=\bigg[C(T)\bigg(x_{j}+\frac{T}{p}f_{r_{j}}\Big(\frac{T}{p}\Big)\bigg)\bigg]_{T}^{(r_{j}-1)}

where

(5.1.12)

x_{j}:=\frac{p\varrho_{j}-\varrho_{j-1}-\Delta^{(j-1)}(-1)}{p}.

Proof.

From Example
2.2.1
, every element of
\left(\frac{\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathcal{D}}{\mathrm{Fil}^{r}S_{E}\mathcal{D}}\right)e_{j}
can be written as

(5.1.13)

v^{r-r_{j}}C(v)\theta_{2,j}\Big(\xi_{j}\eta_{1}+\nu_{j}\eta_{2}+\xi_{j}\frac{v}{p}f_{r_{j}}\Big(\frac{v}{p}\Big)\eta_{2}\Big)e_{j}

for some polynomial
C(T)\in E(T)
of degree
\leq r_{j}-1
, and this can be rewritten as

(5.1.14)

v^{r-r_{j}}C(v)\bigg(\xi_{j}\frac{\theta_{2,j}}{\theta_{1,j}}E_{1}-\xi_{j}\frac{\varrho_{j-1}+\Delta^{(j-1)}(-1)}{p}E_{2}+\nu_{j}E_{2}+\xi_{j}\frac{v}{p}f_{r_{j}}\Big(\frac{v}{p}\Big)E_{2}\bigg)e_{j}

modulo
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}S_{E}\mathcal{D}
by using (
5.1.1
). Recall that we write
\mathfrak{L}_{j}\in\mathbb{P}^{1}(E)
for
\frac{\nu_{j}}{\xi_{j}}
, and note that it is harmless to multiply
\vec{\theta}_{2}
to the quantity in (
5.1.13
).

Now, if
j\in I_{D}
, i.e.,
\mathfrak{L}_{j}=\infty
then it is immediate from (
5.1.14
) that every element of
\left(\frac{\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathcal{D}}{\mathrm{Fil}^{r}S_{E}\mathcal{D}}\right)e_{j}
can be written as
v^{r-r_{j}}C(v)E_{2}e_{j}
. If
j\not\in I_{D}
, i.e.,
\mathfrak{L}_{j}\neq\infty
, then due to (
5.1.12
) it is also easy to see from (
5.1.14
) that every element of
\left(\frac{\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathcal{D}}{\mathrm{Fil}^{r}S_{E}\mathcal{D}}\right)e_{j}
can be written as
v^{r-r_{j}}\left[C(v)\frac{\theta_{2,j}}{\theta_{1,j}}E_{1}+B(v)E_{2}\right]e_{j}
where

B(T)
and
C(T)
satisfy the equation (
5.1.11
). This completes the proof.
∎

By identifying

(5.1.15)

\vec{\Lambda}=\frac{\lambda\vec{\theta}_{1}}{\varphi^{-1}(\vec{\theta}_{1})}\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\vec{\Theta}=\frac{\vec{\theta}_{2}}{\vec{\theta}_{1}}

as well as

(5.1.16)

\vec{x}=\frac{p\vec{\varrho}-\varphi(\vec{\varrho})-\vec{\Delta}^{\varphi}(-1)}{p}

we may consider
\mathfrak{M}_{(\vec{\theta}_{1},\vec{\theta}_{2},\vec{\varrho},\vec{\Delta})}
as parameterized by
(\vec{\Lambda},\vec{\Theta},\vec{x})\in((E^{\times})^{f})^{2}\times E^{f}
together with
\vec{\Delta}(T)\in E(T)_{\Delta}
. (Note that the identity in (
5.1.16
) is induced from (
5.1.12
).) More precisely, by Lemma
5.1.9

\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond}
is parameterized by
(\vec{\Theta},\vec{x})
, and by Lemma
5.1.6

\phi^{\diamond}
and
N^{\diamond}
can be written as

(5.1.17)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\phi^{\diamond}(E_{1})&=p\varphi(\vec{\Lambda})E_{1}-\frac{\varphi(\vec{\Lambda})}{\vec{\Theta}}\big(\varphi(\vec{x})+\vec{\Delta}^{\varphi}(\gamma-1)\big)E_{2}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{\varphi(\vec{\Lambda})}{p\vec{\Theta}}\big(\vec{\Delta}^{\varphi^{2}}(\phi(\gamma)-1)-\vec{\Delta}^{\varphi^{2}}(-1)\big)E_{2};\\
\phi^{\diamond}(E_{2})&=\frac{\varphi(\vec{\Lambda}\vec{\Theta})}{\vec{\Theta}}E_{2}\end{split}

and

(5.1.18)

\displaystyle\begin{split}N^{\diamond}(E_{1})&=\frac{1}{\vec{\Theta}}\bigg(1+\frac{N(\gamma)}{p}\dot{\vec{\Delta}}^{\varphi}(\gamma-1)\bigg)E_{2};\\
N^{\diamond}(E_{2})&=0.\end{split}

It is clear from the first identity in (
5.1.15
) that

(5.1.19)

\prod_{j=0}^{f-1}\Lambda_{j}=\lambda^{f}.

Moreover, the association
(\vec{\theta}_{1},\vec{\theta}_{2})\mapsto(\vec{\Lambda},\vec{\Theta})
defined by the identities in (
5.1.15
) gives rise to a bijection

(5.1.20)

\big((E^{\times})^{f}\big)^{2}/E^{\times}\longrightarrow\bigg\{(\vec{\Lambda},\vec{\Theta})\in\big((E^{\times})^{f}\big)^{2}\mid\prod_{j=0}^{f-1}\Lambda_{j}=\lambda^{f}\bigg\}

where the domain
\big((E^{\times})^{f}\big)^{2}/E^{\times}
is the quotient of
\big((E^{\times})^{f}\big)^{2}
by
E^{\times}
via the diagonal action.

We now show that the isotypic components of
\mathfrak{M}_{(\vec{\theta}_{1},\vec{\theta}_{2},\vec{\varrho},\vec{\Delta})}
are typical examples of pseudo-strongly divisible modules. Fix
j\in\mathcal{J}
, and consider the isotypic component of
\mathfrak{M}_{(\vec{\theta}_{1},\vec{\theta}_{2},\vec{\varrho},\vec{\Delta})}
at
j

(5.1.21)

(\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond}e_{j},\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond}e_{j},\phi^{\diamond,(j)}:\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond}e_{j}\to\mathcal{D}e_{j+1},N^{\diamond,(j)}:\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond}e_{j}\to\mathcal{D}e_{j}),

that is parameterized by
(\Lambda_{j},\Theta_{j},\Theta_{j+1},x_{j})
together with
(\Delta^{(j)},\Delta^{(j-1)})
.

Definition 5.1.22
.

If
\mathfrak{L}_{j}=\infty
and so
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond}e_{j}
can be written as (i) of Lemma
5.1.9
, then we say that
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond}e_{j}
is of
\mathbf{Case}~(\infty)
. If
\mathfrak{L}_{j}\neq\infty
and so
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond}e_{j}
can be written as (ii) of Lemma
5.1.9
, then we consider
(r_{j}+1)
-choices of the generators for
\mathbb{M}
as in (
3.1.1
) (identifying the equation in (
5.1.11
) with the equation in (
3.0.1
) as well as
(r_{j},x_{j},\Theta_{j})=(r^{\prime},x,\Theta)
), each of which we say that
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond}e_{j}
is of
\mathbf{Case}~(k)
, where
k
varies over the integers with
0\leq k\leq r_{j}
.

Consider the natural isomorphisms of the
S_{E}^{\prime}
-modules for all
j\in\mathcal{J}

\psi^{(j)}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}e_{j}:\,E_{1}^{\prime}\mapsto E_{1}e_{j},\,\,E^{\prime}_{2}\mapsto E_{2}e_{j}.

Proposition 5.1.23
.

Fix
j\in\mathcal{J}
, and let
k\in([0,r_{j}]\cap\mathbf{Z})\cup\{\infty\}
. Assume that
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond}e_{j}
is of
\mathbf{Case}~(k)
. By identifying
(r^{\prime},\Lambda,\Theta,\Omega,x,\Delta,\tilde{\Delta})
with
(r_{j},\Lambda_{j},\Theta_{j},\Theta_{j+1},x_{j},\Delta^{(j)},\Delta^{(j-1)})
,
we have the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi^{(j)}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{N^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\psi^{(j)}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi^{(j+1)}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Fil}^{r;r^{\prime}}_{k}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{r}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\psi^{(j)}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}e_{j}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond}e_{j}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{N^{\diamond,(j)}}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{\diamond,(j)}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}e_{j+1}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond}e_{j}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{r}^{\diamond,(j)}}

where
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
is a pseudo-strongly divisible module in
\mathbf{Case}~(k)
with the assumption that
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
is an
S^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}}
-submodule of
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
.

By Proposition
5.1.23
, the quadruple in (
5.1.21
) with
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond}e_{j}
of
\mathbf{Case}~(k)
can be regarded as an example of a pseudo-strongly divisible module in
\mathbf{Case}~(k)
. We also note that the assumption
\mathrm{Fil}^{r;r^{\prime}}_{k}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
is illustrated in Theorem
4.1.10
.

Proof.

The commutativity of the squares involving
\phi^{\prime}
and
N^{\prime}
are immediate from (
5.1.17
) and
(
5.1.18
)
, respectively. For the commutativity of the square involving
\phi^{\prime}_{r}
, it is enough to check that the restriction
\phi^{(j)}:\mathrm{Fil}^{r;r^{\prime}}_{k}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond}e_{j}
is a well-defined isomorphism, as
\phi^{\prime}_{r}
and
\phi_{r}^{\diamond,(j)}
are induced from
\phi^{\prime}
and
\phi^{\diamond,j}
, respectively.

If
k=\infty
, then it is immediate by (i) of Lemma
5.1.9
. Assume
k\neq\infty
. By Lemma
3.1.11
, it is easy to see that we have

\Theta C(v)E^{\prime}_{1}+B(v)E^{\prime}_{2}=\sum_{i=0}^{r^{\prime}-1}D_{i}F^{\prime}_{k,i}.

Hence, by (ii) of Lemma
5.1.9
we complete the proof.
∎

We will apply the properties of the first row of the diagram in Proposition
5.1.23
to the second row, by identifying
(r^{\prime},\Lambda,\Theta,\Omega,x,\Delta,\tilde{\Delta},k^{\prime})
with
(r_{j},\Lambda_{j},\Theta_{j},\Theta_{j+1},x_{j},\Delta^{(j)},\Delta^{(j-1)},k_{j}^{\prime})
, respectively, for all
j\in\mathcal{J}
. In particular, one needs to determine when
(\Lambda_{j},\Theta_{j},\Theta_{j+1},x_{j})
satisfies the equations and inequalities of
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(k_{j}^{\prime})
for
k_{j}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}_{r_{j}}
(see Definition
4.1.22
).

Definition 5.1.24
.

Let
\vec{r}:=(r_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{J}}
with
0<r_{j}<p-1
for all
j\in\mathcal{J}
, and
\vec{k}^{\prime}:=(k^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\in\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\mathcal{K}_{r_{j}}
.
By the
equations and inequalities of
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
, we mean the equations and inequalities of
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(k_{j}^{\prime})
for all
j\in\mathcal{J}
. We say that
(\vec{\Lambda},\vec{\Theta},\vec{x})\in((E^{\times})^{f})^{2}\times E^{f}

satisfies the equations and inequalities of
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
if
(\Lambda_{j},\Theta_{j},\Theta_{j+1},x_{j})
satisfies the equations and inequalities of
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(r_{j};k_{j}^{\prime})
for all
j\in\mathcal{J}
.

We now sharpen the equations and inequalities in Table
1
. As each equation relates
v_{p}(\Theta_{j})
and
v_{p}(\Theta_{j+1})
, the equations and inequalities of
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(r_{j};k^{\prime}_{j})
are quite connected to those of
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(r_{j+1};k^{\prime}_{j+1})
for each
j\in\mathcal{J}
. We first define a sign function
s_{j}
as follows:

s_{j}:=\begin{cases}(-1)^{2k^{\prime}_{j}}&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}_{j}\neq\infty;\\
-1&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}_{j}=\infty.\end{cases}

Proposition 5.1.25
.

Let
\vec{r}:=(r_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{J}}
be given with
0<r_{j}<p-1
for all
j\in\mathcal{J}
and set
\vec{k}^{\prime}:=(k^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\in\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\mathcal{K}_{r_{j}}
. Then
(\vec{\Lambda},\vec{\Theta},\vec{x})\in((E^{\times})^{f})^{2}\times E^{f}
satisfy the equations and inequalities of
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
if and only if the tuples satisfy the following: if we set
T_{j}=v_{p}(\Theta_{j})
and
t_{j}=v_{p}(x_{j})
for all
j\in\mathcal{J}
then

(i)

for all
j\in\mathcal{J}
the equation

(5.1.26)

v_{p}(\Lambda_{j})=\frac{r_{j}-1-T_{j}+T_{j+1}}{2}

hold;

(ii)

for each
j\in\mathcal{J}
the following linear equation

(5.1.27)

T_{j}+s_{j}T_{j+1}=\begin{cases}2k^{\prime}_{j}-r_{j}-\frac{1+s_{j}}{2}&\mbox{if }\frac{1}{2}\leq k^{\prime}_{j}\leq\frac{r_{j}}{2}\,\,\mbox{or}\,\,k^{\prime}_{j}=r_{j}+\frac{1}{2};\\
2k^{\prime}_{j}-r_{j}-\frac{1+s_{j}}{2}+2t_{j}&\mbox{if }\frac{r_{j}+1}{2}\leq k^{\prime}_{j}\leq r_{j};\\
r_{j}+1&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}_{j}=\infty\end{cases}

hold;

(iii)

for each
j\in\mathcal{J}
the following inequalities

(5.1.28)

T_{j}\leq 0\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\begin{cases}T_{j}\leq t_{j}&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}_{j}=\frac{1}{2};\\
T_{j}\leq t_{j},\,-1\leq T_{j+1}&\mbox{if }1\leq k^{\prime}_{j}\leq\frac{r_{j}}{2};\\
T_{j}\geq t_{j}<0,\,-1\leq T_{j+1}&\mbox{if }\frac{r_{j}+1}{2}\leq k^{\prime}_{j}\leq r_{j}-\frac{1}{2};\\
t_{j}\leq T_{j}\leq t_{j}+r_{j}&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}_{j}=r_{j};\\
T_{j}\geq t_{j}+r_{j}&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}_{j}=r_{j}+\frac{1}{2};\\
\mbox{none}&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}_{j}=\infty\end{cases}

hold.

Note that if
(\vec{\Theta},\vec{x})
satisfies (
5.1.27
) and (
5.1.28
) then there always exist
\vec{\Lambda}
satisfying the condition in (
5.1.26
).

Proof.

The equation (
5.1.26
) is immediate from the equation (
4.1.23
) by letting
\Omega=\Theta_{j+1}
. The equation (
5.1.27
) is nothing else than the second equation in Table
1
. Similarly, one can immediately get the inequalities (
5.1.28
) from the inequalities in Table
1
. The inverse direction is also straightforward by replacing
\Theta_{j+1}
with
\Omega
.
∎

5.2.
Main results on Galois stable lattices

In this subsection, we state and prove the main results on Galois stable lattices. We have two versions of the main results. The first one starts with a weakly admissible filtered
(\phi,N)
-module
D
as in Example
2.1.3
, and then we give a candidate of strongly divisible modules and determine when this candidate becomes a strongly divisible module in
\mathcal{D}(D)
(see Theorem
5.2.1
). The second one starts with the data
(\vec{\Lambda},\vec{\Theta},\vec{x})
satisfying all the necessary equations and inequalities for some
\vec{k}^{\prime}
, and then we show that these data together with
\vec{\Delta}(T)
(determined by
\vec{k}^{\prime}
; see (
4.1.20
)) determine a weakly admissible filtered
(\phi,N)
-module
D
and a strongly divisible module in
\mathcal{D}(D)
(see Theorem
5.2.9
).

Throughout this subsection, we keep the notation of Example
2.1.3
, and, in particular, let
D=D(\lambda,\vec{\mathfrak{L}})
where
\vec{\mathfrak{L}}:=(\mathfrak{L}_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\in[\mathbb{P}^{1}(E)]^{f}
. Assume that
0<r_{j}<p-1
for all
j\in\mathcal{J}
, and fix
r
with
p-1>r\geq r_{j}
for all
j\in\mathcal{J}
. We also set
\mathcal{D}:=\mathcal{D}(D)
and
m_{j}:=\lfloor\tfrac{r_{j}}{2}\rfloor
, and recall
\mathfrak{M}_{(\vec{\theta}_{1},\vec{\theta}_{2},\vec{\varrho},\vec{\Delta})}
from (
5.1.4
).

We now prove the first version of our main results.

Theorem 5.2.1
.

Assume that
0<r_{j}<p-1
for all
j\in\mathcal{J}
, and fix an integer
r
with
r_{j}\leq r<p-1
for all
j\in\mathcal{J}
. Let
D=D(\lambda,\vec{\mathfrak{L}})
. If there exists
(k^{\prime}_{j})_{\in\mathcal{J}}\in\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\mathcal{K}_{r_{j}}
such that

(i)

k^{\prime}_{j}=\infty
if and only if
j\in I_{D}
;

(ii)

the triple
(\vec{\Lambda},\vec{\Theta},\vec{x})
induced by (
5.1.15
) and (
5.1.16
) satisfies the equations and inequalities of
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
;

(iii)

for each
j\in\mathcal{J}

\Delta^{(j)}
is equal to
\Delta
of
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(r_{j};k^{\prime}_{j})
,

then the
S_{\mathcal{O}}
-module
\mathfrak{M}_{(\vec{\theta}_{1},\vec{\theta}_{2},\vec{\varrho},\vec{\Delta})}
is a strongly divisible module in
\mathcal{D}
of weight
r
.

Note that the equations in (
5.1.19
) are automatically satisfied due to our definition of
(\vec{\Lambda},\vec{\Theta})
in (
5.1.15
). We also note that the value of
\vec{\Delta}^{\varphi}(-1)
appearing in (
5.1.16
) is explicitly computed in §
3.3
. More precisely,
\Delta^{(j)}(-1):=\delta_{l_{j}}(-1,x_{j})
where
l_{j}:=l(r_{j};k^{\prime}_{j})
is defined in (
4.1.19
), and
\delta_{l_{j}}(-1,x_{j})
is computed as follows:

(5.2.2)

\delta_{l_{j}}(-1,x_{j})=\begin{cases}-x_{j}&\mbox{if }l_{j}=0;\\
-H_{r_{j}-l_{j}}-H_{l_{j}-1}&\mbox{if }1\leq l_{j}\leq\frac{r_{j}+1}{2};\\
-H_{m_{j}}-H_{m_{j}-1}+\frac{1}{m_{j}^{2}(x_{j}+H_{m_{j}}+H_{m_{j}-1})}&\mbox{if }l_{j}=m_{j}+1\mbox{ and }r_{j}=2m_{j}\end{cases}

where the first case is obvious by its definition, the second case is from Lemma
3.3.4
(ii), and the last case is from Lemma
3.3.4
(iii), Lemma
3.3.2
(i), and Lemma
3.3.5
.

Remark 5.2.3
.

If
l_{j}=m_{j}+1
and
r_{j}=2m_{j}
then the identity (
5.1.16
) together with (
5.2.2
) gives rise to an equation of degree
2

px_{j+1}=p\mathfrak{L}_{j+1}-\mathfrak{L}_{j}+H_{m_{j}}+H_{m_{j}-1}-\frac{1}{m_{j}^{2}(x_{j}+H_{m_{j}}+H_{m_{j}-1})}.

This explains why the construction of some of the strongly divisible modules in
[
GP19
]
is more subtle than others. It also explain why a similar phenomenon didn’t occur in
[
BM
]
, as the Hodge–Tate weights treated in
[
BM
]
are
(0,r)
for odd positive integers
r
with
r<p-1
.

Proof of Theorem
5.2.1
.

By the assumption (ii), we see that for each
j\in\mathcal{J}
the quadruple
(\Lambda_{j},\Theta_{j},\Theta_{j+1},x_{j})
satisfies the equations and inequalities of
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(r_{j};k^{\prime}_{j})
, and so by Theorem
4.1.24
the quadruple is good for
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(r_{j};k^{\prime}_{j})
with respect to
\Delta^{(j)}
. In particular, it satisfies the inequalities in (
4.1.8
) and so
\mathfrak{M}_{(\vec{\theta}_{1},\vec{\theta}_{2},\vec{\varrho},\vec{\Delta})}
is
(\phi,N)
-stable by Lemma
4.1.7
. We also note that it satisfies the inequalities in Theorem
4.1.10
. Hence,
\mathfrak{M}_{(\vec{\theta}_{1},\vec{\theta}_{2},\vec{\varrho},\vec{\Delta})}
is a strongly divisible module in
\mathcal{D}
of weight
r
.
∎

For a given weakly admissible filtered
(\phi,N)
-module
D
, it is usually very difficult to construct a strongly divisible module in
\mathcal{D}:=\mathcal{D}(D)
. An advantage of Theorem
5.2.1
is that one can reduce constructing a strongly divisible module in
\mathcal{D}
to finding a triple
(\vec{\Lambda},\vec{\Theta},\vec{x})
satisfying the necessary equations and inequalities from Theorem
5.2.1
(ii) for some tuple
(k^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\in\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\mathcal{K}_{r_{j}}
. On the other hand, there is still an issue finding which tuple
(k^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\in\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\mathcal{K}_{r_{j}}
we need to choose, and the second version resolves this issue by starting with a tuple
(k^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\in\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\mathcal{K}_{r_{j}}
.

For the second version, we start by noting that if
(\vec{\Lambda},\vec{\Theta},\vec{x})
satisfies the equations and inequalities of
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
then we have

\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}}v_{p}(\Lambda_{j})=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}}(r_{j}-1)

due to (
5.1.26
). This is a necessary condition for (
5.1.19
) and (
2.1.4
).

For a given
(k^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{J}}
, it is not difficult to compute the necessary equations and inequalities. More precisely, for a given subset
\mathcal{J}_{0}\subset\mathcal{J}
with

(5.2.4)

\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}}(r_{j}-1)\geq 2\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}_{0}}r_{j}

(coming from (
2.1.4
)) and for each tuple
\vec{k}^{\prime}:=(k^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\in\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\mathcal{K}_{r_{j}}
with
\mathcal{J}_{\infty}(\vec{k}^{\prime})=\mathcal{J}_{0}
where

\mathcal{J}_{\infty}(\vec{k}^{\prime}):=\{j\in\mathcal{J}\mid k^{\prime}_{j}=\infty\},

it is not difficult to find
(\vec{\Lambda},\vec{\Theta},\vec{x})
satisfying the equations and inequalities of
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
. Furthermore, it is also not very difficult to describe
(\mathfrak{L}_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{J}\setminus\mathcal{J}_{\infty}(\vec{k}^{\prime})}
satisfying the equation (
5.1.16
). Before introducing the second version, we will further introduce the notation and the terminologies.

For a given tuple
\vec{r}:=(r_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\in\mathbf{Z}^{f}
with
0<r_{j}<p-1
for all
j\in\mathcal{J}
we set

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}(\vec{r}):=\bigg\{\vec{k}^{\prime}=(k^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\in\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\mathcal{K}_{r_{j}}\mid\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}}(r_{j}-1)\geq 2\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}_{\infty}(\vec{k}^{\prime})}r_{j}\bigg\},

and for an integer
r\in\mathbf{Z}
with
0<r<p-1
we also set

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}(r):=\left\{(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})\mid 0<r_{j}\leq r\,\,\mbox{ and }\,\,\vec{k}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}(\vec{r})\right\}.

For a given subset
\mathcal{J}_{0}\subset\mathcal{J}
with (
5.2.4
), we further define

E_{\mathcal{J}_{0}}^{f}:=\{\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in[\mathbb{P}^{1}(E)]^{f}\mid\mathfrak{L}_{j}=\infty\mbox{ for }j\in\mathcal{J}_{0}\mbox{ and }\mathfrak{L}_{j}\neq\infty\mbox{ for }j\not\in\mathcal{J}_{0}\}

and

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{K}(r,\mathcal{J}_{0}):=\{(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{K}(r)\mid\mathcal{J}_{\infty}(\vec{k}^{\prime})=\mathcal{J}_{0}\};&\\
\mathcal{K}(\vec{r},\mathcal{J}_{0}):=\{\vec{k}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}(\vec{r})\mid\mathcal{J}_{\infty}(\vec{k}^{\prime})=\mathcal{J}_{0}\}.&\end{array}\right.

We will later use the map
\psi^{\mathcal{J}_{0}}:E^{f}\rightarrow E_{\mathcal{J}_{0}}^{f}
sending
(y_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{J}}
to
(y^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{J}_{0}}
where
y^{\prime}_{j}:=y_{j}
if
j\not\in\mathcal{J}_{0}
and
y^{\prime}_{j}:=\infty
if
j\in\mathcal{J}_{0}
.

For
(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{K}(r)
, we define a subset of
\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{f}(=\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathcal{J}})
, denoted by
R^{\prime}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
, by the set of
(t_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{J}}
in
\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{f}
such that
\big((T_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{J}},(t_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\big)
satisfies
(
5.1.27
) and (
5.1.28
) for
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
for some
(T_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\in\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{f}
. We expect that for each
\mathcal{J}_{0}\subset\mathcal{J}
with (
5.2.4
)

\bigcup_{\vec{k}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}(\vec{r},\mathcal{J}_{0})}R^{\prime}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})=\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{f}.

We further let
R^{\prime}_{int}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
be the topological interior of
R^{\prime}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
in
\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{f}
.

Definition 5.2.5
.

For a given
(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{K}(r)
, we say that
\vec{k}^{\prime}
is
valid
if
R^{\prime}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})\neq\emptyset
.

We point out that the equation (
5.1.27
) and the inequality (
5.1.28
) do not impose any restriction on
t_{j}
if
k^{\prime}_{j}=\infty
. Hence, for any valid
\vec{k}^{\prime}
, there is a set
R(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})\subset\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathcal{J}\setminus\mathcal{J}_{\infty}(\vec{k}^{\prime})}
such that

(5.2.6)

R^{\prime}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})=\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathcal{J}_{\infty}(\vec{k}^{\prime})}\times R(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime}),

where
\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathcal{J}_{\infty}(\vec{k}^{\prime})}\times\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathcal{J}\setminus\mathcal{J}_{\infty}(\vec{k}^{\prime})}
is naturally identified with
\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathcal{J}}
. We call this
R(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})\subset\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathcal{J}\setminus\mathcal{J}_{\infty}(\vec{k}^{\prime})}

the areal support of
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
, and we define
R_{int}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
as the topological interior of
R(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
as well. It is obvious that
\vec{k}^{\prime}
is valid if and only if
R(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
is nonempty. It is also immediate that
R(\vec{r};\vec{k})=R^{\prime}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
if
\mathcal{J}_{\infty}(\vec{k}^{\prime})=\emptyset
. We expect that for each
\mathcal{J}_{0}\subset\mathcal{J}
with (
5.2.4
)

\bigcup_{\vec{k}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}(\vec{r};\mathcal{J}_{0})}R(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})=\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathcal{J}\setminus\mathcal{J}_{0}}.

We now attach
\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in[\mathbb{P}^{1}(E)]^{f}
to
\vec{x}
via (
5.1.16
) for each
\vec{k}^{\prime}
. Recall that we set
m_{j}:=\lfloor r_{j}/2\rfloor
. Since
\varphi^{f}=1
, we have the following identity

(p-\varphi)^{-1}=\frac{1}{p^{f}-1}(p^{f-1}+p^{f-2}\varphi+\cdots+\varphi^{f-1}),

as a linear automorphism on
E^{f}
, so that the linear map
(p-\varphi)^{-1}
is well-defined. For each tuple of integers
\vec{l}=(l_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{J}}
with
l_{j}\in[0,m_{j}+1]
, the association

\vec{x}\mapsto p\vec{x}+\vec{\delta}_{\vec{l}}^{\varphi}(-1,\vec{x}),

which is inspired by (
5.1.16
), also gives rise to a function from
E^{f}
to itself, denoted by
\psi_{\vec{l}}
. (In fact, this map
\psi_{\vec{l}}
is ill-defined as its domain can not be the whole
E^{f}
in general. But for our purpose for defining the support below it is good enough since the domain of
\psi_{\vec{l}}
always contains
v_{p}^{-1}(R^{\prime}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime}))
.)

Lemma 5.2.7
.

If
(r_{j};l_{j})\not\in\{(r_{j};0),(2m_{j};m_{j}+1)\}
for some
j\in\mathcal{J}
then
\psi_{\vec{l}}
is injective.

Note that the condition for the lemma is required due to the second comments right after Definition
3.3.1
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume that the condition holds only for
j=f-1
. Then we have

\psi_{\vec{l}}(\vec{x})=(px_{0}+\delta_{l_{f-1}}(-1),px_{1}+\delta_{l_{0}}(-1,x_{0}),\cdots,px_{f-1}+\delta_{l_{f-2}}(-1,x_{f-2})),

and so it is obvious that the injectivity of the first coordinate implies the others.
∎

For
\vec{l}=(l_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{J}}
with
l_{j}\in[0,m_{j}+1]
and for
\mathcal{J}_{0}\subset\mathcal{J}
we have the following sequence of functions

\textstyle{\hat{\mathbf{R}}^{f}}
\textstyle{E^{f}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{v_{p}}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{\vec{l}}}
\textstyle{E^{f}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\,(p-\varphi)^{-1}}
\textstyle{E^{f}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi^{\mathcal{J}_{0}}}
\textstyle{E^{f}_{\mathcal{J}_{0}},}

and for any valid
\vec{k}^{\prime}

the support of
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
is defined as follows:

(5.2.8)

\displaystyle\Supp(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime}):=\big(\psi^{\mathcal{J}_{\infty}(\vec{k}^{\prime})}\circ(p-\varphi)^{-1}\circ\psi_{\vec{l}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})}\big)\left(v_{p}^{-1}(R^{\prime}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime}))\right)

where
\vec{l}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime}):=(l(r_{j};k_{j}^{\prime}))_{j\in\mathcal{J}}
and
l(r_{j};k_{j}^{\prime})
is defined in (
4.1.19
). We emphasize that the composition of maps
\psi^{\mathcal{J}_{\infty}(\vec{k}^{\prime})}\circ(p-\varphi)^{-1}\circ\psi_{\vec{l}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})}
depends on
\vec{k}^{\prime}
.

We are now ready to state and prove the second version. For a valid
\vec{k}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}(\vec{r})
, if
(\vec{\Lambda},\vec{\Theta},\vec{x})
satisfies the equations and inequalities of
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
(see Proposition
5.1.25
) then we have the following data:

(i)

\vec{\mathfrak{L}}=\psi^{\mathcal{J}_{\infty}(\vec{k}^{\prime})}\circ(p-\varphi)^{-1}\circ\psi_{\vec{l}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})}(\vec{x})\in\Supp(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
;

(ii)

\vec{\varrho}=(p-\varphi)^{-1}\circ\psi_{\vec{l}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})}(\vec{x})
;

(iii)

A choice of
\lambda\in\mathcal{O}
satisfying the equation (
5.1.19
);

(iv)

\Delta^{(j)}(T)=\delta_{l(r_{j};k_{j}^{\prime})}(T,x)
for all
j\in\mathcal{J}
(see (
4.1.20
));

(v)

A choice of
(\vec{\theta}_{1},\vec{\theta}_{2})
via the bijection (
5.1.20
).

Moreover, the data (i) and (iii) together with
\vec{r}
determine a weakly admissible filtered
(\phi,N)
-module
D=D(\lambda,\vec{\mathfrak{L}})
, and the data (ii), (iv), and (v) determine an
S_{\mathcal{O}}
-submodule
\mathfrak{M}_{(\vec{\theta}_{1},\vec{\theta}_{2},\vec{\varrho},\vec{\Delta})}
defined in (
5.1.4
). Note that the weakly admissible filtered
(\phi,N)
-module
D=D(\lambda,\vec{\mathfrak{L}})
does not depend on the choice of
\lambda
, by Lemma
2.1.5
.

Theorem 5.2.9
.

For a valid
\vec{k}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}(\vec{r})
, if
(\vec{\Lambda},\vec{\Theta},\vec{x})\in((E^{\times})^{f})^{2}\times E^{f}
satisfies the equations and inequalities of
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
then the corresponding
\mathfrak{M}_{(\vec{\theta}_{1},\vec{\theta}_{2},\vec{\varrho},\vec{\Delta})}
is a strongly divisible module in
\mathcal{D}(D)
of weight
r
.

Proof.

We prove this theorem by checking the conditions of Theorem
5.2.1
. It is obvious that
\mathcal{J}_{0}:=\mathcal{J}_{\infty}(\vec{k}^{\prime})=I_{D}
, which implies the condition (i) of Theorem
5.2.1
. The condition (iii) of Theorem
5.2.1
is also immediate, as that is our choice of
\vec{\Delta}
.
For the condition (ii) of Theorem
5.2.1
, it is clear that
\psi^{\mathcal{J}_{0}}(\vec{\varrho})=\vec{\mathfrak{L}}
, and it is also clear that
\vec{x}
and
\vec{\varrho}
satisfies the equation (
5.1.16
). Moreover,
(\vec{\theta}_{1},\vec{\theta}_{2})
determines
(\vec{\Lambda},\vec{\Theta})
via (
5.1.15
), which completes the proof.
∎

An advantage of Theorem
5.2.9
is that we do not have an issue to find an appropriate
\vec{k}^{\prime}
for a given
D
. Moreover, for a given
\vec{k}^{\prime}
it is easy to compute all the equations and inequalities of Proposition
5.1.25
. However, one can still ask that in this way we can always find a strongly divisible module for each
D
. We expect yes! In other words, we expect that for each
\mathcal{J}_{0}\subset\mathcal{J}
with (
5.2.4
)

(5.2.10)

\bigcup_{\vec{k}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}(\vec{r},\mathcal{J}_{0})}\Supp(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})=E^{f}_{\mathcal{J}_{0}}.

In fact, we expect more! If the mod-
p
reduction is an extension of two distinct characters, then we expect that both non-homothetic lattices can be obtained in this way. The reason is that the only artificial assumption we made for the pseudo-strongly divisible modules is
v_{p}(x)\neq 0
in some cases (see Table
1
or Proposition
5.1.25
), but we expect that such cases will be covered by other cases. We will illustrate that our expectation is correct for certain cases when
f=2
in the next section, §
6
(see Remarks
6.2.2
and
6.3.2
). We will also show that we can recover the results of
[
BM
,
GP19
,
LP
]
of
f=1
case.

Finally, we observe when
\mathfrak{M}_{(\vec{\theta}_{1},\vec{\theta}_{2},\vec{\varrho},\vec{\Delta})}
are homothetic for different choice of the data.

Corollary 5.2.11
.

Assume that
(\vec{\Lambda},\vec{\Theta},\vec{x})
and
(\vec{\Lambda}^{\prime},\vec{\Theta}^{\prime},\vec{x}^{\prime})
satisfy the equations and inequalities of
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
, and choose
(\vec{\theta}_{1},\vec{\theta}_{2})
and
(\vec{\theta}_{1}^{\prime},\vec{\theta}_{2}^{\prime})
, respectively, via the bijection (
5.1.20
). If

(i)

v_{p}(\Lambda_{j})=v_{p}(\Lambda^{\prime}_{j})
for all
j\in\mathcal{J}
and
\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\Lambda_{j}=\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\Lambda^{\prime}_{j}
;

(ii)

v_{p}(\Theta_{j})=v_{p}(\Theta^{\prime}_{j})
for all
j\in\mathcal{J}
;

(iii)

\vec{x}=\vec{x}^{\prime}
and so
\vec{\varrho}:=(p-\varphi)^{-1}\circ\psi_{\vec{l}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})}(\vec{x})=(p-\varphi)^{-1}\circ\psi_{\vec{l}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})}(\vec{x}^{\prime})
,

then
\mathfrak{M}_{(\vec{\theta}_{1},\vec{\theta}_{2},\vec{\varrho},\vec{\Delta})}
is homothetic to
\mathfrak{M}_{(\vec{\theta}^{\prime}_{1},\vec{\theta}^{\prime}_{2},\vec{\varrho},\vec{\Delta})}
.

Proof.

By (i) and (ii), there exist
\vec{a},\vec{b}\in(\mathcal{O}^{\times})^{f}
and
