c\in E^{\times}
such that
\vec{a}\cdot\vec{\theta}_{1}=\vec{\theta}_{1}^{\prime}\cdot c
and
\vec{b}\cdot\vec{\theta}_{2}=\vec{\theta}_{2}^{\prime}\cdot c
. Since
\vec{a},\vec{b}\in(\mathcal{O}^{\times})^{f}
, the multiplication by these two elements does not change
\mathfrak{M}_{(\vec{\theta}_{1},\vec{\theta}_{2},\vec{\varrho},\vec{\Delta})}
and
\mathfrak{M}_{(\vec{\theta}_{1}^{\prime},\vec{\theta}_{2}^{\prime},\vec{\varrho}^{\prime},\vec{\Delta})}
as
S_{\mathcal{O}}
-modules. Moreover, the multiplication by
c
is nothing else than homothety. Hence, the two modules are homothetic.
∎

5.3.
Isotypic components of Breuil modules

In this subsection, we use the mod-
p
reduction of the pseudo-strongly divisible modules
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
developed in §
4

to compute the mod-
p
reduction of the isotypic component of the
S_{\mathcal{O}}
-module
\mathfrak{M}_{\vec{\theta}_{1},\vec{\theta}_{2},\vec{\varrho},\vec{\Delta}}
at
j\in\mathcal{J}
. Once
(\vec{\Lambda},\vec{\Theta},\vec{x})\in((E^{\times})^{f})^{2}\times E^{f}
satisfies the equations and inequalities of
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
for some
\vec{k}^{\prime}\in\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\mathcal{K}_{r_{j}}
, one may directly give the structure of the mod-
p
reduction

\mathcal{M}^{\diamond}:=\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond}/(\varpi,\mathrm{Fil}^{p}S_{\mathcal{O}})\mathfrak{M}^{\diamond}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{1},\overline{E}_{2}).

For each
j\in\mathcal{J}
, the isotypic component
\mathcal{M}^{\diamond}e_{j}
of
\mathcal{M}^{\diamond}
has the following additional structure:

\big(\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathcal{M}^{\diamond}e_{j},\,\,\phi_{r}^{\diamond,(j)}:\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathcal{M}^{\diamond}e_{j}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{\diamond}e_{j+1},\,\,N^{\diamond,(j)}:\mathcal{M}^{\diamond}e_{j}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{\diamond}e_{j}\big)

depending on
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(r_{j};k^{\prime}_{j})
. We write
\underline{E}^{(j)}
for the ordered basis
(\overline{E}_{1}^{(j)}\ \overline{E}_{2}^{(j)})
of the isotypic component
\mathcal{M}^{\diamond}e_{j}
and we let
\overline{F}^{(j)}=(\overline{F}_{1}^{(j)}\ \overline{F}_{2}^{(j)})
be an ordered pair of generators for
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathcal{M}^{\diamond}e_{j}
over
\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}e_{j}(\cong\overline{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{F}})
.

We set
k_{j}:=\lfloor k^{\prime}_{j}\rfloor
, and to lighten the notation we will write

\begin{cases}M_{N,j}:=\Mat_{\underline{E}^{(j)},\underline{E}^{(j)}}(N^{\diamond,(j)});\\
M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{r},j}:=\Mat_{\underline{E}^{(j)},\underline{F}^{(j)}}(\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathcal{M}^{\diamond}e_{j});\\
M_{\phi_{r},j}:=\Mat_{\underline{E}^{(j+1)},\underline{F}^{(j)}}(\phi_{r}^{\diamond,(j)})\end{cases}

throughout this subsection. We further define the following pair of units
\alpha_{j},\beta_{j}
in
\mathbf{F}^{\times}
for each
(r_{j};k^{\prime}_{j})
by

(\alpha_{j},\beta_{j}):=\begin{cases}\big(\frac{\Lambda_{j}}{p^{r_{j}-k_{j}-1}},\ \frac{\Lambda_{j}\Theta_{j}}{p^{k_{j}}\Theta_{j+1}}\big)&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}_{j}=k_{j}+\tfrac{1}{2}\in(\tfrac{1}{2}+\mathbf{Z})\cap[\frac{1}{2},\tfrac{r_{j}}{2}];\\
\big(\frac{\Lambda_{j}\Theta_{j}}{p^{k_{j}-1}},\ \frac{\Lambda_{j}}{p^{r_{j}-k_{j}}\Theta_{j+1}}\big)&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}_{j}=k_{j}\in\mathbf{Z}\cap[\frac{1}{2},\tfrac{r_{j}}{2}];\\
\big(\frac{\Lambda_{j}x_{j}}{p^{r_{j}-k_{j}-1}},\ \frac{\Lambda_{j}\Theta_{j}}{p^{k_{j}}x_{j}\Theta_{j+1}}\big)&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}_{j}=k_{j}+\tfrac{1}{2}\in(\tfrac{1}{2}+\mathbf{Z})\cap[\tfrac{r_{j}+1}{2},r_{j}];\\
\big(\frac{\Lambda_{j}\Theta_{j}}{p^{k_{j}-1}x_{j}},\ \frac{\Lambda_{j}x_{j}}{p^{r_{j}-k_{j}}\Theta_{j+1}}\big)&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}_{j}=k_{j}\in\mathbf{Z}\cap[\tfrac{r_{j}+1}{2},r_{j}];\\
\big(p\Lambda_{j},\ \frac{\Lambda_{j}\Theta_{j}}{p^{r_{j}}\Theta_{j+1}}\big)&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}_{j}=r_{j}+\frac{1}{2},\infty.\end{cases}

It is easy to see from this definition that

(5.3.1)

\left(\frac{\alpha_{j}}{\beta_{j}}\right)^{s_{j}}=\begin{cases}\Theta_{j}\Theta_{j+1}^{s_{j}}p^{r_{j}-2k^{\prime}_{j}+\frac{1+s_{j}}{2}}&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}_{j}\in[\frac{1}{2},\frac{r_{j}}{2}]\cup\{r_{j}+\frac{1}{2}\};\\
\Theta_{j}\Theta_{j+1}^{s_{j}}p^{r_{j}-2k^{\prime}_{j}+\frac{1+s_{j}}{2}}x_{j}^{-2}&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}_{j}\in[\frac{r_{j}+1}{2},r_{j}];\\
\Theta_{j}\Theta_{j+1}^{s_{j}}p^{-r_{j}-1}&\mbox{if }k^{\prime}_{j}=\infty\end{cases}

where
s_{j}
is defined right before Proposition
5.1.25
.

5.3.1.
Description of
N^{\diamond,(j)}

In this section of paragraph, we describe the matrix
M_{N,j}
.

As
N(\gamma)/p\equiv-u^{p-1}\pmod{(\varpi,\mathrm{Fil}^{p}S_{\mathcal{O}})}
one has

M_{N,j}=\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
\frac{1}{\Theta_{j}}\big(1-\dot{\Delta}^{(j-1)}(-1)u^{p-1}\big)&0\end{bmatrix},

coming from (
4.1.5
), where
\Delta^{(j)}(-1)=\delta_{l(r_{j};k^{\prime}_{j})}(-1,x_{j})
. Here,
l(r_{j};k^{\prime}_{j})
has been defined in (
4.1.20
). One can find the value of
\dot{\delta}_{l}(-1)
in Lemma
3.3.7
for
\delta_{l}\neq\delta_{0}
while one obviously has
\dot{\delta}_{0}(-1,x_{j})=0
.

We introducing the following definition which lets us to describe
N^{(j)}
shortly.

Definition 5.3.2
.

For a rank 2 Breuil module
\mathcal{M}
, we define the
monodromy type of
\mathcal{M}
by a tuple of integer
\mathrm{MT}=(\mathrm{MT}_{0},\cdots,\mathrm{MT}_{f-1})
, where each
\mathrm{MT}_{j}
is given by

\mathrm{MT}_{j}=\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbf{F}}(N^{(j)}:\mathcal{M}^{(j)}/u\mathcal{M}^{(j)}\to\mathcal{M}^{(j)}/u\mathcal{M}^{(j)}).

In the same fashion, we define the monodromy type of a mod-
p
reduction of pseudo-strongly divisible module.

Recalling that
v_{p}(\Theta_{j})\leq 0
for all
j\in\mathcal{J}
, it is worth noting that
\mathrm{MT}_{j}=1
if
v_{p}(\Theta_{j})=0
, and
\mathrm{MT}_{j}=0
if
v_{p}(\Theta_{j})<0
.

Remark 5.3.3
.

By Theorem 4.3.2 of
[
Car06
]
, any semisimple rank 2 Breuil module has monodromy type
(0,\cdots,0)
. Hence, any Breuil module with non-trivial monodromy type is not semisimple. This fact will be frequently used to see non-splitness of rank 2 Breuil modules in our examples.

5.3.2.
Description of
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathcal{M}^{\diamond}e_{j}
and
\phi_{r}^{\diamond,(j)}
when
r_{j}\geq 2

In this section of paragraph, we describe the matrices
M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{r},j}
and
M_{\phi_{r},j}
when
k^{\prime}_{j}<\infty
and
r_{j}\geq 2
.

Proposition 5.3.4
.

Assume that
r_{j}\geq 2
. If
k^{\prime}_{j}\in(\frac{1}{2}+\mathbf{Z})
, then the matrices representing
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathcal{M}^{\diamond}e_{j}
and
\phi_{r}^{\diamond,(j)}
are given as follow:

(i)

if
k^{\prime}_{j}=\frac{1}{2}
then

\displaystyle M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{r},j}=\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
\frac{x_{j}}{\Theta_{j}}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}u^{r-r_{j}}&0\\
\frac{\alpha_{j}}{\beta_{j}\Theta_{j+1}}\frac{(-1)^{r_{j}}u^{r-1}}{r_{j}-1}&u^{r}\end{bmatrix}

and

\displaystyle M_{\phi_{r},j}=\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
\frac{r_{j}-1}{\Theta_{j+1}}u^{p-1}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\alpha_{j}(-1)^{r-r_{j}}&0\\
0&\beta_{j}(-1)^{r}\end{bmatrix};

(ii)

if
k^{\prime}_{j}=k_{j}+\frac{1}{2}\in[1,\frac{r_{j}-1}{2}]
then

\displaystyle M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{r},j}=\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
\frac{x_{j}}{\Theta_{j}}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}u^{r-r_{j}+k_{j}}&0\\
\frac{\alpha_{j}}{\beta_{j}\Theta_{j+1}}\frac{(-1)^{r_{j}}u^{r-k_{j}-1}}{(r_{j}-2k_{j}-1)\binom{r_{j}-k_{j}-1}{k_{j}}^{2}}&u^{r-k_{j}}\end{bmatrix}

and

\displaystyle M_{\phi_{r},j}=\begin{bmatrix}1&p\Theta_{j+1}\frac{k_{j}(r_{j}-k_{j})}{r_{j}-2k_{j}}\\
\frac{2(r_{j}-k_{j}-1)k_{j}+r_{j}-1}{\Theta_{j+1}}u^{p-1}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\alpha_{j}\frac{(-1)^{r-r_{j}}}{\binom{r_{j}-k_{j}-1}{k_{j}}}&0\\
0&\beta_{j}(-1)^{r}\binom{r_{j}-k_{j}-1}{k_{j}}\end{bmatrix};

(iii)

if
k^{\prime}_{j}=m_{j}+\frac{1}{2}
with
r_{j}=2m_{j}
then

\displaystyle M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{r},j}=\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{\Theta_{j}}{x_{j}}\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}u^{r-m_{j}}&0\\
-\frac{\alpha_{j}}{\beta_{j}\Theta_{j+1}}m_{j}^{2}u^{r-m_{j}-1}&u^{r-m_{j}}\end{bmatrix}

and

\displaystyle M_{\phi_{r},j}=\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
\frac{2m_{j}^{2}-1}{\Theta_{j+1}}u^{p-1}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\alpha_{j}(-1)^{r+1}m_{j}&0\\
0&\beta_{j}\frac{(-1)^{r+1}}{m_{j}}\end{bmatrix};

(iv)

if
k^{\prime}_{j}=m_{j}+\frac{1}{2}
with
r_{j}=2m_{j}+1
then

\displaystyle M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{r},j}=\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
\frac{x_{j}}{\Theta_{j}}+\frac{\alpha_{j}}{\beta_{j}\Theta_{j+1}}2H_{m_{j}}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}u^{r-m_{j}-1}&0\\
0&u^{r-m_{j}}\end{bmatrix}

and

\displaystyle M_{\phi_{r},j}=\begin{bmatrix}1&p\Theta_{j+1}m_{j}(m_{j}+1)\\
\frac{2m_{j}(m_{j}+1)}{\Theta_{j+1}}u^{p-1}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\alpha_{j}(-1)^{r+1}&0\\
0&\beta_{j}(-1)^{r}\end{bmatrix};

(v)

if
k^{\prime}_{j}=k_{j}+\frac{1}{2}\in[m_{j}+1,r_{j}]
then

\displaystyle M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{r},j}=\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{\Theta_{j}}{x_{j}}\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}u^{r-r_{j}+k_{j}}&0\\
\frac{\alpha_{j}}{\beta_{j}\Theta_{j+1}}(-1)^{r_{j}-1}(2k_{j}-r_{j}+1)\binom{k_{j}}{r_{j}-k_{j}-1}^{2}u^{r-k_{j}-1}&u^{r-k_{j}}\end{bmatrix}

and

M_{\phi_{r},j}=\begin{bmatrix}1&p\Theta_{j+1}\frac{k_{j}(r_{j}-k_{j})}{r_{j}-2k_{j}}\\
\frac{2(r_{j}-k_{j}-1)k_{j}+r_{j}-1}{\Theta_{j+1}}u^{p-1}+\frac{b_{r_{j},k_{j}+1}(-1)}{p\Theta_{j+1}x_{j}}&1\end{bmatrix}\\
\times\begin{bmatrix}\alpha_{j}(-1)^{r+1}(r_{j}-k_{j})\binom{k_{j}}{r_{j}-k_{j}}&0\\
0&\beta_{j}\frac{(-1)^{r-r_{j}+1}}{(r_{j}-k_{j})\binom{k_{j}}{r_{j}-k_{j}}}\end{bmatrix}

where
b_{r_{j},k_{j}+1}(-1)=-\frac{2m_{j}^{2}+2m_{j}+1}{m_{j}^{2}(m_{j}+1)^{2}}
if
(r_{j},k_{j})=(2m_{j}+1,m_{j}+1)
and
b_{r_{j},k_{j}+1}(-1)=0
otherwise;

(vi)

if
k^{\prime}_{j}=r_{j}+\frac{1}{2}
then

\displaystyle M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{r},j}=\begin{bmatrix}u^{r}&0\\
0&u^{r-r_{j}}\end{bmatrix}

and

\displaystyle M_{\phi_{r},j}=\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{\alpha_{j}}{\beta_{j}}\frac{\Theta_{j}}{p^{r_{j}}x_{j}}\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\alpha_{j}(-1)^{r}&0\\
0&\beta_{j}(-1)^{r-r_{j}}\end{bmatrix}.

Proof.

This proposition follows immediately from the results in §
4
. Note that the
r=r_{j}
was assumed for the results in §
4
. But by simply multiplying
v^{r-r_{j}}
to
G_{1}^{\prime},G_{2}^{\prime}
as in (
4.1.11
) and
(-1)^{r-r_{j}}
to the image of
\phi_{r}(\bar{F}_{1}),\phi(\bar{F}_{2})
, we could have the description as in the statement. More precise reference details are provided in the following.

(i)

Let
k^{\prime}_{j}=\frac{1}{2}
. Then
M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{r},j}
is computed in Proposition
4.2.1
, and
M_{\phi_{r},j}
is given by Proposition
4.3.1
, where
P_{0,0}(-1,0)=1
by Lemma
3.3.2
(i) and
\dot{\delta}_{1}(-1)=r_{j}-1
by Lemma
3.3.7
(iii).

(ii)

Let
k^{\prime}_{j}=k_{j}+\frac{1}{2}\in[1,\frac{r_{j}-1}{2}]
. Then
M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{r},j}
is computed in Proposition
4.2.4
, where
\frac{1}{a_{k_{j}+1}}=\frac{(-1)^{r_{j}}}{(r_{j}-2k_{j}-1)\binom{r_{j}-k_{j}-1}{k_{j}}^{2}}
by Lemma
3.3.2
(i) and (ii) and
p^{r_{j}-2k_{j}+1}\Theta_{j}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. Also,
M_{\phi_{r},j}
is given by Proposition
4.3.2
, where
P_{k_{j},k_{j}}(-1,0)=\binom{r_{j}-k_{j}-1}{k_{j}}^{-1}
and
P_{k_{j},0}(-1,0)=(-1)^{r_{j}}k_{j}\binom{r_{j}-k_{j}}{k_{j}}
by Lemma
3.3.2
(i) and (ii),
\delta_{k_{j}}(-1)-\delta_{k_{j}+1}(-1)=\frac{r_{j}-2k_{j}}{k_{j}(r_{j}-k_{j})}
by Lemma
3.3.4
(i), and
\dot{\delta}_{k_{j}+1}(-1)=2(r_{j}-k_{j}-1)k_{j}+r_{j}-1
by Lemma
3.3.7
(iii).

(iii)

Let
k^{\prime}_{j}=m_{j}+\frac{1}{2}
with
r_{j}=2m_{j}
. Then
M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{r},j}
is computed in Proposition
4.2.9
and Proposition
4.3.3
, where
a_{m_{j}}=m_{j}^{2}
by Lemma
3.3.2
(i) and (ii). Also,
M_{\phi_{r},j}
is given by Proposition
4.3.3
, where
P_{m_{j},0}(-1,0)=m_{j}
by Lemma
3.3.2
(i) and
\dot{\delta}_{m_{j}}(-1)=2m_{j}^{2}-1
by Lemma
3.3.7
(iii).

(iv)

Let
k^{\prime}_{j}=m_{j}+\frac{1}{2}
with
r_{j}=2m_{j}+1
. Then
M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{r},j}
is computed in Proposition
4.2.4
, where
\frac{1}{a_{m_{j}+1}}=2H_{m_{j}}
by Lemma
3.2.9
(v) and
p^{r_{j}-2m_{j}+1}\Theta_{j}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. Also,
M_{\phi_{r},j}
is given by Proposition
4.3.3
, where
P_{m_{j},m_{j}}(-1,0)=1
and
P_{m_{j},0}(-1,0)=-m_{j}(m_{j}+1)
by Lemma
3.3.2
(i) and (ii),
\delta_{m_{j}}(-1)-\delta_{m_{j}+1}(-1)=\frac{1}{m_{j}(m_{j}+1)}
by Lemma
3.3.4
(i), and
\dot{\delta}_{m_{j}+1}(-1)=2m_{j}(m_{j}+1)
by Lemma
3.3.7
(iii).

(v)

Let
k^{\prime}_{j}=k_{j}+\frac{1}{2}\in[m_{j}+1,r_{j}]
. Then
M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{r},j}
is computed from in Proposition
4.2.13
, where
a_{r_{j}-k_{j}}=(-1)^{r_{j}}(2k_{j}-r_{j}+1)\binom{k_{j}}{r_{j}-k_{j}-1}^{2}
by Lemma
3.3.2
(i) and (ii) and
\frac{\Theta_{j}}{p^{2k_{j}-r_{j}-1}x_{j}^{2}}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. Also,
M_{\phi_{r},j}
is given by Proposition
4.3.7
and Proposition
4.3.10
, where
P_{r_{j}-k_{j},0}(-1,0)=(-1)^{r_{j}}(r_{j}-k_{j})\binom{k_{j}}{r_{j}-k_{j}}
and
P_{r_{j}-k_{j},r_{j}-k_{j}}(-1,0)=\binom{k_{j}-1}{r_{j}-k_{j}}^{-1}
by Lemma
3.3.2
(i) and (ii),
\delta_{r_{j}-k_{j}+1}(-1)-\delta_{r_{j}-k_{j}}(-1)=\frac{r_{j}-2k_{j}}{k_{j}(r_{j}-k_{j})}
by Lemma
3.3.4
(i),
\dot{\delta}_{r_{j}-k_{j}}(-1)=2(r_{j}-k_{j}+1)k_{j}+r_{j}-1
by Lemma
3.3.7
(iii), and
b_{2m_{j}+1,m_{j}+2}(-1)=-\frac{2m_{j}^{2}+2m_{j}+1}{m_{j}^{2}(m_{j}+1)^{2}}
by Lemma
3.3.11
.

(vi)

Let
k^{\prime}_{j}=r_{j}+\frac{1}{2}
.
M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{r},j}
is computed in Proposition
4.2.21
where
\frac{\Theta_{j}}{x_{j}}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
, and the form of
M_{\phi_{r},j}
is given by Proposition
4.3.13
.

This completes the proof.
∎

Proposition 5.3.5
.

Assume that
r_{j}\geq 2
. If
k^{\prime}_{j}\in\mathbf{Z}
, then the matrices representing
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathcal{M}^{\diamond}e_{j}
and
\phi_{r}^{\diamond,(j)}
are given as follow:

(i)

if
k^{\prime}_{j}=k_{j}\in[\frac{1}{2},\frac{r_{j}-1}{2}]
then

\displaystyle M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{r},j}=\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
\frac{x_{j}}{\Theta_{j}}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}u^{r-r_{j}+k_{j}}&\frac{\alpha_{j}}{\beta_{j}\Theta_{j+1}}(-1)^{r_{j}}(r_{j}-2k_{j}+1)\binom{r_{j}-k_{j}}{k_{j}-1}^{2}u^{r-r_{j}+k_{j}-1}\\
0&u^{r-k_{j}}\end{bmatrix}

and

M_{\phi_{r},j}=\begin{bmatrix}1&p\Theta_{j+1}\frac{-k_{j}(r_{j}-k_{j})}{r_{j}-2k_{j}}\\
\frac{2(r_{j}-k_{j})(k_{j}-1)+r_{j}-1}{\Theta_{j+1}}u^{p-1}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}0&\alpha_{j}(-1)^{r}k_{j}\binom{r_{j}-k_{j}}{k_{j}}\\
\beta_{j}\frac{(-1)^{r-r_{j}+1}}{k_{j}\binom{r_{j}-k_{j}}{k_{j}}}&0\end{bmatrix};

(ii)

if
k^{\prime}_{j}=m_{j}
with
r_{j}=2m_{j}
then

\displaystyle M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{r},j}=\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
\frac{x_{j}}{\Theta_{j}}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}u^{r-m_{j}}&\frac{\alpha_{j}}{\beta_{j}\Theta_{j+1}}m_{j}^{2}u^{r-m_{j}-1}\\
0&u^{r-m_{j}}\end{bmatrix}

and

\displaystyle M_{\phi_{r},j}=\begin{bmatrix}1&-p\Theta_{j+1}m_{j}^{2}(H_{m_{j}}+H_{m_{j}-1})\\
\frac{2m_{j}^{2}-1}{\Theta_{j+1}}u^{p-1}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}0&\alpha_{j}(-1)^{r}m_{j}\\
\beta_{j}\frac{(-1)^{r+1}}{m_{j}}&0\end{bmatrix};

(iii)

if
k^{\prime}_{j}=m_{j}+1
with
r_{j}=2m_{j}+1
then

\displaystyle M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{r},j}=\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{\Theta_{j}}{x_{j}}\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}u^{r-m_{j}}&0\\
0&u^{r-m_{j}-1}\end{bmatrix}

and

\displaystyle M_{\phi_{r},j}=\begin{bmatrix}1&p\Theta_{j+1}m_{j}(m_{j}+1)\\
\frac{2m_{j}(m_{j}+1)}{\Theta_{j+1}}u^{p-1}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}0&\alpha_{j}(-1)^{r+1}\\
\beta_{j}(-1)^{r+1}&0\end{bmatrix};

(iv)

if
k^{\prime}_{j}=k_{j}\in[\frac{r_{j}+2}{2},r_{j}-1]
then

\displaystyle M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{r},j}=\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{\Theta_{j}}{x_{j}}\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}u^{r-r_{j}+k_{j}}&\frac{\alpha_{j}}{\beta_{j}\Theta_{j+1}}\frac{(-1)^{r_{j}-1}u^{r-r_{j}+k_{j}-1}}{(2k_{j}-r_{j}-1)\binom{k_{j}-1}{r_{j}-k_{j}}^{2}}\\
0&u^{r-k_{j}}\end{bmatrix}

and

M_{\phi_{r},j}=\begin{bmatrix}1&p\Theta_{j+1}\frac{k_{j}(r_{j}-k_{j})}{2k_{j}-r_{j}}\\
\frac{2(r_{j}-k_{j})(k_{j}-1)+r_{j}-1}{\Theta_{j+1}}u^{p-1}+\frac{b_{r_{j},k_{j}}(-1)}{p\Theta_{j+1}x_{j}}&1\end{bmatrix}\\
\times\begin{bmatrix}0&\alpha_{j}\frac{(-1)^{r-r_{j}}}{\binom{k_{j}-1}{r_{j}-k_{j}}}\\
\beta_{j}(-1)^{r+1}\binom{k_{j}-1}{r_{j}-k_{j}}&0\end{bmatrix}

where
b_{r_{j},k_{j}}(-1)=-\frac{2m_{j}^{2}+2m_{j}+1}{m_{j}^{2}(m_{j}+1)^{2}}
if
(r_{j},k_{j})=(2m_{j}+1,m_{j}+2)
and
b_{r_{j},k_{j}}(-1)=0
otherwise;

(v)

if
k^{\prime}_{j}=r_{j}
then

\displaystyle M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{r},j}=\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{\Theta_{j}}{x_{j}}\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}u^{r}&\frac{\alpha_{j}}{\beta_{j}\Theta_{j+1}}\frac{(-1)^{r_{j}-1}u^{r-1}}{r_{j}-1}\\
0&u^{r-r_{j}}\end{bmatrix}

and

\displaystyle M_{\phi_{r},j}=\begin{bmatrix}1&-\frac{\alpha_{j}}{\beta_{j}}\frac{p^{r_{j}}x_{j}}{\Theta_{j}}\\
\frac{r_{j}-1}{\Theta_{j+1}}u^{p-1}+\frac{b_{r_{j},r_{j}}(-1)}{p\Theta_{j+1}x_{j}}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}0&\alpha_{j}(-1)^{r-r_{j}}\\
\beta_{j}(-1)^{r+1}&0\end{bmatrix}

where
b_{r_{j},r_{j}}(-1)=-\frac{5}{4}
if
r_{j}=3
and
b_{r_{j},r_{j}}(-1)=0
otherwise.

Proof.

By the same reason as Proposition
5.3.4
, we get the description as in the statement. More precise reference details are provided in the following.

(i)

Let
k^{\prime}_{j}=k_{j}\in[\frac{1}{2},\frac{r_{j}-1}{2}]
. Then
M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{r},j}
is computed in Proposition
4.2.4
, where
a_{k_{j}}=(-1)^{r_{j}}(r_{j}-2k_{j}+1)\binom{r_{j}-k_{j}}{k_{j}-1}^{2}
by Lemma
3.3.2
(i) and (ii) and
\frac{1}{p^{r_{j}-2k_{j}-1}\Theta_{j}}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. Also,
M_{\phi_{r},j}
is given by Proposition
4.3.2
, where
P_{k_{j},0}(-1,0)=(-1)^{r_{j}}k_{j}\binom{r_{j}-k_{j}}{k_{j}}
and
P_{k_{j},k_{j}}(-1,0)=\binom{r_{j}-k_{j}-1}{k_{j}}^{-1}
by Lemma
3.3.2
(i) and (ii),
\delta_{k_{j}+1}(-1)-\delta_{k_{j}}(-1)=\frac{2k_{j}-r_{j}}{k_{j}(r_{j}-k_{j})}
by Lemma
3.3.4
(i), and
\dot{\delta}_{k_{j}}(-1)=2(r_{j}-k_{j})(k_{j}-1)+r_{j}-1
by Lemma
3.3.7
(iii).

(ii)

Let
k^{\prime}_{j}=m_{j}
with
r_{j}=2m_{j}
. Then
M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{r},j}
is computed in Proposition
4.2.9
, where
a_{m_{j}}=m_{j}^{2}
by Lemma
3.3.2
(i) and (ii). Also,
M_{\phi_{r},j}
is given by Proposition
4.3.3
, where
P_{m_{j},0}(-1,0)=m_{j}
by Lemma
3.3.2
(i),
P_{m_{j},m_{j}}(-1,0)=-m_{j}(H_{m_{j}}+H_{m_{j}-1})
by Lemma
3.3.5
, and
\dot{\delta}_{m_{j}}(-1)=2m_{j}^{2}-1
by Lemma
3.3.7
(iii).

(iii)

Let
k^{\prime}_{j}=m_{j}+1
with
r_{j}=2m_{j}+1
. Then
M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{r},j}
is computed in Proposition
4.2.13
, where
\frac{p^{2(m_{j}+1)-r_{j}+1}x_{j}^{2}}{\Theta_{j}}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. Also,
M_{\phi_{r},j}
is given by Proposition
4.3.7
, where
P_{r_{j}-m_{j}-1,r_{j}-m_{j}-1}(-1,0)=1
and
P_{r_{j}-m_{j}-1,0}(-1,0)=-m_{j}(m_{j}+1)
by Lemma
3.3.2
(i) and (ii),
\delta_{r_{j}-m_{j}-1}(-1)-\delta_{m_{j}+1}(-1,x_{j})=\delta_{m_{j}}(-1)-\delta_{m_{j}+1}(-1)=\frac{1}{m_{j}(m_{j}+1)}
by Lemma
3.3.4
(i), and
\dot{\delta}_{r_{j}-m_{j}}(-1)=\dot{\delta}_{m_{j}+1}(-1)=2m_{j}(m_{j}+1)
by Lemma
3.3.7
(iii).

(iv)

Let
k^{\prime}_{j}=k_{j}\in[\frac{r_{j}+2}{2},r_{j}-1]
. Then
M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{r},j}
is computed in Proposition
4.2.13
, where
\frac{1}{a_{r_{j}-k_{j}+1}}=\frac{(-1)^{r_{j}}}{(2k_{j}-r_{j}-1)\binom{k_{j}-1}{r_{j}-k_{j}}^{2}}
by Lemma
3.3.2
(i) and (ii) and
\frac{p^{2k_{j}-r_{j}+1}x_{j}^{2}}{\Theta_{j}}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. If
(r_{j};k_{j})=(2m_{j};m_{j}+1)
, then the form of
M_{\phi_{r},j}
is given by Proposition
4.3.7
where
\delta_{r_{j}-m_{j}-1}(-1)-\delta_{m_{j}+1}(-1,x_{j})=\delta_{m_{j}-1}(-1)-\delta_{m_{j}}(-1)
in
\mathbf{F}
by Lemma
3.3.4
(iii). Otherwise, the form of
M_{\phi_{r},j}
is given by Proposition
4.3.10
. Here, we have
P_{r_{j}-k_{j},r_{j}-k_{j}}(-1,0)=\binom{k_{j}-1}{r_{j}-k_{j}}^{-1}
and
P_{r_{j}-k_{j},0}(-1,0)=(-1)^{r_{j}}(r_{j}-k_{j})\binom{k_{j}}{r_{j}-k_{j}}
by Lemma
3.3.2
(i) and (ii),
\delta_{r_{j}-k_{j}}(-1)-\delta_{r_{j}-k_{j}+1}(-1)=\frac{2k_{j}-r_{j}}{k_{j}(r_{j}-k_{j})}
by Lemma
3.3.4
(i),
\dot{\delta}_{r_{j}-k_{j}}(-1)=2(r_{j}-k_{j}+1)k_{j}+r_{j}-1
by Lemma
3.3.7
(iii), and
b_{2m_{j}+1,m_{j}+2}(-1)=-\frac{2m_{j}^{2}+2m_{j}+1}{m_{j}^{2}(m_{j}+1)^{2}}
by Lemma
3.3.11
.

(v)

Let
k^{\prime}_{j}=r_{j}
. Then
M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{r},j}
is computed in Proposition
4.2.21
. Also,
M_{\phi_{r},j}
is given by Proposition
4.3.13
, where
b_{3,3}(-1)=-\frac{5}{4}
by Lemma
3.3.11
and
\dot{\delta}_{1}(-1)=r_{j}-1
for
r_{j}\geq 3
and
\dot{\delta}_{2}(-1,x)=\dot{\delta}_{1}(-1)=1
for
r_{j}=2
by Lemma
3.3.7
(iii) and (iv).

This completes the proof.
∎

5.3.3.
Description of
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathcal{M}^{\diamond}e_{j}
and
\phi_{r}^{\diamond,(j)}
for exceptional cases

In this section of paragraph, we describe the matrices
M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{r},j}
and
M_{\phi_{r},j}
when either
r_{j}=1
or
k^{\prime}_{j}=\infty
.

Proposition 5.3.6
.

Assume that
r_{j}=1
. The matrices representing
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathcal{M}^{\diamond}e_{j}
and
\phi_{r}^{\diamond,(j)}
are given as follow:

(i)

if
k^{\prime}_{j}=\frac{1}{2}
then

\displaystyle M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{r},j}=\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
\frac{x_{j}}{\Theta_{j}}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}u^{r-1}&0\\
0&u^{r}\end{bmatrix}

and

\displaystyle M_{\phi_{r},j}=\begin{bmatrix}\alpha_{j}(-1)^{r-1}&0\\
0&\beta_{j}(-1)^{r}\end{bmatrix};

(ii)

if
k^{\prime}_{j}=1
then

\displaystyle M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{r},j}=\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{\Theta_{j}}{x_{j}}\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}u^{r}&0\\
0&u^{r-1}\end{bmatrix}

and

\displaystyle M_{\phi_{r},j}=\begin{bmatrix}1&-\frac{\alpha_{j}}{\beta_{j}}\frac{px_{j}}{\Theta_{j}}\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}0&\alpha_{j}(-1)^{r-1}\\
\beta_{j}(-1)^{r-1}&0\end{bmatrix};

(iii)

if
k^{\prime}_{j}=\frac{3}{2}
then

\displaystyle M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{r},j}=\begin{bmatrix}u^{r}&0\\
0&u^{r-1}\end{bmatrix}

and

\displaystyle M_{\phi_{r},j}=\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{\alpha_{j}}{\beta_{j}}\frac{\Theta_{j}}{px_{j}}\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\alpha_{j}(-1)^{r}&0\\
0&\beta_{j}(-1)^{r-1}\end{bmatrix}.

Proof.

By the same reason as Proposition
5.3.4
, we get the description as in the statement. More precisely,

(i)

if
k^{\prime}_{j}=\frac{1}{2}
, then
M_{\phi_{r},j}
is given by Proposition
4.4.3
;

(ii)

if
k^{\prime}_{j}=1
or
\frac{3}{2}
, then
M_{\phi_{r},j}
is given by Proposition
4.4.4
, and for
M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{r},j}
with
k^{\prime}_{j}=\frac{3}{2}
,
\frac{\Theta_{j}}{x_{j}}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
.

This completes the proof.
∎

Proposition 5.3.7
.

Assume that
r_{j}\geq 1
and
k^{\prime}_{j}=\infty
. The matrices representing
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathcal{M}^{\diamond}e_{j}
and
\phi_{r}^{\diamond,(j)}
are given by

\displaystyle M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{r},j}=\begin{bmatrix}u^{r}&0\\
0&u^{r-r_{j}}\end{bmatrix}

and

\displaystyle M_{\phi_{r},j}=\begin{bmatrix}\alpha_{j}(-1)^{r}&0\\
0&\beta_{j}(-1)^{r-r_{j}}\end{bmatrix}.

Proof.

By the same reason as Proposition
5.3.4
, we get the description as in the statement. More precisely,
M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{r},j}
and
M_{\phi_{r},j}
is given by Proposition
4.4.6
.
∎

6.
Applications

In this section, we apply our main results to give mod-
p
reduction of some
2
-dimensional semi-stable representations. We first show that we can recover the known results of the mod-
p
reduction in
[
BM
,
GP19
,
LP
]
. In fact, our method provides more strongly divisible modules when
r
is even, which are not discovered before. We also use the main results to compute the mod-
p
reduction of the semi-stable non-crystalline representations when
\vec{r}=(2,2)
and
\vec{r}=(1,5)
. We note that we do not try to compute the unramified characters due to the size of the paper.

Recall that if
v_{p}(\vec{x})\in R^{\prime}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
then the association
\vec{x}\mapsto\vec{\mathfrak{L}}
depends on
\vec{k}^{\prime}
(see (
5.2.8
)). In other words,
\vec{\mathfrak{L}}
is determined by the equation (
5.1.16
), and
\vec{\Delta}(-1)
depends on
l(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
(and so on
\vec{k}^{\prime}
) as explicitly described in (
5.2.2
). Hence, we will often write
\vec{x}^{l(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})}
for
\vec{x}
in
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
to distinguish
\vec{x}
in other cases, and its
j
-th coordinate will be written as
x^{l(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})}_{j}
. For instance, if
f=2
,
\vec{r}=(2,2)
, and
\vec{k}^{\prime}=(\frac{1}{2},1)
then we have
l(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})=(1,1)
and so we write
\vec{x}^{(1,1)}=(x^{(1,1)}_{0},x^{(1,1)}_{1})
for
\vec{x}=(x_{0},x_{1})
in this case. We also recall that we have defined the monodromy type of the Breuil modules in Definition
5.3.2
.

For each
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
in the following examples, we denote
\mathcal{M}
be the Breuil module obtained from
\mathfrak{M}=\mathfrak{M}_{(\vec{\theta}_{1},\vec{\theta}_{2},\vec{\varrho},\vec{\Delta})}
, which is given by Theorem
5.2.9
. Also, we denote
\overline{\rho}=\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{st}}^{\ast}(\mathcal{M})
for each example.

6.1.
Recovering the case
f=1

For the case
f=1
, it is not difficult to see that Theorem
5.2.1
recovers the results on strongly divisible modules in
[
BM
,
GP19
,
LP
]
, which is the goal of this subsection. In fact, we even found strongly divisible modules that were not discovered in
[
GP19
]
. As
f=1
, we write
(\Lambda,\Theta,x)
for
(\Lambda_{0},\Theta_{0},x_{0})
and
(r,k^{\prime},l)
for
(r_{0},k^{\prime}_{0},l(r_{0};k^{\prime}_{0}))
. We also write
\Delta
for
\Delta^{(0)}
and
\mathrm{MT}
for
\mathrm{MT}_{0}
.

By (
5.1.19
), we have
\Lambda=\lambda
with
v_{p}(\lambda)=\frac{r-1}{2}
. Moreover, if
(r,l)\neq(2m,m+1)
we have
x=\frac{1}{p}(p\mathfrak{L}-\mathfrak{L}-\delta_{l}(-1))
, and if
(r,l)=(2m,m+1)
then
x
is a solution of the equation
x=\frac{1}{p}(p\mathfrak{L}-\mathfrak{L}-\delta_{m+1}(-1,x))
, where
\delta_{l}(-1,x)
is explicitly written in (
5.2.2
).

6.1.1.
The case
r=1

If
r=1
then it is easy to see from Proposition
5.1.25
that only the cases
k^{\prime}=\frac{1}{2}
and
k^{\prime}=1
survive as in Table
2
.

and Proposition

, the case

\overline{\rho}

\mathrm{MT}=0
. Note that this case coincides with
\mbox{Case}_{\phi}(1;1)
.

, and we summarize the non-split mod-

For
r=2

We set
\xi:=\frac{1}{p}(p\mathfrak{L}-\mathfrak{L}+1)
, which is the same as
x^{(1)}
. We also set
\xi^{\prime}:=x^{(2)}
, which satisfies the equation
p\xi^{\prime}=p\mathfrak{L}-\mathfrak{L}+1-\frac{1}{\xi^{\prime}+1}=p\xi-\frac{1}{\xi^{\prime}+1}
. Note that the existence of
\xi^{\prime}

\mathfrak{L}

\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}}
as follow:

\circ

Assume
v_{p}(\xi)\geq-\frac{1}{2}
.

–

We first consider
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(2;1)
. In this case it is easy to see that
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}}^{ss}\cong\omega\oplus\omega
with
\mathrm{MT}=0
, by looking at the Breuil modules. By tedious computation on
\Mat(\phi_{2})
, one can see that
\overline{\rho}
is non-split if and only if
v_{p}(p\xi^{2}-4)>0
.

–

We now consider
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(2;\frac{3}{2})
.
Note that the strongly divisible modules in this case was not discovered before.
Assuming
E
is large enough, one can see that there exists
\xi^{\prime}
with
v_{p}(\xi^{\prime})=-\frac{1}{2}
such that
p\xi^{\prime}=p\xi-\frac{1}{\xi^{\prime}+1}
. It is also easy to see that
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}}^{ss}\cong\omega\oplus\omega
, by looking at the Breuil modules, and we can further sharpen the extension class as follow:

*

if
v_{p}(\Theta)=-\frac{1}{2}
, then we have
\mathrm{MT}=0
, and
\overline{\rho}
is non-split if and only if
v_{p}(p\xi^{2}-4)>0
. Note that this case coincides with
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(2;1)
.

*

if
v_{p}(\Theta)\in(-\frac{1}{2},0)
, we have
\mathrm{MT}=0
and one can see that
\overline{\rho}
is split.

*

if
v_{p}(\Theta)=0
, we have
\mathrm{MT}=1
and so
\overline{\rho}
is non-split. Note that this case coincides with
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(2;2)
when
v_{p}(\xi^{\prime})=-\frac{1}{2}
.

\circ

Assume
v_{p}(\xi)<-\frac{1}{2}
. In this case we consider
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(2;2)
, and one can prove that there exists
\xi^{\prime}\in E
with
v_{p}(\xi)=v_{p}(\xi^{\prime})
satisfying
p\xi^{\prime}=p\xi-\frac{1}{\xi^{\prime}+1}
. It is easy to see that
\overline{\rho}
is irreducible satisfying
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}}\cong\omega_{2}^{2}\oplus\omega_{2}^{2p}
.

Finally, we point out that
v_{p}(\xi)\geq-\frac{1}{2}
if and only if

, and we summarize the non-split mod-
p
reduction when
r=2
in the table, Table
5
.

\xi

\circ

Assume
v_{p}(\xi)=m-k-\frac{1}{2}
for
m+2\leq k\leq r-1
. Equivalently, we have
v_{p}(x^{(r-k+1)})=v_{p}(x^{(r-k)})=m-k-\frac{1}{2}
. In this case we consider
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(r;k+\frac{1}{2})
, and there are two non-homothetic lattices whose mod-
p
reductions are non-split and reducible.

–

If
v_{p}(\Theta)=-1
, then it is easy to see that
\overline{\rho}
is non-split and
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}}\cong\tiny\begin{bmatrix}\omega^{k}&*\\
0&\omega^{r-k}\end{bmatrix}
with
\mathrm{MT}=0
. Note that this case coincides with
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(r;k)
when
v_{p}(x^{(r-k+1)})=m-k-\frac{1}{2}
.

–

If
v_{p}(\Theta)=0
, then it is easy to see that
\overline{\rho}
is non-split with
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}}\cong\tiny\begin{bmatrix}\omega^{r-k}&*\\
0&\omega^{k}\end{bmatrix}
and
\mathrm{MT}=1
. Note that this case coincides with
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(r;k+1)
when
v_{p}(x^{(r-k)})=m-k-\frac{1}{2}
.

–

If
v_{p}(\Theta)\in(-1,0)
, then
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}}\cong\omega^{k}\oplus\omega^{r-k}
. In particular, it is split.

\circ

Assume
v_{p}(\xi)<-m+\frac{1}{2}
. Equivalently, we have
v_{p}(x^{(1)})<-m+\frac{1}{2}
. In this case it is easy to see that
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}}\cong\omega_{2}^{r}\oplus\omega_{2}^{rp}
, by looking at the Breuil modules.

Finally, we summarize the non-split mod-
p
reduction when
r=2m\geq 4
in the table, Table
9
.

6.2.
An example of the case
f=2
:
\vec{r}=(2,2)

In this subsection, we apply our main result, Theorem
5.2.9
, to compute the mod-
p
reduction of
2
-dimensional semi-stable representations of
G_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}
with parallel Hodge–Tate weights
(0,2)
. We set
\vec{r}=(2,2)
and
r=2
. We assume that
2<p-1
.

We first need to compute the valid
\vec{k}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}(\vec{r})
, and it is immediate that
\mathcal{J}_{\infty}(\vec{k}^{\prime})=\emptyset
for all
\vec{k}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}(\vec{r})
as
\vec{r}
is parallel. Recall that we say that
\vec{k}^{\prime}
is valid if
R(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})\neq\emptyset
, i.e., the equations and inequalities have common solutions. It turns out that it is enough to consider those
\vec{k}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}(\vec{r})
such that
R_{int}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})\neq\emptyset
, and such
\vec{k}^{\prime}
is listed as follow:

(\tfrac{1}{2},1),(\tfrac{1}{2},2),(1,\tfrac{1}{2}),(1,1),(1,\tfrac{3}{2}),(\tfrac{3}{2},1),(\tfrac{3}{2},2),(2,\tfrac{1}{2}),(2,\tfrac{3}{2}).

Moreover, we describe the areal support
R(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
for each such
\vec{k}^{\prime}
in the table, Table

.

(6.2.1)

. We point out that the pairs of (half) integers in the figure indicate

For the rest of this subsection, we compute the mod-
p
reduction of semi-stable representations in each
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
for
\vec{k}^{\prime}\in J(\vec{r})
. Then we summarize the mod-

reduction when

at the end of this subsection, §
6.2.4
.

6.2.1.
For
\vec{k}^{\prime}=(\frac{1}{2},2)
or
\vec{k}^{\prime}=(2,\frac{1}{2})

We only consider the case
\vec{k}^{\prime}=(\tfrac{1}{2},2)
, as the case
\vec{k}^{\prime}=(2,\frac{1}{2})
is symmetric. In this section of paragraph, by
\vec{k}^{\prime}
we always mean
(\frac{1}{2},2)
.

In this case, we have
\vec{x}=\vec{x}^{(1,2)}
, and we will write
\mathcal{M}
for the Breuil modules. From the results in §
5.3
, the Breuil module
\mathcal{M}
is determined by the following data:

\circ

M_{N,0}=\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
\frac{1-u^{p-1}}{\Theta_{0}}&0\end{bmatrix}\quad\&\quad M_{N,1}=\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
\frac{1-u^{p-1}}{\Theta_{1}}&0\end{bmatrix}
;

\circ

M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{2},0}=\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
\frac{x_{0}}{\Theta_{0}}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
\frac{\alpha_{0}u}{\beta_{0}\Theta_{1}}&u^{2}\end{bmatrix}\quad\&\quad M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{2},1}=\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{\Theta_{1}}{x_{1}}\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}u^{2}&-\frac{\alpha_{1}u}{\beta_{1}\Theta_{0}}\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}
;

\circ

M_{\phi_{2},0}=\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
\frac{u^{p-1}}{\Theta_{1}}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\alpha_{0}&0\\
0&\beta_{0}\end{bmatrix}\quad\&\quad M_{\phi_{2},1}=\begin{bmatrix}1&-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\beta_{1}}\frac{p^{2}x_{1}}{\Theta_{1}}\\
\frac{u^{p-1}}{\Theta_{0}}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}0&\alpha_{1}\\
-\beta_{1}&0\end{bmatrix}.

We further have the identities
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})=v_{p}(x_{1})
and
v_{p}(\Theta_{1})=1+v_{p}(x_{1})
, and so we conclude that
\frac{1}{\Theta_{0}}=\frac{\Theta_{1}}{x_{1}}=\frac{p^{2}x_{1}}{\Theta_{1}}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. We will divide
R(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
into the following four areas, and in each area we will determine the mod-
p
reduction:

(i)

\vec{t}\in R_{int}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
: It is easy to see that
\mathcal{M}\cong\mathcal{M}_{1}((0,2),(2,0);(\alpha_{0},\alpha_{1}),(\beta_{0},-\beta_{1}))
, and so
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{4}^{2+2p}\oplus\omega_{4}^{2p^{2}+2p^{3}}
by Lemma
2.3.7
(ii).

(ii)

t_{1}=-1
,
t_{0}>-1
: We have
\frac{1}{\Theta_{1}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
and
\frac{x_{0}}{\Theta_{0}}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. In particular, the monodromy type is
(0,1)
. Put
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{1}^{(0)},\overline{E}_{2}^{(1)})\subset\mathcal{M}
. It is easy to see that
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(1,0;-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{\Theta_{1}},\alpha_{1})
and
\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(1,2;\beta_{0}\Theta_{1},-\beta_{1})
, and so we conclude that
\overline{\rho}^{ss}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{2}^{1+2p}\oplus\omega_{2}
, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i), and
\overline{\rho}
is non-split as the monodromy type is nonzero.

(iii)

t_{0}=t_{1}=-1
: We have
\frac{1}{\Theta_{1}},\frac{x_{0}}{\Theta_{0}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
, and so in particular its monodromy type is
(0,1)
. Put
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{1}^{(0)},\overline{E}_{2}^{(1)})\subseteq\mathcal{M}
. It is easy to see that
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(2,0;-\frac{\beta_{0}x_{0}}{\Theta_{0}},\alpha_{1})
and
\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(0,2;\frac{\alpha_{0}\Theta_{0}}{x_{0}},-\beta_{1})
, and so we conclude that
\overline{\rho}^{ss}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{2}^{2p}\oplus\omega_{2}^{2}
, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i), and
\overline{\rho}
is non-split as the monodromy type is nonzero.

(iv)

t_{0}-t_{1}=0
,
t_{0}<-1
: We have
\frac{x_{0}}{\Theta_{0}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
and
\frac{1}{\Theta_{1}}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
, and so in particular its monodromy type is
(0,0)
. Put
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{M}
as in (iii). Using the same isomorphisms as in (iii) we have
\overline{\rho}^{ss}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{2}^{2p}\oplus\omega_{2}^{2}
, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i). It is not difficult to show that the short exact sequence determined by
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}
is non-split.

We summarize the mod-
p
reduction for
\vec{k}^{\prime}=(\frac{1}{2},2)
in the first table of Table
11
. Moreover, the case
\vec{k}^{\prime}=(2,\frac{1}{2})
is summarized in the second table. Note that by
\ast
in the following tables we mean a non-split extension.

6.2.2.
For
\vec{k}^{\prime}=(1,1)

In this section of paragraph, by
\vec{k}^{\prime}
we always mean
(1,1)
. In this case, we have
\vec{x}=\vec{x}^{(1,1)}
, and we will write
\mathcal{M}
for the Breuil modules. From the results in §
5.3
, the Breuil modules
\mathcal{M}
is determined by the following data:

\circ

M_{N,0}=\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
\frac{1-u^{p-1}}{\Theta_{0}}&0\end{bmatrix}\quad\&\quad M_{N,1}=\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
\frac{1-u^{p-1}}{\Theta_{1}}&0\end{bmatrix}
;

\circ

M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{2},0}=\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
\frac{x_{0}}{\Theta_{0}}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}u&\frac{\alpha_{0}}{\beta_{0}\Theta_{1}}\\
0&u\end{bmatrix}\quad\&\quad M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{2},1}=\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
\frac{x_{1}}{\Theta_{1}}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}u&\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\beta_{1}\Theta_{0}}\\
0&u\end{bmatrix}
;

\circ

M_{\phi_{2},0}=\begin{bmatrix}1&-p\Theta_{1}\\
\frac{u^{p-1}}{\Theta_{1}}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}0&\alpha_{0}\\
-\beta_{0}&0\end{bmatrix}\quad\&\quad M_{\phi_{2},1}=\begin{bmatrix}1&-p\Theta_{0}\\
\frac{u^{p-1}}{\Theta_{0}}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}0&\alpha_{1}\\
-\beta_{1}&0\end{bmatrix}.

Note that one can choose any
\vec{\Theta}
satisfying

\max\{-1,-1-v_{p}(x_{1})\}\leq v_{p}(\Theta_{0})=1-v_{p}(\Theta_{1})\leq\min\{0,v_{p}(x_{0})\}.

We will divide
R(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
into following four areas, and in each area we will determine the mod-
p
reduction:

(i)

\vec{t}\in R_{int}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
: Note that we have either
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})\not\in\{0,v_{p}(x_{0})\}
or
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})\not\in\{-1,-1-v_{p}(x_{1})\}
, in this case. Consider two
\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}
-submodules
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{1}^{(0)},\overline{E}_{2}^{(1)})
and
\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{2}^{(0)},\overline{E}_{1}^{(1)})
of
\mathcal{M}
. Then one can observe that

\circ

if
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})\not\in\{0,v_{p}(x_{0})\}
, then
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
is a Breuil submodule of
\mathcal{M}
such that
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(1,1;-\beta_{0},\alpha_{1})
and
\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(1,1;\alpha_{0},-\beta_{1})
;

\circ

if
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})\not\in\{-1,-1-v_{p}(x_{1})\}
, then
\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}
is a Breuil submodule of
\mathcal{M}
such that
\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(1,1;\alpha_{0},-\beta_{1})
and
\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(1,1;-\beta_{0},\alpha_{1})
.

In particular, if
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})\not\in\{-1,0,v_{p}(x_{0}),-1-v_{p}(x_{1})\}
, then
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\oplus\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}
as Breuil modules. Otherwise, It is not difficult to show that the short exact sequence determined by
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}
or
\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}
is non-split. So,
\overline{\rho}^{ss}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{2}^{1+p}\oplus\omega_{2}^{1+p}
, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i). Furthermore, the monodromy type is given as follow.

(a)

If
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})=0
, then
t_{0}\geq 0
, and the monodromy type is
(1,0)
.

(b)

If
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})=-1
, then
t_{1}\geq 0
, and the monodromy type is
(0,1)
.

(c)

Otherwise, the monodromy type is
(0,0)
.

(ii)

t_{1}=-1
,
t_{0}\geq 0
: We have
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})=0=-1-v_{p}(x_{1})
, and so
\frac{1}{\Theta_{0}},\frac{x_{1}}{\Theta_{1}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
and
\frac{1}{\Theta_{1}}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. In particular, the monodromy type is
(1,0)
. Put
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{2}^{(0)},\overline{E}_{1}^{(1)})
. It is easy to see that
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(1,2;\alpha_{0},-px_{1}\beta_{1})
and
\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(1,0;-\beta_{0},\frac{\alpha_{1}}{px_{1}})
, and so we conclude that
\overline{\rho}^{ss}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{2}\oplus\omega_{2}^{1+2p}
, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i), and
\overline{\rho}
is non-split as the monodromy type is nonzero.

(iii)

t_{0}+t_{1}=-1
,
-1<t_{0}<0
: We have
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})=v_{p}(x_{0})=-1-v_{p}(x_{1})\not\in\{-1,0\}
, and so
\frac{x_{0}}{\Theta_{0}},\frac{x_{1}}{\Theta_{1}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
and
\frac{1}{\Theta_{0}}=\frac{1}{\Theta_{1}}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. In particular, the monodromy type is
(0,0)
. Put

\displaystyle A:=\begin{bmatrix}-\frac{x_{1}}{\Theta_{1}}\alpha_{1}&\alpha_{1}\\
-\beta_{1}&0\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}-\frac{x_{0}}{\Theta_{0}}\alpha_{0}&\alpha_{0}\\
-\beta_{0}&0\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\frac{x_{0}x_{1}}{\Theta_{0}\Theta_{1}}\alpha_{0}\alpha_{1}-\beta_{0}\alpha_{1}&-\frac{x_{1}}{\Theta_{1}}\alpha_{0}\alpha_{1}\\
\frac{x_{0}}{\Theta_{0}}\alpha_{0}\beta_{1}&-\alpha_{0}\beta_{1}\end{bmatrix}.

Under the base change

\underline{E}^{\prime(1)}=\underline{E}^{(1)}\begin{bmatrix}0&-\frac{1}{\beta_{1}}\\
\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}&-\frac{x_{1}}{\beta_{1}\Theta_{1}}\end{bmatrix},\,\underline{F}^{\prime(0)}=\underline{F}^{(0)}\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
-\frac{x_{0}}{\Theta_{0}}&1\end{bmatrix},\,\underline{F}^{\prime(1)}=\underline{F}^{(1)}\begin{bmatrix}0&-\frac{1}{\beta_{1}}\\
\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}&0\end{bmatrix},

we have

\circ

\Mat_{\underline{E}^{(0)},\underline{F}^{\prime(0)}}(\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathcal{M}^{(0)})=\Mat_{\underline{E}^{\prime(1)},\underline{F}^{\prime(1)}}(\mathrm{Fil}^{r}\mathcal{M}^{(1)})=uI_{2}
;

\circ

\Mat_{\underline{E}^{\prime(1)},\underline{F}^{\prime(0)}}(\phi_{2}^{(0)})=A\quad\&\quad\Mat_{\underline{E}^{(0)},\underline{F}^{\prime(1)}}(\phi_{2}^{(1)})=I_{2}
.

Note that we have
\frac{\alpha_{0}}{\beta_{0}}=\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\beta_{1}}=p\Theta_{0}\Theta_{1}
and
\alpha_{0}\alpha_{1}=\lambda^{2}\Theta_{0}\Theta_{1}
, and so one can describe the mod-
p
reduction as follow:

\circ

if
px_{0}x_{1}\neq 4
in
\mathbf{F}
, then
A
is diagonalizable. So, extending
\mathbf{F}
large enough, we have
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{2}^{1+p}\oplus\omega_{2}^{1+p}
by Lemma
2.3.7
(i).

\circ

if
px_{0}x_{1}=4
in
\mathbf{F}
, then
A
is similar to
\tiny\begin{bmatrix}\frac{\lambda^{2}}{p}&1\\
0&\frac{\lambda^{2}}{p}\end{bmatrix}

and so
\overline{\rho}
is non-split with
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}^{ss}\cong\omega_{2}^{1+p}\oplus\omega_{2}^{1+p}
by Lemma
2.3.7
(i).

(iv)

t_{0}=-1
,
t_{1}\geq 0
: We have
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})=-1=v_{p}(x_{0})
, and so
\frac{1}{\Theta_{1}},\frac{x_{0}}{\Theta_{0}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
and
\frac{1}{\Theta_{0}}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. In particular, the monodromy type is
(0,1)
. Put
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{1}^{(0)},\overline{E}_{2}^{(1)})
. It is easy to see that
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(2,1;-px_{0}\beta_{0},\alpha_{1})
and
\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(0,1;\frac{\alpha_{0}}{px_{0}},-\beta_{1})
, and so we conclude that
\overline{\rho}^{ss}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{2}^{p}\oplus\omega_{2}^{2+p}
, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i), and
\overline{\rho}
is non-split as the monodromy type is nonzero.

We summarize the mod-
p
reduction for
\vec{k}^{\prime}=(1,1)
in the following table, Table
12
. Note that by
\ast
in the following table we mean a non-split extension.

(t_{0},t_{1})

6.2.3.
For
\vec{k}^{\prime}=(\frac{3}{2},2)
or
\vec{k}^{\prime}=(2,\frac{3}{2})

We only consider the case
\vec{k}^{\prime}=(\tfrac{3}{2},2)
, as the case
\vec{k}^{\prime}=(2,\frac{3}{2})
is symmetric. In this section of paragraph, by
\vec{k}^{\prime}
we always mean
(\frac{3}{2},2)
.

In this case, we have
\vec{x}=\vec{x}^{(2,2)}
, and we will write
\mathcal{M}
for the Breuil modules. From the results in §
5.3
, the Breuil module
\mathcal{M}
is determined by the following data:

\circ

M_{N,0}=\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
\frac{1-u^{p-1}}{\Theta_{0}}&0\end{bmatrix}\quad\&\quad M_{N,1}=\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
\frac{1-u^{p-1}}{\Theta_{1}}&0\end{bmatrix}
;

\circ

M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{2},0}=\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{\Theta_{0}}{x_{0}}\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}u&0\\
-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{\beta_{0}\Theta_{1}}&u\end{bmatrix}\quad\&\quad M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{2},1}=\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{\Theta_{1}}{x_{1}}\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}u^{2}&-\frac{\alpha_{1}u}{\beta_{1}\Theta_{0}}\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}
;

\circ

M_{\phi_{2},0}=\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
\frac{u^{p-1}}{\Theta_{1}}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}-\alpha_{0}&0\\
0&-\beta_{0}\end{bmatrix}\quad\&\quad M_{\phi_{2},1}=\begin{bmatrix}1&-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\beta_{1}}\frac{p^{2}x_{1}}{\Theta_{1}}\\
\frac{u^{p-1}}{\Theta_{0}}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}0&\alpha_{1}\\
-\beta_{1}&0\end{bmatrix}.

We further have the identities
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})=1+v_{p}(x_{0})+v_{p}(x_{1})
and
v_{p}(\Theta_{1})=-v_{p}(x_{0})+v_{p}(x_{1})
, and so we conclude that
\frac{\Theta_{1}}{x_{1}}=\frac{p^{2}x_{1}}{\Theta_{1}}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. We will divide
R(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
into the following five areas, and in each area we will determine the mod-
p
reduction:

(i)

\vec{t}\in R_{int}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
: It is easy to see that
\mathcal{M}\cong\mathcal{M}_{1}((1,2),(1,0);(-\alpha_{0},-\beta_{0}),(\alpha_{1},\beta_{1}))
, and so we conclude that
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{4}^{1+2p+p^{2}}\oplus\omega_{4}^{1+p^{2}+2p^{3}}
by Lemma
2.3.7
(ii).

(ii)

t_{0}+t_{1}=-1
,
-\frac{1}{2}<t_{0}<0
: We have
\frac{1}{\Theta_{0}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
and
\frac{1}{\Theta_{1}}=\frac{\Theta_{0}}{x_{0}}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. In particular, the monodromy type is
(1,0)
. Put
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{2})\subset\mathcal{M}
. It is easy to see that
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(1,1;-\beta_{0},-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\Theta_{0}})
and
\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(1,1;-\alpha_{0},-\beta_{1}\Theta_{0})
, and so we conclude that
\overline{\rho}^{ss}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{2}^{1+p}\oplus\omega_{2}^{1+p}
, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i), and
\overline{\rho}
is non-split as the monodromy type is nonzero.

(iii)

t_{0}=t_{1}=-\frac{1}{2}
: We have
\frac{1}{\Theta_{0}},\frac{1}{\Theta_{1}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
and
\frac{\Theta_{0}}{x_{0}}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. In particular, the monodromy type is
(1,1)
. Put
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
as in (ii). Using the same isomorphisms as in (ii), we conclude that
\overline{\rho}^{ss}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{2}^{1+p}\oplus\omega_{2}^{1+p}
, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i), and
\overline{\rho}
is non-split as the monodromy type is nonzero.

(iv)

t_{0}-t_{1}=0
,
-1\leq t_{0}<-\frac{1}{2}
: We have
\frac{1}{\Theta_{1}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
and
\frac{1}{\Theta_{0}}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. In particular, the monodromy type is
(0,1)
. Put
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{1}^{(0)},\overline{E}_{2}^{(1)})\subset\mathcal{M}
. It is easy to see that
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(2,0;-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{\Theta_{1}},\alpha_{1})
and
\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(0,2;\beta_{0}\Theta_{1},-\beta_{1})
, and so we conclude that
\overline{\rho}^{ss}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{2}^{2p}\oplus\omega_{2}^{2}
, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i), and
\overline{\rho}
is non-split as the monodromy type is nonzero.

(v)

t_{1}=-1
,
-1<t_{0}<0
: We have
\frac{\Theta_{0}}{x_{0}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
and
\frac{1}{\Theta_{0}}=\frac{1}{\Theta_{1}}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. In particular, the monodromy type is
(0,0)
. Put
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{2}^{(0)},\overline{E}_{1}^{(1)}-\frac{\beta_{0}x_{0}}{\alpha_{0}\Theta_{0}}\overline{E}_{2}^{(1)})
. It is easy to see that
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(1,2;\frac{\alpha_{0}x_{0}}{\Theta_{0}},-\beta_{1})
and
\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(1,0;-\frac{\beta_{0}x_{0}}{\Theta_{0}},\alpha_{1})
, and so we conclude that
\overline{\rho}^{ss}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{2}\oplus\omega_{2}^{1+2p}
, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i). It is not difficult to show that the short exact sequence determined by
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}
is non-split.

6.2.4.
Summary on mod-
p
reduction when
\vec{r}=(2,2)

In this section of paragraph, we summarize the results on mod-
p
reduction when
\vec{r}=(2,2)
. We set
\vec{\xi}:=(\xi_{0},\xi_{1})\in E^{2}
where

\xi_{0}=\frac{1}{p}\left(p\mathfrak{L}_{0}-\mathfrak{L}_{1}+1\right)\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\xi_{1}=\frac{1}{p}(p\mathfrak{L}_{1}-\mathfrak{L}_{0}+1).

We first partition
E^{2}(=E^{2}_{\emptyset})
in terms of
\vec{\xi}
, such that within each subset the semi-simplification of
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}
remains constant, as follow:

\circ

S_{1}:=\{\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in E^{2}\mid v_{p}(\xi_{1})<-1,\ v_{p}(\xi_{0})>v_{p}(\xi_{1})\}
;

\circ

S_{2}:=\{\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in E^{2}\mid v_{p}(\xi_{0})<-1,\ v_{p}(\xi_{0})<v_{p}(\xi_{1})\}
;

\circ

S_{3}:=\{\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in E^{2}\mid v_{p}(\xi_{0})>-1,\ v_{p}(\xi_{1})>-1,v_{p}(\xi_{0})+v_{p}(\xi_{1})>-1\}
;

\circ

S_{4}:=\{\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in E^{2}\mid v_{p}(\xi_{0})+v_{p}(\xi_{1})<-1,\ v_{p}(\xi_{0})>v_{p}(\xi_{1})>-1\}
;

\circ

S_{5}:=\{\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in E^{2}\mid v_{p}(\xi_{0})+v_{p}(\xi_{1})<-1,\ v_{p}(\xi_{1})>v_{p}(\xi_{0})>-1\}
;

\circ

S_{6}:=\{\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in E^{2}\mid v_{p}(\xi_{1})=-1,\ v_{p}(\xi_{0})>-1\}
;

\circ

S_{7}:=\{\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in E^{2}\mid v_{p}(\xi_{0})=-1,\ v_{p}(\xi_{1})>-1\}
;

\circ

S_{8}:=\{\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in E^{2}\mid v_{p}(\xi_{0})+v_{p}(\xi_{1})=-1,\ -1<v_{p}(\xi_{0})<0\}
;

\circ

S_{9}:=\{\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in E^{2}\mid v_{p}(\xi_{0})=v_{p}(\xi_{1})<-\frac{1}{2}\}
.

It is not difficult to show that
S_{i}\cap S_{j}=\emptyset
if
i\neq j
and that
\cup_{i=1}^{9}S_{i}=E^{2}
. The following picture, Figure
2
, describes those sets
S_{1},\cdots,S_{9}
on
(v_{p}(\xi_{0}),v_{p}(\xi_{1}))
-plane, emphasizing the disjointness. These sets are carefully defined to describe the mod-
p
reduction on each set.

Figure 2.
Partition by
S_{1},\cdots,S_{9}
, displayed on
v_{p}(\vec{\xi})
-plane.

We further define subsets of
S_{3}
and
S_{8}
as follow:

\circ

S^{\prime}_{3}=\{\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in S_{3}\mid-1<v_{p}(\xi_{0}+1)-v_{p}(\xi_{1}+1)<1\}\subset S_{3}
;

\circ

S^{\prime}_{8}=\{\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in S_{8}\mid v_{p}(p\xi_{0}\xi_{1}-4)>0\}\subset S_{8}
.

Remark 6.2.2
.

One can extract the following results from Table
14
:

\circ

our method provides with at least one Galois stable lattice in each
2
-dimensional semi-stable non-crystalline representation of
G_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}
with parallel Hodge–Tate weights
(0,2)
, as
\cup_{i=1}^{9}S_{i}=E^{2}
;

\circ

if the mod-
p
reduction is an extension of two distinct characters then our method provides the two non-homothetic lattices (For instance, if
\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in S_{8}\setminus S_{8}^{\prime}
then there is one more lattice whose mod-
p
reduction is split);

\circ

if
\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in S_{3}^{\prime}
then three non-homothetic lattices are provided, as
S_{3}^{\prime}\subset S_{3}
. In particular, if
\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in S_{3}^{\prime}
then
\overline{\rho}
is an extension of the same characters.

For the rest of this subsection, we explain how one can induce Table
14
from the results in the previous sections of paragraph.
We start by noting the following identities
\vec{x}^{(1,1)}=(\xi_{0},\xi_{1})
,
\vec{x}^{(1,2)}=(\xi_{0},\xi_{1}-\frac{1}{p(\xi_{0}+1)})
, and
\vec{x}^{(2,1)}=(\xi_{0}-\frac{1}{p(\xi_{1}+1)},\xi_{1})
. Moreover, if we let
\vec{\xi}^{\prime}=(\xi^{\prime}_{0},\xi^{\prime}_{1})
for the solutions of the equation

(6.2.3)

\xi^{\prime}_{0}=\xi_{0}-\frac{1}{p(\xi^{\prime}_{1}+1)}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\xi^{\prime}_{1}=\xi_{1}-\frac{1}{p(\xi^{\prime}_{0}+1)},

then we have
\vec{\xi}^{\prime}=\vec{x}^{(2,2)}
, and we describe its necessary properties in the following lemma.

Lemma 6.2.4
.

Let
\vec{\xi}\in E^{2}
and let
\vec{\xi}^{\prime}
be a solution of the equations in (
6.2.3
).

(i)

If
v_{p}(\xi_{0})>-1
,
v_{p}(\xi_{1})>-1
and
v_{p}(\xi_{0})+v_{p}(\xi_{1})>-1
, then there exists a solution
\vec{\xi}^{\prime}\in E^{2}
whose valuation satisfies

v_{p}(\xi^{\prime}_{0})=\frac{v_{p}(\xi_{0}+1)-v_{p}(\xi_{1}+1)-1}{2}\quad\mbox{and}\quad v_{p}(\xi^{\prime}_{1})=\frac{v_{p}(\xi_{1}+1)-v_{p}(\xi_{0}+1)-1}{2}.

(ii)

If
-1<v_{p}(\xi_{0})<0
and
v_{p}(\xi_{0})+v_{p}(\xi_{1})=-1
, then there exits a solution
\vec{\xi}^{\prime}\in E^{2}
whose valuation satisfies
v_{p}(\vec{\xi}^{\prime})=v_{p}(\vec{\xi})
, assuming
E
is large enough.

(iii)

If
v_{p}(\xi_{0})\geq-1
,
v_{p}(\xi_{1})\geq-1
and
v_{p}(\xi_{0})+v_{p}(\xi_{1})<-1
, then there exists a unique solution
\vec{\xi}^{\prime}\in E^{2}
whose valuation satisfies
v_{p}(\vec{\xi}^{\prime})=v_{p}(\vec{\xi})
.

Proof.

The equation of
\vec{\xi}^{\prime}
can be translated to

p(\xi^{\prime}_{0}+1)(\xi^{\prime}_{1}+1)+1=p(\xi_{0}+1)(\xi^{\prime}_{1}+1)=p(\xi^{\prime}_{0}+1)(\xi_{1}+1).

Put
X=p(\xi^{\prime}_{0}+1)(\xi^{\prime}_{1}+1)
and
A=p(\xi_{0}+1)(\xi_{1}+1)
, then one have
(X+1)^{2}=AX
and
\xi^{\prime}_{j}+1=\frac{X+1}{p(\xi_{j+1}+1)}
for
j=0,1
.

(i)

If
v_{p}(\xi_{0})>-1
,
v_{p}(\xi_{1})>-1
and
v_{p}(\xi_{0})+v_{p}(\xi_{1})>-1
, then
v_{p}(A)>0
. By Hensel’s Lemma, two solutions for
X
can be found in
-1+\mathfrak{m}
. For this
X
,
v_{p}(X+1)=\frac{1}{2}v_{p}(AX)=\frac{v_{p}(\xi_{0}+1)+v_{p}(\xi_{1}+1)+1}{2}
. This with
\xi^{\prime}_{j}+1=\frac{X+1}{p(\xi_{j+1}+1)}
gives the result.

(ii)

If
-1<v_{p}(\xi_{0})<0
and
v_{p}(\xi_{0})+v_{p}(\xi_{1})=-1
, then
v_{p}(A)=0
. Extending
E
large enough, two solutions for
X
can be found in
\mathcal{O}^{\times}
. For this
X
,
v_{p}(X+1)=\frac{1}{2}v_{p}(AX)=-\frac{1}{2}
, giving the desired result.

(iii)

If
v_{p}(\xi_{0})\geq-1
,
v_{p}(\xi_{1})\geq-1
and
v_{p}(\xi_{0})+v_{p}(\xi_{1})<-1
, then
v_{p}(A)>0
. By Hensel’s Lemma, two solutions
X=X_{1}
and
X=X_{2}
are found in
E
, where
v_{p}(X_{1})=v_{p}(A^{-1})
and
v_{p}(X_{2})=v_{p}(A)
. This gives
v_{p}(X_{1}+1)=0
and
v_{p}(X_{2}+1)=v_{p}(A)
. Now it is easy to see that only
\vec{\xi}^{\prime}
from
X_{2}
can satisfy the condition
v_{p}(\xi_{0})\geq-1
,
v_{p}(\xi_{1})\geq-1
and
v_{p}(\xi_{0})+v_{p}(\xi_{1})<-1
, and moreover
v_{p}(\vec{\xi}^{\prime})=v_{p}(\vec{\xi})
.

This completes the proof.
∎

We are now ready to verify how one can induce Table
14
.

\circ

Assume
\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in S_{1}
. Equivalently, we have
v_{p}(x_{0}^{(1,2)})<-1
and
v_{p}(x_{0}^{(1,2)})>v_{p}(x_{1}^{(1,2)})
. Then we have
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{4}^{2+2p}\oplus\omega_{4}^{2p^{2}+2p^{3}}
from
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};(\frac{1}{2},2))
(see §
6.2.1
(i)).

\circ

Assume
\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in S_{2}
. Equivalently, we have
v_{p}(x_{1}^{(2,1)})<-1
and
v_{p}(x_{0}^{(2,1)})<v_{p}(x_{1}^{(2,1)})
. Then we have
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{4}^{2p+2p^{2}}\oplus\omega_{4}^{2+2p^{3}}
from
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};(\frac{1}{2},2))
(see §
6.2.1
(i), as
S_{2}=\varphi(S_{1})
).

\circ

Assume
\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in S_{3}
, i.e,
v_{p}(\xi_{0})>-1
,
v_{p}(\xi_{1})>-1
, and
v_{p}(\xi_{0})+v_{p}(\xi_{1})>-1
. In this case there are two non-homothetic lattices whose mod-
p
reductions are non-split and reducible.

–

We first consider the lattice from
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})\in\{0,v_{p}(\xi_{0})\}
of
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};(1,1))
(see §
6.2.2
(i)). This case gives
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\begin{bmatrix}\omega&*\\
0&\omega\end{bmatrix}
with
\vec{\mathrm{MT}}=(1,0)
if
v_{p}(\xi_{0})\geq 0
and
\vec{\mathrm{MT}}=(0,0)
otherwise.

–

We also consider the lattices from
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})\in\{-1,-1-v_{p}(\xi_{1})\}
of
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};(1,1))
(see §
6.2.2
(i)). This case gives
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\begin{bmatrix}\omega&*\\
0&\omega\end{bmatrix}
with
\vec{\mathrm{MT}}=(0,1)
if
v_{p}(\xi_{1})\geq 0
and
\vec{\mathrm{MT}}=(0,0)
otherwise.

Now we assume
\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in S^{\prime}_{3}\subset S_{3}
. By Lemma
6.2.4
(i), we have
v_{p}(\xi^{\prime}_{0})+v_{p}(\xi^{\prime}_{1})=-1
and
-1<v_{p}(\xi^{\prime}_{0})<0
. In this case, there are another lattice giving non-split mod-
p
reduction, which is different from the above two. For this lattice, we have
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\begin{bmatrix}\omega&*\\
0&\omega\end{bmatrix}
, with
\vec{\mathrm{MT}}=(0,1)
if
-1<v_{p}(\xi_{0}+1)-v_{p}(\xi_{1}+1)<0
,
\vec{\mathrm{MT}}=(1,1)
if
v_{p}(\xi_{0}+1)=v_{p}(\xi_{1}+1)
, and
\vec{\mathrm{MT}}=(1,0)
if
0<v_{p}(\xi_{0}+1)-v_{p}(\xi_{1}+1)<1
. This is from §
6.2.3
(ii) if
v_{p}(\xi^{\prime}_{0})\neq-\frac{1}{2}
(i.e.,
v_{p}(\xi_{0}+1)\neq v_{p}(\xi_{1}+1)
in
S^{\prime}_{3}
), and from §
6.2.3
(iii) if
v_{p}(\xi^{\prime}_{0})=-\frac{1}{2}
(i.e.,
v_{p}(\xi_{0}+1)=v_{p}(\xi_{1}+1)
in
S^{\prime}_{3}
).

\circ

Assume
\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in S_{4}
. Equivalently, we have
v_{p}(\xi^{\prime}_{0})+v_{p}(\xi^{\prime}_{1})<-1
,
v_{p}(\xi^{\prime}_{0})>v_{p}(\xi^{\prime}_{1})
and
v_{p}(\xi^{\prime}_{1})>0
by Lemma
6.2.4
(iii). Then
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{4}^{1+2p+p^{2}}\oplus\omega_{4}^{1+p^{2}+2p^{3}}
from
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};(\frac{3}{2},2))
(see §
6.2.3
(i)).

\circ

Assume
\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in S_{5}
. Equivalently, we have
v_{p}(\xi_{0})+v_{p}(\xi_{1})<-1
,
v_{p}(\xi_{0})<v_{p}(\xi_{1})
, and
v_{p}(\xi_{0})>0
by Lemma
6.2.4
. Then,
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{4}^{p+2p^{2}+p^{3}}\oplus\omega_{4}^{2+p+p^{3}}
from
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};(2,\frac{3}{2}))
(see §
6.2.3
(i), as
S_{5}=\varphi(S_{4})
).

\circ

Assume
\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in S_{6}
. Equivalently, we have
v_{p}(x_{1}^{(1,2)})=-1
and
v_{p}(x_{0}^{(1,2)})>-1
. Also, if
-1<v_{p}(\xi_{0})<0
, then
-1<v_{p}(\xi^{\prime}_{0})<0
and
v_{p}(\xi^{\prime}_{1})=-1
, by Lemma
6.2.4
(iii). In this case there are two non-homothetic lattices whose mod-
p
reductions are non-split and reducible.

–

We first consider
\overline{\rho}
from
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};(\frac{1}{2},2))
(see §
6.2.1
(ii)). In this case we have
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\begin{bmatrix}\omega_{2}&*\\
0&\omega_{2}^{1+2p}\end{bmatrix}
with
\vec{\mathrm{MT}}=(0,1)
.

–

We also consider
\overline{\rho}
from
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};(\frac{3}{2},2))
or
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};(1,1))
(see §
6.2.3
(v) if
-1<v_{p}(\xi_{0})<0
and from §
6.2.2
(ii) if
v_{p}(\xi_{0})\geq 0
). In this case we have
\overline{\rho}_{2}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\begin{bmatrix}\omega_{2}^{1+2p}&*\\
0&\omega_{2}\end{bmatrix}
with
\vec{\mathrm{MT}}=(0,0)
.

\circ

Assume
\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in S_{7}
. Equivalently, we have
v_{p}(x_{0}^{(2,1)})=-1
and
v_{p}(x_{1}^{(2,1)})>-1
. Also, if
-1<v_{p}(\xi_{1})<0
, then
-1<v_{p}(\xi^{\prime}_{1})<0
and
v_{p}(\xi^{\prime}_{0})=-1
, by Lemma
6.2.4
(iii). In this case there are two non-homothetic lattices whose mod-
p
reductions are non-split and reducible.

–

We first consider
\overline{\rho}
from
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};(2,\frac{1}{2}))
(see §
6.2.1
(ii), as
S_{7}=\varphi(S_{6})
). In this case we have
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\begin{bmatrix}\omega_{2}^{p}&*\\
0&\omega_{2}^{2+p}\end{bmatrix}
with
\vec{\mathrm{MT}}=(1,0)
.

–

We also consider
\overline{\rho}
from
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};(2,\frac{3}{2}))
or
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};(1,1))
(see §
6.2.3
(v) if
-1<v_{p}(\xi_{1})<0
and from §
6.2.2
(ii) if
v_{p}(\xi_{1})\geq 0
, as
S_{7}=\varphi(S_{6})
). In this case we have
\overline{\rho}_{2}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\begin{bmatrix}\omega_{2}^{2+p}&*\\
0&\omega_{2}^{p}\end{bmatrix}
with
\vec{\mathrm{MT}}=(0,0)
.

\circ

Assume
\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in S_{8}
. Extending
E
large enough, we have
-1<v_{p}(\xi^{\prime}_{0})<0
and
v_{p}(\xi^{\prime}_{0})+v_{p}(\xi^{\prime}_{1})=-1
, by Lemma
6.2.4
(ii). In this case, we consider either
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};(\frac{3}{2},2))
or
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};(2,\frac{3}{2}))
, which coincides if
v_{p}(\xi_{0})=-\frac{1}{2}
(see §
6.2.3
(ii)). It is shown that
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\begin{bmatrix}\omega&*\\
0&\omega\end{bmatrix}
with
\vec{\mathrm{MT}}=(0,1)
if
-1<v_{p}(\xi_{0})<-\frac{1}{2}
,
\vec{\mathrm{MT}}=(1,1)
if
v_{p}(\xi_{0})=-\frac{1}{2}
, and
\vec{\mathrm{MT}}=(1,0)
if
-\frac{1}{2}<v_{p}(\xi_{0})<0
.

Now we consider
\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in S^{\prime}_{8}\subset S_{8}
, consisting
\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in S_{8}
with
v_{p}(p\xi_{0}\xi_{1}-4)>0
. In this case, we also consider
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};(1,1))
to get another lattice giving non-split reducible mod-
p
reduction (see §
6.2.2
(iii)). It is shown that
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\begin{bmatrix}\omega&*\\
0&\omega\end{bmatrix}
with
\vec{\mathrm{MT}}=(0,0)
.

\circ

Assume
\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in S_{9}
. Equivalently, we have

–

v_{p}(x_{0}^{\vec{l}})=v_{p}(x_{1}^{\vec{l}})\leq 0
for
\vec{l}=(1,2)
and
(2,1)
, if
v_{p}(\xi_{0})\leq-1
;

–

-1\leq v_{p}(\xi^{\prime}_{0})=v_{p}(\xi^{\prime}_{1})<-\frac{1}{2}
, if
-1\leq v_{p}(\xi_{0})<-\frac{1}{2}
, by Lemma
6.2.4
.

In this case there are two non-homothetic lattices whose mod-
p
reductions are non-split and reducible.

–

We first consider
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};(\frac{1}{2},2))
or
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};(\frac{3}{2},2))
, which coincide if
v_{p}(\xi_{0})=-1
(see §
6.2.1
(iii)(iv) if
v_{p}(\xi_{0})\leq-1
and §
6.2.3
(iii),(iv) if
-1\leq v_{p}(\xi_{0})<-\frac{1}{2}
). It is shown that
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\begin{bmatrix}\omega_{2}^{2}&*\\
0&\omega_{2}^{2p}\end{bmatrix}
with
\vec{\mathrm{MT}}=(0,0)
if
v_{p}(\xi_{0})<-1
and
\vec{\mathrm{MT}}=(0,1)
if
-1\leq v_{p}(\xi_{0})<-\frac{1}{2}
.

–

We also consider
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};(2,\frac{1}{2}))
or
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};(2,\frac{3}{2}))
, which coincide if
v_{p}(\xi_{1})=-1
(see §
6.2.1
(iii)(iv) if
v_{p}(\xi_{1})\leq-1
and §
6.2.3
(iii),(iv) if
-1\leq v_{p}(\xi_{1})<-\frac{1}{2}
). It is shown that
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\begin{bmatrix}\omega_{2}^{2p}&*\\
0&\omega_{2}^{2}\end{bmatrix}
with
\vec{\mathrm{MT}}=(0,0)
if
v_{p}(\xi_{1})<-1
and
\vec{\mathrm{MT}}=(1,0)
if
-1\leq v_{p}(\xi_{1})<-\frac{1}{2}
.

6.3.
An example of the case
f=2
:
\vec{r}=(1,5)

In this subsection, we apply our main result, Theorem
5.2.9
, to compute the mod-
p
reduction of
2
-dimensional semi-stable representations of
G_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}
with
j
-labeled Hodge–Tate weights
(0,r_{j})
where
\vec{r}=(1,5)
. We set
r=5
and assume
5<p-1
.

We first need to compute the valid
\vec{k}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}(\vec{r})
, and it is immediate from (
5.2.4
) that
\mathcal{J}_{\infty}(\vec{k}^{\prime})\in\{\emptyset,\{0\}\}
for all
\vec{k}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}(\vec{r})
. Recall that we say that
\vec{k}^{\prime}
is valid if
R(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})\neq\emptyset
, i.e., the equations and inequalities have common solutions. Following the notation in (
5.2.4
), by
\mathcal{J}_{0}=\emptyset
(resp. by
\mathcal{J}_{0}=\{0\}
) we mean we will consider
\vec{k}^{\prime}
with
\mathcal{J}_{\infty}(\vec{k}^{\prime})=\emptyset
(resp. with
\mathcal{J}_{\infty}(\vec{k}^{\prime})=\{0\}
). It turns out that it is enough to consider those
\vec{k}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}(\vec{r})
such that
R_{int}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})\neq\emptyset
, and such
\vec{k}^{\prime}
is listed as follow:

\circ

if
\mathcal{J}_{\infty}(\vec{k}^{\prime})=\emptyset
then
\vec{k}^{\prime}
is one of the following

(\tfrac{1}{2},2),(\tfrac{1}{2},\tfrac{5}{2}),(\tfrac{1}{2},3),(\tfrac{1}{2},4),(\tfrac{1}{2},5),(1,\tfrac{3}{2}),(1,\tfrac{5}{2}),(1,\tfrac{7}{2}),(1,\tfrac{9}{2}),(\tfrac{3}{2},1),(\tfrac{3}{2},\tfrac{3}{2}),(\tfrac{3}{2},2),(\tfrac{3}{2},4),(\tfrac{3}{2},5);

\circ

if
\mathcal{J}_{\infty}(\vec{k}^{\prime})=\{0\}
then
\vec{k}^{\prime}
is one of the following

(\infty,1),(\infty,\tfrac{3}{2}),(\infty,2),(\infty,4),(\infty,5).

Moreover, we describe the areal support
R(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
for each such
\vec{k}^{\prime}
in the table, Table
15
.

We further note that

\circ

\Supp(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})\subset\Supp(\vec{r};(\frac{1}{2},\frac{5}{2}))
for
\vec{k}^{\prime}=(\frac{1}{2},2),(\frac{1}{2},3),(1,\frac{5}{2})
;

\circ

\Supp(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})\subset\Supp(\vec{r};(\frac{3}{2},\frac{3}{2}))
for
\vec{k}^{\prime}=(1,\frac{3}{2})
,
(\frac{3}{2},1)
,
(\frac{3}{2},2)
, and
(\frac{3}{2},4)
;

\circ

\Supp(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})\subset\Supp(\vec{r};(\infty,\frac{3}{2}))
for
\vec{k}^{\prime}=(\infty,1)
,
(\infty,2)
, and
(\infty,4)
.

Hence, we exclude these redundancies and consider only

(6.3.1)

\{(\tfrac{1}{2},\tfrac{5}{2}),(\tfrac{1}{2},4),(\tfrac{1}{2},5),(1,\tfrac{7}{2}),(1,\tfrac{9}{2}),(\tfrac{3}{2},\tfrac{3}{2}),(\tfrac{3}{2},5)\}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\{(\infty,\tfrac{3}{2}),(\infty,5)\}.

We will write
J(\vec{r};\emptyset)
(resp.
J(\vec{r};\{0\})
) for the set of
\vec{k}^{\prime}
with
\mathcal{J}_{\infty}(\vec{k}^{\prime})=\emptyset
(resp. with
\mathcal{J}_{\infty}(\vec{k}^{\prime})=\{0\}
) listed in (
6.3.1
). Moreover, we let
J(\vec{r}):=J(\vec{r};\emptyset)\cup J(\vec{r};\{0\})
.

Each areal support
R(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
for
\vec{k}^{\prime}\in J(\vec{r})
is depicted in Figure
3
. We point out that the pairs of (half) integers in the figure indicate
\vec{k}^{\prime}\in J(\vec{r})
, and that each polygon indicate the areal support
R(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
.

Figure 3.
R(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
for
\vec{r}=(1,5)
and
\vec{k}^{\prime}\in J(\vec{r})

For the rest of this subsection, we compute the mod-
p
reduction of semi-stable representations in each
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
for
\vec{k}^{\prime}\in J(\vec{r})
. Then we summarize the mod-
p
reduction when
\vec{r}=(1,5)
at the end of this subsection, §
6.3.10
.

6.3.1.
For
\vec{k}^{\prime}=(\frac{1}{2},\frac{5}{2})

In this section of paragraph, by
\vec{k}^{\prime}
we always mean
(\frac{1}{2},\frac{5}{2})
. In this case, we have
\vec{x}=\vec{x}^{(1,3)}
, and we will write
\mathcal{M}
for the Breuil modules. From the results in §
5.3
, the Breuil modules
\mathcal{M}
is determined by the following data:

\circ

M_{N,0}=\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
\frac{1-12u^{p-1}}{\Theta_{0}}&0\end{bmatrix}\quad\&\quad M_{N,1}=\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
\frac{1}{\Theta_{1}}&0\end{bmatrix}
;

\circ

M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{5},0}=\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
\frac{x_{0}}{\Theta_{0}}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}u^{4}&0\\
0&u^{5}\end{bmatrix}\quad\&\quad M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{5},1}=\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
\frac{x_{1}}{\Theta_{1}}+\frac{3\alpha_{1}}{\beta_{1}\Theta_{0}}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}u^{2}&0\\
0&u^{3}\end{bmatrix}
;

\circ

M_{\phi_{5},0}=\begin{bmatrix}\alpha_{0}&0\\
0&-\beta_{0}\end{bmatrix}\quad\&\quad M_{\phi_{5},1}=\begin{bmatrix}1&6p\Theta_{0}\\
\frac{12u^{p-1}}{\Theta_{0}}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\alpha_{1}&0\\
0&-\beta_{1}\end{bmatrix}.

Note that one can choose any
\vec{\Theta}
satisfying

-1\leq v_{p}(\Theta_{0})=v_{p}(\Theta_{1})\leq\min\{0,v_{p}(x_{0}),v_{p}(x_{1})\}.

We will divide
R(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
into following four areas, and in each area we will determine the mod-
p
reduction:

(i)

\vec{t}\in R_{int}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
: Note that we have either
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})\not\in\{0,v_{p}(x_{0}),v_{p}(x_{1})\}
or
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})\neq-1
, in this case. Consider two
\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}
-submodules
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{1})
and
\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{2})
of
\mathcal{M}
. Then one can observe that

\circ

if
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})\not\in\{0,v_{p}(x_{0}),v_{p}(x_{1})\}
, then
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
is a Breuil submodule of
\mathcal{M}
such that
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(4,2;\alpha_{0},\alpha_{1})
and the submodule
\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{2}^{(0)},6p\Theta_{0}\overline{E}_{1}^{(1)}+\overline{E}_{2}^{(1)})
gives
\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(5,3;-\beta_{0},-\beta_{1})
and
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\oplus\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime\prime}
;

\circ

if
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})\neq-1
, then
\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}
is a Breuil submodule of
\mathcal{M}
and it satisfies
\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(5,3;-\beta_{0},-\beta_{1})
and
\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(4,2;\alpha_{0},\alpha_{1})
. It is not difficult to show that the short exact sequence determined by
\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}
is non-split.

So
\overline{\rho}^{ss}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{2}^{1+3p}\oplus\omega_{2}^{2p}
, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i). In addition, the monodromy type is given as follow.

(a)

If
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})=0
, then
t_{0}\geq 0
and
t_{1}\geq 0
and the monodromy type is
(1,1)
.

(b)

If
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})\neq 0
, then the monodromy type is
(0,0)
. Note that, in particular, if
\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}
is a Breuil submodule of
\mathcal{M}
but
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
is not, then either
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})=v_{p}(x_{0})
or
v_{p}(x_{1})
, and so we have either
t_{0}<0
or
t_{1}<0
.

(ii)

t_{1}=-1
,
t_{0}>-1
: We have
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})=-1=v_{p}(x_{1})\neq v_{p}(x_{0})
, and so
\frac{x_{1}}{\Theta_{1}},p\Theta_{0},p\Theta_{1}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
and
\frac{1}{\Theta_{0}}=\frac{1}{\Theta_{1}}=\frac{x_{0}}{\Theta_{0}}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. In particular, the monodromy type is
(0,0)
. Put
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(6p\Theta_{0}\overline{E}_{1}^{(0)}+\overline{E}_{2}^{(0)},\overline{E}_{2}^{(1)})
. It is easy to see that
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(5,3;-\beta_{0},-\beta_{1})
and
\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{1}+\mathcal{M}^{\prime})\cong\mathcal{M}(4,2;\alpha_{0},\alpha_{1})
, and so we conclude that
\overline{\rho}^{ss}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{2}^{2p}\oplus\omega_{2}^{1+3p}
, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i). It is not difficult to show that the short exact sequence determined by
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}
is non-split.

(iii)

t_{0}=t_{1}=-1
: We have
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})=-1=v_{p}(x_{0})=v_{p}(x_{1})
, and so
\frac{x_{0}}{\Theta_{0}},\frac{x_{1}}{\Theta_{1}},p\Theta_{0}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
and
\frac{1}{\Theta_{0}}=\frac{1}{\Theta_{1}}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. In particular, the monodromy type is
(0,0)
.

(a)

Assume
px_{0}=\frac{1}{6}
in
\mathbf{F}
, and put
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{1}^{(0)}+\frac{x_{0}}{\Theta_{0}}\overline{E}_{2}^{(0)},\overline{E}_{1}^{(1)})
. It is easy to see that
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
satisfies
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(4,3;\alpha_{0},\frac{6p\beta_{1}\Theta_{0}x_{1}}{\Theta_{1}})
and
\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{2}+\mathcal{M}^{\prime})\cong\mathcal{M}(5,2;-\beta_{0},-\frac{\alpha_{1}\Theta_{1}x_{0}}{\Theta_{0}x_{1}})
, and so
\overline{\rho}^{ss}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{2}^{1+2p}\oplus\omega_{2}^{3p}
, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i). It is not difficult to show that the short exact sequence determined by
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}
is non-split.

(b)

Assume
px_{0}\neq\frac{1}{6}
in
\mathbf{F}
, put
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(6p\Theta_{0}\overline{E}_{1}^{(0)}+\overline{E}_{2}^{(0)},\overline{E}_{2}^{(1)})

, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i).It is not difficult to show that the short exact sequence determined by
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}
is non-split.

,

: We have

and

in
\mathbf{F}
. In particular, the monodromy type is
(0,0)
.

2.3.7
(ii).

6.3.2.
For
\vec{k}^{\prime}=(\frac{1}{2},4)

In this section of paragraph, by
\vec{k}^{\prime}
we always mean
(\frac{1}{2},4)
. In this case, we have
\vec{x}=\vec{x}^{(1,2)}
, and we will write
\mathcal{M}
for the Breuil modules. From the results in §
5.3
, the Breuil modules
\mathcal{M}
is determined by the following data:

\circ

M_{N,0}=\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
\frac{1-10u^{p-1}}{\Theta_{0}}&0\end{bmatrix}\quad\&\quad M_{N,1}=\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
\frac{1}{\Theta_{1}}&0\end{bmatrix}
;

\circ

M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{5},0}=\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
\frac{x_{0}}{\Theta_{0}}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}u^{4}&0\\
0&u^{5}\end{bmatrix}\quad\&\quad M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{5},1}=\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{\Theta_{1}}{x_{1}}\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}u^{4}&\frac{\beta_{1}u^{3}}{18\alpha_{1}\Theta_{0}}\\
0&u\end{bmatrix}
;

\circ

M_{\phi_{5},0}=\begin{bmatrix}\alpha_{0}&0\\
0&-\beta_{0}\end{bmatrix}\quad\&\quad M_{\phi_{5},1}=\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{4p\Theta_{0}}{3}\\
\frac{10u^{p-1}}{\Theta_{0}}-\frac{13}{36p\Theta_{0}x_{1}}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}0&\frac{\alpha_{1}}{3}\\
3\beta_{1}&0\end{bmatrix}.

Note that we have
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})=v_{p}(\Theta_{1})=1+v_{p}(x_{1})
, and so
\frac{\Theta_{1}}{x_{1}}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. We will divide
R(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
into following five areas, and in each area we will determine the mod-
p
reduction:

(i)

\vec{t}\in R_{int}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
: It is easy to see that
\mathcal{M}\cong\mathcal{M}_{1}((4,4),(5,1);(\alpha_{0},\frac{\alpha_{1}}{3}),(-\beta_{0},3\beta_{1}))
, and so we conclude that
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{4}^{1+4p+p^{3}}\oplus\omega_{4}^{p+p^{2}+4p^{3}}
by Lemma
2.3.7
(ii).

(ii)

t_{1}=-1
,
t_{0}\geq 0
: We have
\frac{1}{\Theta_{0}},\frac{1}{\Theta_{1}},\frac{1}{px_{1}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
and
p\Theta_{0}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. In particular, the monodromy type is
(1,1)
. Put
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{2})\subset\mathcal{M}
. It is easy to see that
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(5,2;-\beta_{0},-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{18\Theta_{0}})
and
\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(4,3;\alpha_{0},18\beta_{1}\Theta_{0})
, and so we conclude that
\overline{\rho}^{ss}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{2}^{3p}\oplus\omega_{2}^{1+2p}
, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i), and
\overline{\rho}
is non-split as the monodromy type is nonzero.

(iii)

t_{0}-t_{1}=1
,
-1<t_{0}<0
: We have
\frac{x_{0}}{\Theta_{0}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
and
\frac{1}{\Theta_{0}}=\frac{1}{\Theta_{0}}=p\Theta_{0}=\frac{1}{px_{1}}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. In particular, the monodromy type is
(0,0)
. Put
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{1}^{(0)},\overline{E}_{2}^{(1)})\subseteq\mathcal{M}
. It is easy to see that
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(5,1;\frac{\beta_{0}x_{0}}{\Theta_{0}},\alpha_{1})
and
\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(4,4;\frac{\alpha_{0}\Theta_{0}}{x_{0}},\beta_{1})
, and so we conclude that
\overline{\rho}^{ss}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{2}^{4p}\oplus\omega_{2}^{1+p}
, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i).It is not difficult to show that the short exact sequence determined by
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}
is non-split.

(iv)

t_{0}=-1
,
t_{1}=-2
: We have
\frac{x_{0}}{\Theta_{0}},p\Theta_{0}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
and
\frac{1}{\Theta_{0}}=\frac{1}{\Theta_{1}}=\frac{1}{px_{1}}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. In particular, the monodromy type is
(0,0)

(a)

Assume
px_{0}=\frac{3}{4}
in
\mathbf{F}

2.3.7
(i).

(b)

in
\mathbf{F}

2.3.7

\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}
is non-split.

,
t_{0}>-1
: We have

in

. In particular, the monodromy type is

\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{4}^{1+4p+p^{3}}\oplus\omega_{4}^{p+p^{2}+4p^{3}}

\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(4,2;\alpha_{0},-\frac{\beta_{1}x_{1}}{6\Theta_{1}})
, and so we conclude that
\overline{\rho}^{ss}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{2}^{2p}\oplus\omega_{2}^{1+3p}
, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i). It is not difficult to show that the short exact sequence determined by
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}
is non-split.

6.3.5.
For

In this section of paragraph, by
\vec{k}^{\prime}
we always mean
(1,\frac{9}{2})
. In this case, we have
\vec{x}=\vec{x}^{(1,1)}
, and we will write
\mathcal{M}
for the Breuil modules. From the results in §
5.3
, the Breuil modules
\mathcal{M}
is determined by the following data:

\circ

M_{N,0}=\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
\frac{1-4u^{p-1}}{\Theta_{0}}&0\end{bmatrix}\quad\&\quad M_{N,1}=\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
\frac{1}{\Theta_{1}}&0\end{bmatrix}
;

\circ

M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{5},0}=\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{\Theta_{0}}{x_{0}}\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}u^{5}&0\\
0&u^{4}\end{bmatrix}\quad\&\quad M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{5},1}=\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{\Theta_{1}}{x_{1}}\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}u^{4}&0\\
\frac{4\alpha_{1}}{\beta_{1}\Theta_{0}}&u\end{bmatrix}
;

\circ

M_{\phi_{5},0}=\begin{bmatrix}1&-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{\beta_{0}}\frac{px_{0}}{\Theta_{0}}\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}0&\alpha_{0}\\
\beta_{0}&0\end{bmatrix}\quad\&\quad M_{\phi_{5},1}=\begin{bmatrix}1&-\frac{4p\Theta_{0}}{3}\\
\frac{4u^{p-1}}{\Theta_{0}}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}4\alpha_{1}&0\\
0&-\frac{\beta_{1}}{4}\end{bmatrix}.

Note that we have
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})=-2+v_{p}(x_{0})-v_{p}(x_{1})
and
v_{p}(\Theta_{1})=2+v_{p}(x_{0})+v_{p}(x_{1})
. We will divide
R(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
into following eight areas, and in each area we will determine the mod-
p
reduction:

(i)

(t_{0},t_{1})\in R_{int}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
: It is easy to see that
\mathcal{M}\cong\mathcal{M}_{0}((5,4),(4,1);(\alpha_{0},4\alpha_{1}),(\beta_{0},-\frac{\beta_{1}}{4}))
, and so we conclude that
\overline{\rho}^{ss}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{4}^{p+p^{2}+4p^{3}}\oplus\omega_{4}^{1+4p+p^{3}}
by Lemma
2.3.7
(ii).

(ii)

t_{1}=-2
,
-1<t_{0}<0
: We have
\frac{\Theta_{0}}{x_{0}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
and
\frac{1}{\Theta_{0}}=\frac{1}{\Theta_{1}}=\frac{\Theta_{1}}{x_{1}}=\frac{px_{0}}{\Theta_{0}}=p\Theta_{0}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. In particular, the monodromy type is
(0,0)
. Put
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{2})\subset\mathcal{M}
. It is easy to see that
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(5,1;-\frac{\beta_{0}\Theta_{0}}{x_{0}},-\frac{\beta_{1}}{4})
and
\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(4,4;\frac{\alpha_{0}x_{0}}{\Theta_{0}},4\alpha_{1})
, and so we conclude that
\overline{\rho}^{ss}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{2}^{4p}\oplus\omega_{2}^{1+p}
, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i). It is not difficult to show that the short exact sequence determined by
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}
is non-split.

(iii)

t_{0}=0
,
t_{1}=-2
: We have
\frac{1}{\Theta_{0}},\frac{1}{\Theta_{1}},\frac{\Theta_{0}}{x_{0}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
and
\frac{\Theta_{1}}{x_{1}}=\frac{px_{0}}{\Theta_{0}}=p\Theta_{0}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. In particular, the monodromy type is
(1,1)
. Put
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
as in (ii). By the same isomorphisms for
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
we have
\overline{\rho}^{ss}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{2}^{4p}\oplus\omega_{2}^{1+p}
, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i), and
\overline{\rho}
is non-split as the monodromy type is nonzero.

(iv)

t_{0}-t_{1}=2
,
-1\leq t_{0}<0
: We have
\frac{1}{\Theta_{0}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
and
\frac{1}{\Theta_{1}}=\frac{\Theta_{0}}{x_{0}}=\frac{\Theta_{1}}{x_{1}}=p\Theta_{0}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. In particular, the monodromy type is
(1,0)
. Put
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{2}^{(0)},\overline{E}_{1}^{(1)})\subset\mathcal{M}
. It is easy to see that
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(4,5;\alpha_{0},\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\Theta_{0}})
and
\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(5,0;\beta_{0},\beta_{1}\Theta_{0})
, and so we conclude that
\overline{\rho}^{ss}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{2}\oplus\omega_{2}^{5p}
, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i), and
\overline{\rho}
is non-split as the monodromy type is nonzero.

(v)

t_{1}=-3
,
-2<t_{0}<-1
: We have
\frac{px_{0}}{\Theta_{0}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
and
\frac{1}{\Theta_{0}}=\frac{1}{\Theta_{1}}=\frac{\Theta_{0}}{x_{0}}=\frac{\Theta_{1}}{x_{1}}=p\Theta_{0}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. In particular, the monodromy type is
(0,0)
. Put
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{1}^{(0)},\overline{E}_{1}^{(1)}-\frac{\beta_{0}}{\alpha_{0}}\frac{\Theta_{0}}{px_{0}}\overline{E}_{2}^{(1)})

and

, and so we conclude that
\overline{\rho}^{ss}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{2}^{p}\oplus\omega_{2}^{1+4p}
, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i). It is not difficult to show that the short exact sequence determined by
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}
is non-split.

(vi)

t_{0}=-2
,
t_{1}=-3
: We have
\frac{\Theta_{1}}{x_{1}},\frac{px_{0}}{\Theta_{0}},p\Theta_{0}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
and
\frac{1}{\Theta_{0}}=\frac{1}{\Theta_{1}}=\frac{\Theta_{0}}{x_{0}}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. In particular, the monodromy type is
(0,0)
. We note that

(a)

in
\mathbf{F}

, and so we conclude that
\overline{\rho}^{ss}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{2}^{4p}\oplus\omega_{2}^{1+p}

2.3.7
(i). It is not difficult to show that the short exact sequence determined by
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}
is non-split.

(b)

Assume
p^{2}(px_{1}+x_{0})\neq 0
in
\mathbf{F}
. Put

\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{1}^{(0)},-\frac{p\Theta_{1}}{x_{0}}\overline{E}_{1}^{(1)}+\overline{E}_{2}^{(1)})

, and so we conclude that
\overline{\rho}^{ss}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{2}^{p}\oplus\omega_{2}^{1+4p}
, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i). It is not difficult to show that the short exact sequence determined by
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}
is non-split.

(vii)

t_{0}-t_{1}=1
,
-2<t_{0}<-1
: We have

and

. In particular, the monodromy type is

. Put

, and so we conclude that

, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i). It is not difficult to show that the short exact sequence determined by
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}
is non-split.

,

: We have

in

(a)

Assume

in
