6.3.6.
For
\vec{k}^{\prime}=(\frac{3}{2},\frac{3}{2})

In this section of paragraph, by
\vec{k}^{\prime}
we always mean
(\frac{3}{2},\frac{3}{2})
. In this case, we have
\vec{x}=\vec{x}^{(0,2)}
, and we will write
\mathcal{M}
for the Breuil modules. From the results in §
5.3
, the Breuil modules
\mathcal{M}
is determined by the following data:

\circ

M_{N,0}=\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
\frac{1-10u^{p-1}}{\Theta_{0}}&0\end{bmatrix}\quad\&\quad M_{N,1}=\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
\frac{1}{\Theta_{1}}&0\end{bmatrix}
;

\circ

M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{5},0}=\begin{bmatrix}u^{5}&0\\
0&u^{4}\end{bmatrix}\quad\&\quad M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{5},1}=\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
\frac{x_{1}}{\Theta_{1}}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}u&0\\
-\frac{\alpha_{1}u^{3}}{18\beta_{1}\Theta_{0}}&u^{4}\end{bmatrix}
;

\circ

M_{\phi_{5},0}=\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{\alpha_{0}}{\beta_{0}}\frac{\Theta_{0}}{px_{0}}\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}-\alpha_{0}&0\\
0&\beta_{0}\end{bmatrix}\quad\&\quad M_{\phi_{5},1}=\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{4p\Theta_{0}}{3}\\
\frac{10u^{p-1}}{\Theta_{0}}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\frac{\alpha_{1}}{3}&0\\
0&-3\beta_{1}\end{bmatrix}
.

Note that one can choose any
\vec{\Theta}
satisfying

\max\{-1,v_{p}(x_{0})+1\}\leq v_{p}(\Theta_{0})=v_{p}(\Theta_{1})+2\leq\min\{0,v_{p}(x_{1})+2\},

and in particular
\frac{1}{\Theta_{1}}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. We will divide
R(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
into following five areas, and in each area we will determine the mod-
p
reduction:

(i)

\vec{t}\in R_{int}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
: Note that we have either
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})\not\in\{0,v_{p}(x_{1})+2\}
or
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})\not\in\{-1,v_{p}(x_{0})+1\}
. Consider two
\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}
-submodules
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{1})
and
\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{2})
of
\mathcal{M}
. Then one can observe that

\circ

if
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})\not\in\{0,v_{p}(x_{1})+2\}
, then
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
is a Breuil submodule of
\mathcal{M}
satisfying
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(5,1;-\alpha_{0},\frac{\alpha_{1}}{3})
and
\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(4,4;\beta_{0},-3\beta_{1})
;

\circ

if
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})\not\in\{-1,v_{p}(x_{0})+1\}
, then
\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}
is a Breuil submodule of
\mathcal{M}
satisfying
\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(4,4;\beta_{0},-3\beta_{1})
and
\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(5,1;-\alpha_{0},\frac{\alpha_{1}}{3})
.

In particular, if
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})\not\in\{-1,0,v_{p}(x_{0})+1,v_{p}(x_{1})+2\}
, then
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\oplus\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}
as Breuil modules, and if
v_{p}(\Theta)=v_{p}(x_{0})+1\not\in\{-1,0,v_{p}(x_{1})+2\}
, then the short exact sequence determined by
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}
is split. Otherwise, It is not difficult to show that the short exact sequence determined by
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}
or
\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}
is non-split. Note that the first sequence is non-split only for

v_{p}(\Theta_{0})=-1\not\in\{0,v_{p}(x_{1})+2\}
, and so it can be happen if
t_{0}\leq-2
, in order to have
v_{p}(x_{0})+1\leq v_{p}(\Theta_{0})
. Hence, we conclude that
\overline{\rho}^{ss}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{2}^{4p}\oplus\omega_{2}^{1+p}
, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i). In addition, the monodromy type is given as follow.

(a)

If
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})<0
, then
t_{1}<-2
, and the monodromy type is
(0,0)
.

(b)

If
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})=0
, then
t_{1}\geq-2
, and the monodromy type is
(1,0)
.

(ii)

t_{0}=-1
,
t_{1}>-2
: We have
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})=0=v_{p}(x_{0})+1\neq v_{p}(x_{1})+2
, so
\frac{1}{\Theta_{0}},\frac{\Theta_{0}}{px_{0}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
and
\frac{x_{1}}{\Theta_{1}}=p\Theta_{0}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. In particular, the monodromy type is
(1,0)
. Put
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{2}^{(0)},\frac{\alpha_{0}}{\beta_{0}}\frac{\Theta_{0}}{px_{0}}\overline{E}_{1}^{(1)}+\overline{E}_{2}^{(1)})
. It is easy to see that
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(4,4;\beta_{0},-3\beta_{1})
and
\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{1}+\mathcal{M}^{\prime})\cong\mathcal{M}(5,1;-\alpha_{0},\frac{\alpha_{1}}{3})
, and so we conclude that
\overline{\rho}^{ss}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{2}^{1+p}\oplus\omega_{2}^{4p}
, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i), and
\overline{\rho}
is non-split as the monodromy type is nonzero.

(iii)

t_{0}=-1
,
t_{1}=-2
: We have
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})=0=v_{p}(x_{0})+1=v_{p}(x_{1})+2
, so
\frac{1}{\Theta_{0}},\frac{x_{1}}{\Theta_{1}},\frac{\Theta_{0}}{px_{0}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
and
p\Theta_{0}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. In particular, the monodromy type is
(1,0)
. We note that
\frac{\beta_{0}}{\alpha_{0}}=\frac{\Theta_{0}}{p^{2}\Theta_{1}}
from (
5.3.1
).

(a)

Assume
\frac{px_{1}}{x_{0}}=1
in
\mathbf{F}
, and put
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{2}^{(0)},\overline{E}_{1}^{(1)}+\frac{x_{1}}{\Theta_{1}}\overline{E}_{2}^{(1)})
. It is easy to see that
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(4,2;-\frac{\beta_{0}\Theta_{1}}{px_{0}},-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{6\Theta_{0}})
and
\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{1}^{(0)}+\mathcal{M}^{\prime},\overline{E}_{2}^{(1)}+\mathcal{M}^{\prime})\cong\mathcal{M}(5,3;-\frac{\alpha_{0}x_{1}}{\Theta_{1}},-6\beta_{1}\Theta_{0})
, and so we conclude that
\overline{\rho}^{ss}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{2}^{1+3p}\oplus\omega_{2}^{2p}
, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i), and
\overline{\rho}
is non-split as the monodromy type is nonzero.

(b)

Assume
\frac{px_{1}}{x_{0}}\neq 1
in
\mathbf{F}
, and put
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{2}^{(0)},\frac{\Theta_{1}}{px_{0}}\overline{E}_{1}^{(1)}+\overline{E}_{2}^{(1)})
. It is easy to see that
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(4,4;\beta_{0},\frac{-3\beta_{1}(px_{0}-x_{1})}{px_{0}})
and
\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{1}+\mathcal{M}^{\prime})\cong\mathcal{M}(5,1;-\alpha_{0},\frac{p\alpha_{1}x_{0}}{3(px_{0}-x_{1})})
, and so we conclude that
\overline{\rho}^{ss}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{2}^{1+p}\oplus\omega_{2}^{4p}
, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i), and
\overline{\rho}
is non-split as the monodromy type is nonzero.

(iv)

t_{0}-t_{1}=1
,
-2<t_{0}<-1
: We have
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})=v_{p}(x_{0})+1=v_{p}(x_{1})+2\neq-1,0
, and so
\frac{x_{1}}{\Theta_{1}},\frac{\Theta_{0}}{px_{0}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
and
\frac{1}{\Theta_{0}}=p\Theta_{0}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. In particular, the monodromy type is
(0,0)
. We note that
\frac{\beta_{0}}{\alpha_{0}}=\frac{\Theta_{0}}{p^{2}\Theta_{1}}
from
5.3.1
.

(a)

Assume
\frac{px_{1}}{x_{0}}=1
in
\mathbf{F}
. Choosing basis
\underline{E}^{\prime(1)}=(\overline{E}_{1}^{(1)},\frac{\Theta_{1}}{x_{1}}\overline{E}_{1}^{(1)}+\overline{E}_{2}^{(1)})
and
\underline{F}^{\prime(1)}=(u^{3}\overline{F}_{1}^{(1)}-\frac{x_{1}}{\Theta_{1}}\overline{F}_{2}^{(1)},\frac{\Theta_{1}}{x_{1}}\overline{F}_{1}^{(1)})
, it is easy to see that
\mathcal{M}
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{M}_{1}((5,4),(4,1);(-\alpha_{0},\frac{\alpha_{1}\Theta_{1}}{3x_{1}}),(\beta_{0},\frac{3\beta_{1}x_{1}}{\Theta_{1}}))
, and so we conclude that
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{4}^{4p+p^{2}+p^{3}}\oplus\omega_{4}^{1+p+4p^{3}}
, by Lemma
2.3.7
(ii).

(b)

Assume
\frac{px_{1}}{x_{0}}\neq 1
in
\mathbf{F}
. Put
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
as in (iii)(b). By the same isomorphisms for
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
we have
\overline{\rho}^{ss}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{2}^{1+p}\oplus\omega_{2}^{4p}
, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i). It is not difficult to show that the short exact sequence determined by
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}
is non-split.

(v)

t_{1}=-3
,
t_{0}\leq-2
: We have
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})=-1=v_{p}(x_{1})+2
, so
\frac{x_{1}}{\Theta_{1}},p\Theta_{0}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
and
\frac{1}{\Theta_{0}}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. In particular, the monodromy type is
(0,0)
. Put
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\frac{4p\Theta_{0}}{3}\overline{E}_{1}^{(0)}+\overline{E}_{2}^{(0)},\overline{E}_{1}^{(1)})
. It is easy to see that
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(5,4;-\frac{3\beta_{1}x_{1}}{\Theta_{1}},-\frac{4p\alpha_{0}\Theta_{0}}{3})
and
\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{2}+\mathcal{M}^{\prime})\cong\mathcal{M}(4,1;\beta_{0},-\frac{\alpha_{1}\Theta_{1}}{4p\Theta_{0}x_{1}})
, and so we conclude that
\overline{\rho}^{ss}\cong\omega_{2}^{p}\oplus\omega_{2}^{1+4p}
, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i). It is not difficult to show that the short exact sequence determined by
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}
is non-split.

6.3.7.
For
\vec{k}^{\prime}=(\frac{3}{2},5)

In this section of paragraph, by
\vec{k}^{\prime}
we always mean
(\frac{3}{2},5)
. In this case, we have
\vec{x}=\vec{x}^{(0,1)}
, and we will write
\mathcal{M}
for the Breuil modules. From the results in §
5.3
, the Breuil modules
\mathcal{M}
is determined by the following data:

\circ

M_{N,0}=\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
\frac{1-4u^{p-1}}{\Theta_{0}}&0\end{bmatrix}\quad\&\quad M_{N,1}=\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
\frac{1}{\Theta_{1}}&0\end{bmatrix}
;

\circ

M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{5},0}=\begin{bmatrix}u^{5}&0\\
0&u^{4}\end{bmatrix}\quad\&\quad M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{5},1}=\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{\Theta_{1}}{x_{1}}\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}u^{5}&\frac{\alpha_{1}u^{4}}{4\beta_{1}\Theta_{0}}\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}
;

\circ

M_{\phi_{5},0}=\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{\alpha_{0}}{\beta_{0}}\frac{\Theta_{0}}{px_{0}}\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}-\alpha_{0}&0\\
0&\beta_{0}\end{bmatrix}\quad\&\quad M_{\phi_{5},1}=\begin{bmatrix}1&-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\beta_{1}}\frac{p^{5}x_{1}}{\Theta_{1}}\\
\frac{4u^{p-1}}{\Theta_{0}}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}0&\alpha_{1}\\
\beta_{1}&0\end{bmatrix}
.

Note that we have
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})=3+v_{p}(x_{1})
and
v_{p}(\Theta_{1})=1+v_{p}(x_{1})
, and so
\frac{1}{\Theta_{1}}=\frac{\Theta_{1}}{x_{1}}=\frac{p^{5}x_{1}}{\Theta_{1}}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. We will divide
R(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
into following four areas, and in each area we will determine the mod-
p
reduction:

(i)

\vec{t}\in R_{int}(\vec{r};\vec{k})
: It is easy to see that
\mathcal{M}\cong\mathcal{M}_{1}((5,5),(4,0);(-\alpha_{0},\alpha_{1}),(\beta_{0},\beta_{1}))
, and so we conclude that

by Lemma
2.3.7
(ii).

(ii)

t_{1}=-3
,
t_{0}<-1
: We have

and

in
\mathbf{F}
. In particular, the monodromy type is

. Put

. It is easy to see that

\overline{\rho}
is non-split as the monodromy type is nonzero.

(iii)

t_{0}=-1
,

: We have

. In particular, the monodromy type is
(1,0)
. Put

and

, and so we conclude that
\overline{\rho}^{ss}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{2}\oplus\omega_{2}^{5p}
, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i), and
\overline{\rho}
is non-split as the monodromy type is nonzero.

(iv)

t_{0}-t_{1}=2
,
t_{0}\leq-1
: We have
\frac{\Theta_{0}}{px_{0}}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
and
\frac{1}{\Theta_{0}}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. In particular, the monodromy type is
(0,0)
. Put
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
as in (iii). By the same isomorphisms for
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
we have
\overline{\rho}^{ss}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{2}\oplus\omega_{2}^{5p}
, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i). It is not difficult to show that the short exact sequence determined by
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}
is non-split.

6.3.8.

we always mean
(\infty,\frac{3}{2})
. In this case, we have
\vec{x}=\vec{x}^{(0,2)}
, and we will write
\mathcal{M}
for the Breuil modules. From the results in §
5.3
, the Breuil modules
\mathcal{M}
is determined by the following data:

\circ

M_{N,0}=\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
\frac{1-10u^{p-1}}{\Theta_{0}}&0\end{bmatrix}\quad\&\quad M_{N,1}=\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
\frac{1}{\Theta_{1}}&0\end{bmatrix}
;

\circ

M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{5},0}=\begin{bmatrix}u^{5}&0\\
0&u^{4}\end{bmatrix}\quad\&\quad M_{\mathrm{Fil}^{5},1}=\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
\frac{x_{1}}{\Theta_{1}}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}u&0\\
-\frac{\alpha_{1}u^{3}}{18\beta_{1}\Theta_{0}}&u^{4}\end{bmatrix}
;

\circ

M_{\phi_{5},0}=\begin{bmatrix}-\alpha_{0}&0\\
0&\beta_{0}\end{bmatrix}\quad\&\quad M_{\phi_{5},1}=\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{4p\Theta_{0}}{3}\\
\frac{10u^{p-1}}{\Theta_{0}}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\frac{\alpha_{1}}{3}&0\\
0&-3\beta_{1}\end{bmatrix}
.

Note that one can choose any
\vec{\Theta}
satisfying

-1\leq v_{p}(\Theta_{0})=v_{p}(\Theta_{1})+2\leq\min\{0,v_{p}(x_{1})+2\},

and in particular
\frac{1}{\Theta_{1}}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. We will divide
R(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
into following two areas, and in each area we will determine the mod-
p
reduction:

(i)

\vec{t}\in R_{int}(\vec{r};\vec{k}^{\prime})
: Note that we have either
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})\not\in\{0,v_{p}(x_{1})+2\}
or
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})\neq-1
. Consider two
\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}
-submodules
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{1})
and
\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{2})
of
\mathcal{M}
. Then one can observe that

\circ

if
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})\not\in\{0,v_{p}(x_{1})+2\}
, then
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
is a Breuil submodule of
\mathcal{M}
satisfying
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(5,1;-\alpha_{0},\frac{\alpha_{1}}{3})
and
\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(4,4;\beta_{0},-3\beta_{1})
;

\circ

if
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})\neq-1
, then
\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}
is a Breuil submodule of
\mathcal{M}
and it satisfies
\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(4,4;\beta_{0},-3\beta_{1})
and
\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(5,1;-\alpha_{0},\frac{\alpha_{1}}{3})
.

In particular, if
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})\not\in\{-1,0,v_{p}(x_{1})+2\}
, then
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\oplus\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}
as Breuil modules. Otherwise, It is not difficult to show that the short exact sequence determined by
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}
or
\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}
is non-split. Hence, we conclude that
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{2}^{4p}\oplus\omega_{2}^{1+p}
, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i). In addition, the monodromy type is given as follow.

(a)

If
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})<0
, then
t_{1}<-2
, and the monodromy type is
(0,0)
.

(b)

If
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})=0
, then
t_{1}\geq-2
, and the monodromy type is
(1,0)
.

(ii)

t_{1}=-3
: We have
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})=-1=v_{p}(x_{1})+2
, so
\frac{x_{1}}{\Theta_{1}},p\Theta_{0}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
and
\frac{1}{\Theta_{0}}=0
in
\mathbf{F}
. In particular, the monodromy type is
(0,0)
. Put
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\frac{4p\Theta_{0}}{3}\overline{E}_{1}^{(0)}+\overline{E}_{2}^{(0)},\overline{E}_{1}^{(1)})
. It is easy to see that
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(5,4;-\frac{3\beta_{1}x_{1}}{\Theta_{1}},-\frac{4p\alpha_{0}\Theta_{0}}{3})
and
\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=(\overline{S}_{\mathbf{F}}(\overline{E}_{2})+\mathcal{M}^{\prime})/\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{M}(4,1;\beta_{0},-\frac{\alpha_{1}\Theta_{1}}{4p\Theta_{0}x_{1}})
, and so we conclude that
\overline{\rho}^{ss}\cong\omega_{2}^{p}\oplus\omega_{2}^{1+4p}
, by Lemma
2.3.7
(i). It is not difficult to show that the short exact sequence determined by
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}
is non-split.

6.3.10.
Summary on mod-
p
reduction when
\vec{r}=(1,5)

In this section of paragraph, we summarize the results on mod-
p
reduction when
\vec{r}=(1,5)
.

We first consider the case
\mathcal{J}_{0}=\emptyset
, i.e., those
\vec{k}^{\prime}\in J(\vec{r};\emptyset)
. We set
\vec{\xi}:=(\xi_{0},\xi_{1})\in E^{2}(=E^{2}_{\emptyset})
where

\xi_{0}=\frac{1}{p}\left(p\mathfrak{L}_{0}-\mathfrak{L}_{1}+\frac{17}{6}\right)\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\xi_{1}=\frac{1}{p}(p\mathfrak{L}_{1}-\mathfrak{L}_{0}).

Note that we have
\vec{x}^{(1,2)}=\vec{\xi}
, and that we further have

\displaystyle\vec{x}^{(1,1)}

\displaystyle=\left(\xi_{0}-\tfrac{3}{4p},\xi_{1}\right);

\displaystyle\vec{x}^{(1,3)}

\displaystyle=\left(\xi_{0}+\tfrac{1}{6p},\xi_{1}\right);

\displaystyle\vec{x}^{(0,1)}

\displaystyle=\left(\xi_{0}-\tfrac{3}{4p},\xi_{1}+\tfrac{1}{p}\xi_{0}-\tfrac{3}{4p^{2}}\right);

\displaystyle\vec{x}^{(0,2)}

\displaystyle=\left(\xi_{0},\xi_{1}+\tfrac{1}{p}\xi_{0}\right).

We partition
E^{2}(=E^{2}_{\emptyset})
, such that within each subset the semi-simplification of
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}
remains constant, as follow:

\circ

S_{1}=\{\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in E^{2}\mid v_{p}(\xi_{0})>-1,\ v_{p}(\xi_{1})>-1\}
;

\circ

S_{2}=\{\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in E^{2}\mid v_{p}(\xi_{0})=-1,\ v_{p}(\xi_{1})>-2\}
;

\circ

S_{3}=\{\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in E^{2}\mid v_{p}(\xi_{0})>-1,\ v_{p}(\xi_{1})=-1\}
;

\circ

S_{4}=\{\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in E^{2}\mid v_{p}(\xi_{0})-v_{p}(\xi_{1})<1,\ v_{p}(\xi_{0})>-1,\ v_{p}(\xi_{1})<-1\}
;

\circ

S_{5}=\{\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in E^{2}\mid v_{p}(\xi_{0})-v_{p}(\xi_{1})>1,\ -2\leq v_{p}(\xi_{1})<-1\}
;

\circ

S_{6}=\{\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in E^{2}\mid v_{p}(\xi_{0})-v_{p}(\xi_{1})=1,\ -2<v_{p}(\xi_{0})<0\}
;

\circ

S_{7}=\{\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in E^{2}\mid v_{p}(\xi_{0})-v_{p}(\xi_{1})<1,\ -2<v_{p}(\xi_{0})<-1\}
;

\circ

S_{8}=\{\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in E^{2}\mid 1<v_{p}(\xi_{0}-\tfrac{3}{4p})-v_{p}(\xi_{1})<2,\ -3<v_{p}(\xi_{1})<-2\}
;

\circ

S_{9}=\{\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in E^{2}\mid v_{p}(\xi_{0}-\tfrac{3}{4p})-v_{p}(\xi_{1})=2,\ v_{p}(\xi_{0}-\tfrac{3}{4p})<0\}
;

\circ

S_{10}=\{\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in E^{2}\mid v_{p}(\xi_{0}-\tfrac{3}{4p})-v_{p}(\xi_{1})>2,\ v_{p}(\xi_{1})<-2\}
;

\circ

S_{11}=\{\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in E^{2}\mid v_{p}(\xi_{0})\leq-2,\ v_{p}(\xi_{1}+\tfrac{1}{p}\xi_{0})>-3\}
;

\circ

S_{12}=\{\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in E^{2}\mid v_{p}(\xi_{0})<-1,\ v_{p}(\xi_{1}+\tfrac{1}{p}\xi_{0})=-3\}
;

\circ

S_{13}=\{\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in E^{2}\mid v_{p}(\xi_{0})-v_{p}(\xi_{1}+\tfrac{1}{p}\xi_{0})<2,\ v_{p}(\xi_{1}+\tfrac{1}{p}\xi_{0})<-3\}
.

Note that
S_{1},\cdots,S_{7}
are defined in terms of
v_{p}(\vec{\xi})
, while
S_{8}
,
S_{9}
,
S_{10}
(resp.
S_{11}
,
S_{12}
,
S_{13}
) are in terms of
(v_{p}(\xi_{0}-\frac{3}{4p}),v_{p}(\xi_{1}))
(resp. of
(v_{p}(\xi_{0}),v_{p}(\xi_{1}+\frac{1}{p}\xi_{0}))
). But it is easy to see that the unions
S_{8}\cup S_{9}\cup S_{10}
and
S_{11}\cup S_{12}\cup S_{13}
can be described in terms of
v_{p}(\vec{\xi})
as follows:

\displaystyle S_{8}\cup S_{9}\cup S_{10}=

\displaystyle\{\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in E^{2}\mid v_{p}(\xi_{0})-v_{p}(\xi_{1})>1,\ -3<v_{p}(\xi_{1})<-2\}

\displaystyle\,\,\,\cup\{\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in E^{2}\mid v_{p}(\xi_{0})-v_{p}(\xi_{1})\geq 2,\ v_{p}(\xi_{1})<-2\};

\displaystyle S_{11}\cup S_{12}\cup S_{13}=

\displaystyle\{\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in E^{2}\mid v_{p}(\xi_{0})\leq-2,\ v_{p}(\xi_{1})\geq-3\}

\displaystyle\,\,\,\cup\{\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in E^{2}\mid v_{p}(\xi_{0})-v_{p}(\xi_{1})<2,\ v_{p}(\xi_{1})\leq-3\}.

It is not difficult to show that
S_{i}\cap S_{j}=\emptyset
if
i\neq j
and that
\cup_{i=1}^{13}S_{i}=E^{2}
. The following picture, Figure
4
, describes those sets
S_{1},\cdots,S_{7},S_{8}\cup S_{9}\cup S_{10},S_{11}\cup S_{12}\cup S_{13}
on
(v_{p}(\xi_{0}),v_{p}(\xi_{1}))
-plane, emphasizing the disjointness.

Figure 4.
Partition of
E^{2}
by
S_{1},\cdots,S_{7},S_{8}\cup S_{9}\cup S_{10},S_{11}\cup S_{12}\cup S_{13}
.

We further describe the sets
S_{8}
,
S_{9}
,
S_{10}
(resp.
S_{11}
,
S_{12}
,
S_{13}
) on
(v_{p}(\xi_{0}-\frac{3}{4p}),v_{p}(\xi_{1}))
-plane (resp. on
(v_{p}(\xi_{0}),v_{p}(\xi_{1}+\frac{1}{p}\xi_{0}))
-plane) in Figure
5
.

Figure 5.
Partitions of
S_{8}\cup S_{9}\cup S_{10}
(left) and of
S_{11}\cup S_{12}\cup S_{13}
(right)

We now explain how one can induce Table

from the results in the previous sections of paragraph.

\circ

Assume
\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in S_{1}
. Equivalently, we have
v_{p}(x_{1}^{(1,3)})=-1
,
v_{p}(x_{0}^{(1,3)})=-1
and
v_{p}(px_{0}^{(1,3)}-\frac{1}{6})>0
. Then we have
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{4}^{3p+p^{2}+2p^{3}}\oplus\omega_{4}^{1+2p+3p^{3}}
, from
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};(\frac{1}{2},\frac{5}{2}))
(see §
6.3.1
(iv)(a)).

\circ

Assume
\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in S_{2}
, i.e.,
v_{p}(\xi_{0})=-1
and
v_{p}(\xi_{1})>-2
. Equivalently we have
v_{p}(x_{0}^{(0,2)})=-1
,
v_{p}(x_{1}^{(0,2)})=-2
and
v_{p}(1-\frac{px_{1}^{(0,1)}}{x_{0}^{(0,1)}})>0
. Also, for
v_{p}(\xi_{1})\geq-1
, we have another equivalent formulation given by
v_{p}(x_{0}^{(1,3)})\geq-1
,
v_{p}(x_{1}^{(1,3)})\geq-1
and
v_{p}(px_{0}^{(1,3)}-\frac{1}{6})=0
. In this case there are two non-homothetic lattices whose mod-
p
reductions are non-split and reducible.

–

We first consider
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};(\frac{3}{2},\frac{3}{2}))
(see §
6.3.6
(iii)(a)). In this case we have
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\begin{bmatrix}\omega_{2}^{2p}&*\\
0&\omega_{2}^{1+3p}\end{bmatrix}
with
\vec{\mathrm{MT}}=(1,0)
.

–

We also consider
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};(\frac{1}{2},\frac{5}{2}))
or
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};(1,\frac{7}{2}))
, which coincide if
v_{p}(\xi_{1})=-1
(see §

and §
6.3.4
(v) if
-2<v_{p}(\xi_{1})\leq-1
). In this case we have
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\begin{bmatrix}\omega_{2}^{1+3p}&*\\
0&\omega_{2}^{2p}\end{bmatrix}
, with
\vec{\mathrm{MT}}=(1,1)
if
v_{p}(\xi_{0}+\frac{1}{6p})\geq 0
and
v_{p}(\xi_{1})\geq 0
, and
\vec{\mathrm{MT}}=(0,0)
otherwise.

\circ

Assume
\vec{\mathfrak{L}}\in S_{3}
, i.e.,
v_{p}(\xi_{0})>-1
and

,

. In this case there are two non-homothetic lattices whose mod-

We now treat the case
\mathcal{J}_{0}=\{0\}
, i.e., those
\vec{k}^{\prime}\in J(\vec{r};\{0\})
. We set

\xi^{\prime}=\frac{1}{p^{2}}\left(p^{2}\mathfrak{L}_{1}-\mathfrak{L}_{1}+\frac{17}{6}\right)\in E(=E^{2}_{\{0\}}).

Here, we identify
E
with
E^{2}_{\{0\}}
in the obvious manner. One can readily induce
\xi^{\prime}
by solving (
5.1.16
) for
x^{(0,2)}_{1}
, keeping in mind that
\Delta_{0}(-1)=-x_{0}^{(0,2)}
and
\Delta_{1}(-1)=-\frac{17}{6}
. It is easy to see that

\vec{x}^{(0,1)}_{1}=\xi^{\prime}-\tfrac{3}{4p^{2}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\vec{x}^{(0,2)}_{1}=\xi^{\prime}.

Note that
\vec{x}^{(0,1)}_{0}
and
\vec{x}^{(0,2)}_{0}
do not affect the mod-
p
reduction, as
\mathcal{J}_{0}=\{0\}
(see (
5.2.6
)).

We summarize the non-split mod-
p
reduction when
\vec{r}=(1,5)
and
J_{0}=\{0\}
in Table
26
. One can easily induce the summary in Remark
6.3.2
from Table
26
.

\xi^{\prime}

\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}

\vec{\mathrm{MT}}

v_{p}(\xi^{\prime})>-3

\begin{bmatrix}\omega_{2}^{4p}&*\\
0&\omega_{2}^{1+p}\end{bmatrix}

\begin{cases}(0,0)&\mbox{if }-3<v_{p}(\xi^{\prime})<-2;\\
(1,1)&\mbox{if }v_{p}(\xi^{\prime})\geq-2\end{cases}

\begin{bmatrix}\omega_{2}^{p}&*\\

Table 26.
Non-split mod-
p
reduction for
\vec{r}=(1,5)
,
\mathcal{J}_{0}=\{0\}

We now explain how one can induce Table
26
from the results in the previous sections of paragraph.

\circ

Assume
v_{p}(\xi^{\prime})>-3
. In this case, there are two non-homothetic lattices whose mod-
p
reductions are non-split and reducible.

–

We first consider the lattice from
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})=-1
of
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};(\infty,\frac{3}{2}))
(see §
6.3.8
(i)). In this case, we have
\overline{\rho}_{1}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\begin{bmatrix}\omega_{2}^{4p}&*\\
0&\omega_{2}^{1+p}\end{bmatrix}
with
\vec{\mathrm{MT}}=(0,0)
.

–

We also consider the lattice from
v_{p}(\Theta_{0})\in\{0,v_{p}(\xi^{\prime})+2\}

\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};(\infty,\frac{3}{2}))
(see §
6.3.8
(i)). In this case, we have
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\begin{bmatrix}\omega_{2}^{1+p}&*\\
0&\omega_{2}^{4p}\end{bmatrix}
, with
\vec{\mathrm{MT}}=(0,0)
if
-3<v_{p}(\xi^{\prime})<-2
and
\vec{\mathrm{MT}}=(1,1)
if
v_{p}(\xi^{\prime})\geq-2
.

\circ

Assume
v_{p}(\xi^{\prime})=-3
. Equivalently, we have
v_{p}(x_{1}^{(0,1)})=-3
. In this case, there are two non-homothetic lattices whose mod-
p
reductions are non-split and reducible.

–

We first consider
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};(\infty,5))
(see §
6.3.9
(ii)). In this case, we have
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\begin{bmatrix}\omega_{2}^{p}&*\\
0&\omega_{2}^{1+4p}\end{bmatrix}
with
\vec{\mathrm{MT}}=(1,0)
.

–

We also consider
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};(\infty,\frac{3}{2}))
(see §
6.3.8
(ii)). In this case, we have
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\begin{bmatrix}\omega_{2}^{1+4p}&*\\
0&\omega_{2}^{p}\end{bmatrix}
with
\vec{\mathrm{MT}}=(0,0)
.

\circ

Assume
v_{p}(\xi^{\prime})<-3
. Equivalently, we have
v_{p}(x_{1}^{(0,1)})<-3
. Then
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}}\cong\omega_{4}^{5p+p^{2}}\oplus\omega_{4}^{1+5p^{3}}
, from
\mathbf{Case}_{\phi}(\vec{r};(\infty,5))
(see §
6.3.9
(i)).

Remark 6.3.2
.

One can extract the following results from Table
25
and Table
26
:

\circ

our method provides with at least one Galois stable lattice in each
2
-dimensional semi-stable non-crystalline representation
V
of
G_{\mathbf{Q}_{p^{2}}}
with Hodge–Tate weights
\mathrm{HT}(V)_{0}=(0,1)
and
\mathrm{HT}(V)_{1}=(0,5)
;

\circ

if the mod-
p
reduction is an extension of two distinct characters then our method provides the two non-homothetic lattices.
